NLS BARD and My Talking Books Help Guide

The National Library Service’s official documentation for BARD, BARD Mobile, BARD Express and the My Talking Books Alexa skill, gathered into one screen-reader-friendly document

2026-07-29

NLS BARD and My Talking Books Help Guide

This guide gathers the National Library Service’s official documentation for BARD — Braille and Audio Reading Download — and for the apps and skill that read it aloud. It was taken from the Library of Congress’s own pages on 29 July 2026.

BARD is the rare product in this collection that reaches all four of the surfaces these guides usually look for. There is a Windows program in BARD Express, the BARD website in a browser, BARD Mobile on iPhone and iPad, and the My Talking Books skill on Amazon Alexa. Android and Fire tablets are covered too. Each has its own user guide, and all of them are here.

The My Talking Books material is the fullest treatment of the Alexa skill anywhere: a welcome page, an introduction written for people who have never used an Alexa skill, the user guide, the command list, and separate pages on browsing and searching, wish lists, choosing a book from a list, bookmarks, the sleep timer, reading speed, jumping by time, book descriptions and streaming history.

One convention of the Library’s own writing is preserved rather than corrected. Throughout the My Talking Books documents the smart speaker’s wake word is replaced by the phrase “Lady A”, so that reading the guide aloud does not set your speaker off. That is the Library’s safeguard and it stays.

Three things were set aside. The Spanish-language pages, since this is an English volume — the Library publishes a Spanish BARD tree of its own. The Talking Book Topics magazine issues, which the crawl reached and which are catalogues of new books rather than help; twenty-one issues of them, running to hundreds of thousands of characters each. And one page the Library’s server refused during gathering, on working with bookshelves in My Talking Books.

Headings run three deep: this title, then a section, then a document, with each document’s own headings nested beneath. Every document is listed in the table of contents below.

Getting started with BARD

Sign up for BARD and Accessing BARD

BARD, the NLS Braille and Audio Reading Download service, provides instant access to hundreds of thousands of books, magazines, and music materials in audio and electronic braille. You’ll find current bestsellers, literary classics, mysteries, westerns, romances, and more; a wide selection of books for school-age children and teens; and books in Spanish and other world languages. Dozens of titles are added each week! And you can subscribe to magazines automatically and easily through BARD. This page helps you get started with BARD and helps you use BARD.

On this page, learn more about BARD service:

  • Ready to get started with BARD?
  • BARD Mobile, BARD Express, and My Talking Books
    • BARD Mobile
      • iOS
      • Android
      • Fire
    • BARD Express
    • My Talking Books skill on smart speaker
  • Still need help with something related to BARD?
Ready to get started with BARD?

First—if you’re not one already—become an NLS patron. Submit this online form to get information on how to become a patron from the NLS network library that serves your state or locality or simply Find Your Library and contact them about becoming a patron.

Second, contact your network library, tell them you are a patron, and they can sign you up for BARD.

Third, start enjoying BARD!

  • As a patron, you can get a free talking-book player on long-term loan from your network library. You can also buy a commercial model that you’ve confirmed can play NLS-produced talking books and magazines.
  • You’ll need a refreshable braille display to read electronic braille books downloaded from BARD. You can get a refreshable braille display on loan from NLS. Contact your network library to find out how.
  • If you plan to use your own smart device, download the free BARD Mobile app, available at the App Store External for iOS or the Play Store External or Amazon Kindle Appstore External for Android or Fire.
  • If you have a PC, you can download books and magazines from BARD and transfer them to a USB drive or NLS digital cartridge for use with a talking-book player. Or, with the Window-based BARD Express, you can play books and magazines from BARD directly on your PC!
  • If you have an Apple computer, you can download books and magazines from BARD and transfer them to a USB drive or NLS digital cartridge for use with a talking-book player.

Being able to download titles and manage my reading list independently has made an outstanding service so much better. The BARD Mobile app for the iPhone has also been so wonderful since it makes it so easy to take books with you anywhere. I truly cannot imagine my life without the NLS program.

– NLS patron

BARD Mobile, BARD Express, and My Talking Books

Once you’ve enrolled for BARD, there are many resources to help you. Also check for updates to BARD, BARD Mobile, and BARD Express on our BARD and BARD Mobile Update page.

BARD

The Welcome to BARD and BARD Frequently Asked Questions pages have answers to most of the questions newcomers ask. These pages are also available in Spanish: Bienvenido a BARD and Preguntas frecuentes de BARD.

Learn more by listening to the BARD Basics MP3 audio file while reviewing the short BARD Basics Handout [PDF].

Attend The Many Faces of BARD online events.

Or subscribe to the NLS BARD support list to receive the latest new about BARD.

BARD Mobile

Go mobile with BARD available as an app for your smart phone or tablet. To download the free BARD Mobile app, go to the App Store External for iOS phones and other devices or the Play Store External or Amazon Kindle Appstore External for Android or Fire mobile devices.

iOS

For help with iOS, access the BARD Mobile User Manual for iOS [HTML] or BARD Mobile User Manual for iOS in electronic braille [BRF] volume one and BARD Mobile User Manual for iOS in electronic braille [BRF] volume two. You can also watch videos on How to Use BARD Mobile for iOS External.

Android

For help with Android, access the BARD Mobile User Manual for Android [HTML] or BARD Mobile User Manual for Android in electronic braille [BRF]. You can also watch videos on How to Use BARD Mobile for Android External.

Fire

For help with a Fire Tablets, access BARD Mobile User Manual for Fire Tablets [HTML] or BARD Mobile User Manual for Fire Tablet in electronic braille [BRF].

BARD Express

Do you already have a BARD account and a PC and want to begin using BARD Express? Download BARD Express now.

PC-users can learn more about BARD Express for downloading and transferring to players, as well as playing on the built-in BARD Express audio player, by visiting the Welcome to BARD Express page or browsing the Frequently Asked Questions about BARD Express page and the BARD Express User Guide page. You can also watch videos on How to Use BARD Express External.

My Talking Books skill on smart speaker

The My Talking Books skill on Amazon Alexa smart speakers is available to NLS patrons using BARD. Patrons need to register in order to access the service. The registration form can be found at www.surveymonkey.com/r/mtb-open-beta External. For patrons who are minors, a parent or legal guardian must sign a parental acknowledgement form found at www.surveymonkey.com/r/mtb-parental-acknowledgment External. Patrons will be notified by email that they may access the service. Patrons participating in the open beta will be asked to report any issues they encounter so these can be promptly addressed.

Learn more about My Talking Books by reading the My Talking Books User Guide. Get up to speed with voice commands for interacting with the skill by reading the My Talking Book Command List.

Still need help with something related to BARD?

You can send a detailed email to [email protected] with your specific questions. You can also learn more about how to use BARD and NLS equipment through patron services and online events or by accessing any of the users guides and other documentation mentioned above on this page.

The BARD Mobile app is easy to use. The narrators are superb.

– NLS patron

How to Enroll

You can apply for NLS service by requesting an application online or directly contacting the NLS network library that serves your state or locality. You can also complete a printable application form and send it to your NLS network library. Applications are available in English and Spanish. A family member or friend can fill out an application for you. The application must be signed by a competent authority. To learn more, review the eligibility requirements. The network library will contact you to confirm your enrollment and provide further information on equipment, reading material, and services. Assisted living communities, schools, hospitals, rehabilitation centers, libraries, and other facilities with a number of eligible users can apply for NLS services as an institution.

Once you’re enrolled, you can contact your network library to get access to BARD, the Braille and Audio Reading Download online service. With BARD, you don’t need to wait for books and magazines to arrive in the mail—you can download them and start reading right away. And if you have an iOS or Android device (including a Kindle Fire device), you can enjoy books and magazines on the go with the BARD Mobile app.

Do you play an instrument or want to learn one? The NLS Music collection includes braille and large-print scores, recorded instructional materials, and recorded materials about music and musicians. Learn more about what’s available in the Music collection and how to request music materials.

You don’t have to live in the US to be an NLS patron. If you’re a US citizen living abroad full- or part-time, you can receive books and magazine through the NLS Overseas Service.

Besides books and magazines, NLS provides playback equipment—including a braille eReader—and accessories such as breath switches and remote controls. We also share information about purchasing NLS-compatible audio players and braille displays and notetakers.

Here’s what people are saying about NLS Services:

This service is wonderful. I have macular degeneration in both eyes, can no longer read, and I receive this service and am most grateful.

– NLS patron

Apply for NLS Services

The National Library Service for the Blind and Print Disabled (NLS), Library of Congress, is a free braille and talking book library service for people with temporary or permanent low vision, blindness, or a physical, perceptual, or reading disability that prevents them from reading or holding the printed page. Through a national network of cooperating libraries, NLS offers books and magazines the way you want them: in braille or audio formats, instantly downloadable or mailed to your door for free. NLS offers a wide variety of titles, including bestsellers, biographies, fictional works, how-to books, magazines, and music scores and instructional materials.

If you or someone you know is interested in receiving talking book or braille service, please take the following steps:

  1. Read the eligibility guidelines below to determine if you or the interested individual is eligible for service.
  2. Request an application for service or search the local network library address list or call 888-657-7323 to locate the network library that would serve the eligible party. You can also contact the network library by email, telephone, fax, or in writing by mail. If the eligible individual is an American citizen living outside of the US, please visit our page on Overseas Service.
  3. Complete and return the application with a signature from one of the listed certifying authorities. (You can also find and complete a printable application form and mail it to your local network library with a signature from a certifying authority.)
  4. Wait for the network library to contact you or the eligible party with further steps or the news of enrollment. Once enrolled, the network library will provide further information on available equipment, reading material, and other services and resources.
  5. Start using NLS!

If you are unable to locate a library or would like additional assistance, please contact NLS or call 202-707-5100 or 800-424-8567 toll-free.

Eligibility for individual service

Any resident of the United States or American citizen living abroad who is unable to read or use regular print materials as a result of temporary or permanent visual or physical limitations may receive service through NLS. This includes those who are blind or have a visual, physical, perceptual, or reading disability that prevents them from reading or handling print materials.

Who can qualify?

NLS provides service to individuals who fall into any of the following categories:

  1. Blind persons whose visual acuity, as determined by competent authority, is 20/200 or less in the better eye with correcting lenses, or whose widest diameter of visual field subtends an angular distance no greater than 20 degrees.
  2. Persons whose visual disability, with correction and regardless of optical measurement, is certified by competent authority as preventing the reading of standard printed material
  3. Persons certified by competent authority as unable to read or unable to use standard printed material as a result of physical limitations.
  4. Persons certified by competent authority as having a perceptual or reading disability and unable to read printed works to substantially the same degree as a person without an impairment or disability.
  5. Persons eligible for service (falling into any of the above categories) who are now living as residents of the United States (including its territories, insular possessions, and the District of Columbia), or are American citizens eligible for service who are now living abroad, or dependents of active military personnel or diplomats.

This includes individuals who have had a qualifying disability from birth, individuals who are disabled because of medical conditions or trauma, and individuals who become disabled as they age. Individuals who have a temporary disability may qualify for service on a temporary basis. Individuals who are blind or have a print disability and who have been honorably discharged from the armed forces of the United States receive special priority.

Who is a “competent authority”?

Regarding NLS eligibility, “competent authority” is defined to include:

  • Doctors of medicine
  • Doctors of osteopathy
  • Ophthalmologists
  • Optometrists
  • Registered nurses
  • Therapists
  • Professional staff of hospitals, institutions, and public or private welfare agencies, such as an educator, social worker, case worker, counselor, rehabilitation teacher, certified reading specialist, school psychologist, superintendent, or librarian.

In the absence of any of these, certification may be made by professional librarians or by any person whose competence under specific circumstances is acceptable to the Library of Congress.

Request an application for service.

Ease of Use

With simple features and clear instructions, NLS makes it easy to read your way, no matter which format you choose.

NLS talking-book players feature large buttons, braille letters beside the controls, a sleep timer, and built-in audio instructions.

The BARD and BARD Mobile app allows users to quickly and easily download “Braille and Audio Reading Downloads” (BARD) directly to their iOS and Android devices. Watch our series of instructional videos for BARD Mobile External.

“It’s huge to be able to have access to the same books all your friends are reading. To be able to talk about the same books as others are reading, as a kid and as an adult, is about being part of the community, being able to engage. Imagination, adventure, reading! It’s all about that but also to make connections with others about what you’re reading, and even make friends through what you are reading. It is one of those things that brings us all together and it is hugely important.”

– Brian M., NLS patron since 1985

Request NLS Equipment for NLS Materials

Table of Contents
  • How to request equipment
    • Audiobooks players

  • Want to know more about BARD and equipment to play BARD books and magazine?

How to request equipment

The National Library Service for the Blind and Print Disabled (NLS) loans playback equipment free of charge for use with NLS books and magazines. NLS equipment and its collections are available to individuals who have difficulty reading regular print and who are NLS patrons.

Audiobooks players

Playback equipment needed to read audiobooks (called “talking books”) and magazines is circulated to patrons by network libraries via postage-free mail, just as talking books on cartridges are. Digital audio and electronic braille materials are also available through BARD (Braille and Audio Reading Download (BARD) online service. BARD audio materials can be downloaded onto cartridges and USB flash devices. Currently, there are three NLS players. Interested NLS patrons should ask their network library about receiving a player.

Patrons can also read NLS books on their personal smart devices, freeing up NLS equipment for other patrons, by using the BARD Mobile app or the My Talking Books skill for smart speakers.

Accessories for the players include adapters for USB drives, lightweight headphones, breath switches, and remote controls. Readers with significant hearing loss may request a high-volume player and headphones. For more information about these accessories, patrons should contact their network library.

NLS eReader

NLS has been authorized to loan refreshable braille displays to eligible readers. The NLS Braille eReader allows NLS patrons to read electronic braille files (BRFs) as well as Word files, HTML files, DAISY files, and PDF files. The unit has 20 braille cells, a Perkins-style keyboard, internal memory, and the ability to use an SD card and a USB drive for external storage. It connects to Wi-Fi so that braille files can be downloaded directly from BARD and has Bluetooth capability so patrons can connect directly to a smartphone and read the info on the screen in braille. It also can connect to a computer so that the information on the screen will be displayed on the braille display. Other features include a braille translation application and settings to display files in uncontracted and contracted braille. It can connect directly to NFB Newsline. Its navigational capability includes searching for a passage and moving by sentence, paragraph, and volume. Interested NLS patrons should ask their network library about receiving an eReader.

To learn more or request equipment and accessories, just contact your local network library.

Need to return your equipment?

Playback equipment and accessories are supplied to eligible persons on extended loan. If this equipment is not being used for reading recorded material provided by the Library of Congress and its cooperating libraries, please return it to the issuing agency. NLS equipment and materials are the property of the Library of Congress and should not be sold, reproduced and/or copied.

In the event you find NLS property, such as machines or accessories, please return them immediately one of two ways:

  • Take items to a local network library; a list of these libraries can be found on our Find Your Library page.
  • Package items in any securely sealed box and send through the U.S. Postal Service to the address: Equipment Control Officer, The Library of Congress, NLS, Washington, DC 20542. This package may be marked FREE MATTER FOR THE BLIND and sent with no postage.
Want to know more about BARD and equipment to play BARD books and magazine?

The BARD (Braille and Audio Reading Download) is a web-based service that provides access to braille and talking books, magazines, and music scores available from NLS. The service is available to registered users of the braille and talking book program through their local braille and talking book library. Learn more about BARD.

Braille books and magazines may be also downloaded through the BARD Mobile app, which requires connection to a refreshable braille display via a Bluetooth connection. NLS loans braille eReaders to patrons. The BARD website and mobile app are password-protected, and all files are in an electronically downloadable form of compressed audio or formatted braille. BARD is available to registered users of NLS through their local braille and talking book library.

BARD Players available from NLS include the most recent machine DA2 and other talking book players.

Learn more about the NLS Braille eReader for use with the BARD.

To learn earn more about where to purchase commercial players, visit our resource on braille writing equipment or other assistive technology

BARD Mobile Apps

BARD Mobile for iOS devices External delivers braille and talking books to your iPhone, iPad, or iPod touch. BARD Mobile for Android External delivers talking books to your Android smartphone or tablet. And BARD Mobile for Fire tablets External delivers braille and talking books on your Amazon Fire tablet. Commercially Available Reading Apps for reading are available from commercial vendors.

The BARD Mobile app is easy to use. The narrators are superb.

– NLS patron

Enroll for Music Service and Materials

The Music Section of the National Library Service for the Blind and Print Disabled (NLS), Library of Congress, includes more than 25,000 titles including braille and large-print scores and recorded instructional materials about music and musicians. It is the largest accessible collection of its kind in the world. Music-related magazines in audio and braille are also available. While patrons receive most NLS services through the network of cooperating libraries, NLS music materials circulate directly to patrons from the NLS headquarters in Washington, DC.

Enrollment Process

Any person eligible for NLS service is also eligible for NLS music services. Patrons already registered with a cooperating network library should contact the NLS Music Section directly either via phone (800-424-8567 ext. 2) or via email ([email protected]). Eligible individuals who are not yet registered with a network library can receive more information by calling 888-NLS-READ (888-657-7323) or visiting the Apply for NLS Service page.

Though phone and email are the most efficient means of contact, the Music Section may also be contacted by mail at:

Music Section
The National Library Service for the Blind and Print Disabled
Library of Congress
Washington, DC 20542

Request Overseas Services

The overseas program serves US citizens living outside of the United States who are unable to use regular print materials because of a visual impairment or a physical or reading disability. Eligible non-US citizens who are dependents of active-duty military or diplomatic personnel may also apply for NLS service. Overseas patrons have full access to the NLS collection of books and magazines.

Enrollment Process

Overseas patrons must meet basic NLS eligibility requirements and complete an NLS application. In addition, they must supply proof of citizenship or service member’s orders listing them as a dependent.

Patrons already registered with a cooperating network library who are planning to live abroad for six months or longer may transfer their service to the overseas library service. They do not need to complete an additional service application but will still need to contact the overseas librarian and supply proof of citizenship or service member’s orders listing them as a dependent.

An email to [email protected] is the most efficient means of submitting a signed copy of the application and citizenship documentation to the overseas librarian. However, the overseas librarian may also be contacted by mail:

Overseas Librarian
Library of Congress
The National Library Service for the Blind and Print Disabled
Washington, DC 20542
USA

What Proofs of Citizenship Are Acceptable?

A US passport number or a copy of a birth certificate showing the place of birth is acceptable proof of citizenship. A Social Security number is not acceptable for proof. Eligible non-US citizens who are dependents of active-duty military or diplomatic personnel should send a photocopy of the service member’s orders posting them abroad and listing the applicant as a dependent.

What Happens after I Enroll?

You will be contacted by the overseas librarian, who will work with you to supply any equipment you requested. Once enrolled, you will have full access to the NLS collection, including recreational and informational reading for adults and children at all grade levels, in audio and in braille. All overseas patrons will also automatically receive an electronic subscription to Overseas Outlook via email. Learn more on the Overseas Services page.

Catalog and BARD

Explore now the NLS Catalog and BARD (Braille and Audio Reading Download online service) to locate items in NLS’s vast collection. Recently added titles use publisher-provided descriptions to make it easier for readers to identify titles of interest.

Talking Book Topics, Braille Book Review, and International Language Quarterly also list selections of titles recently added to the NLS collection. Your network library can help you find more books or select books for you that match your interests. Contact your network library to learn more.

NLS Catalog

The main NLS Catalog search has many features that make it easy to enjoy our vast collection of audio and braille titles.

  • Optimizes search functionality and speed
  • Directly links to BARD from catalog listings
  • Allows patrons to submit a request to NLS for a title not found in the catalog
  • Makes it easy to create and print book lists
  • Allows patrons to sort search results by date (newest to oldest or oldest to newest)
  • Includes:
    • an ask-a-librarian feature, which goes to the NLS Information Services section
    • an email button so patrons can send themselves information about a title
    • optimized searching and cross-searching via links by author, subject, and format
    • the ability to create saved searches
    • automatic search history tabs
    • optimized short and long-form formatting for printing
    • easy-to-find links to the Find Your Library page and other NLS resources
BARD

The Braille and Audio Reading Download online service has several features that make it easy to search and immediately enjoy our vast collection of audio and braille titles.

  • Optimizes search functionality with subjects, language, and account settings
  • Direct links for download titles immediately or to save to a wish list for future downloading
  • Links to previous downloads and subscriptions to magazines
  • Works with BARD Mobile, BARD Express, and My Talking Book skill on smart speaker

You can ask your library to sign you up for BARD, so you can download titles through your computer or your mobile device using the BARD Mobile apps. Learn more about BARD and BARD Mobile on the sign up and accessing BARD page. And the latest version of BARD Express allows you to play audio books and magazines on your PC computer. To order titles from the catalog without using BARD, contact your network library and ask them about options for ordering titles by phone, email, or mail.

BARD on the web

Welcome to BARD, version 2.0

Welcome to the NLS Braille Audio and Reading Download (BARD) website!

BARD participation

Eligible users may download and read any book, magazine, or other material found on the BARD website.

You may not share any materials downloaded from this site. It is also strictly forbidden to share your user ID and password. The only exception is for NLS patrons who rely on trusted personal assistants to access BARD. In that case, those patrons are responsible for ensuring their assistants’ compliance with the BARD user agreement. Your access to BARD can be terminated if you are found in violation of the agreement.

You may be tempted to download as many items as possible. There is no need to do that—the materials are not going away. Please download books and other items only as you need them.

Using the BARD website
Logging in

The address for the BARD website is https://nlsbard.loc.gov. The site has been tested with the Google Chrome, Microsoft Edge, Safari, and Firefox web browsers. The site has been tested using screen reading and screen magnification software.

When your application for BARD access is approved by your NLS cooperating library, you will be sent an email welcoming you to BARD. That email will contain a link that, when selected, opens the BARD website in your browser where you will be prompted to create a new password. Important: This must be done through a web browser, not the BARD Mobile app.

Make sure to follow the password rules listed below:

  • Your password must contain a minimum of eight characters, with a maximum of 32 characters.
  • Your new password cannot contain any special characters, such as !@#$%^&*.

Reminder: You may not share your log-in ID and password with anyone except a trusted personal assistant!

If you ever forget your BARD password:

  1. Open the BARD login page.
  2. Activate the Reset your BARD password here link.
  3. Enter your email address and select the “Submit” button. You will be sent an email with a link to reset your password.
  4. Choose the link in the email to open a web page where you can create a new password.
Pledge information

When you first log in to the BARD website, a BARD user pledge will appear. You must select “I agree” in order to use the service. Should you inadvertently select: “I do not agree,” you will be given the opportunity to reconsider and the pledge will appear again.

The BARD user pledge will appear any time you change your email address.

The BARD Main Page

Once you have agreed to the BARD pledge, you will be presented with the BARD Main Page. This page contains links to lists of books, magazines, and music materials, along with links to other helpful BARD-related pages. There are five headings on the main page: BARD Main Page, Browse and Search Books, Browse by Subject, Other Ways to Access BARD, along with the page footer. These headings will be described in more detail in the below sections.

My BARD

Under the BARD Main Page heading is a button called “My BARD.” When expanded, additional links are displayed including:

  • Wish list
  • Reading History
  • Subscriptions
  • Account Settings

Each of these are described in more detail below.

Wish list

The Wish list is a place to keep track of books you are interested in reading but not yet ready to download. The number of result pages is affected by the number of results you choose to show at a time. This can be configured in Account Settings under My BARD. The Wish list can be filtered and/or searched based on desired criteria. Each Wish list record displays the title, author, subject, narrator (for audiobooks) or number of volumes (for braille books), and a description. The only exception to this is magazines, since they do not have the same cataloging information. This information is followed by a “Take Action” button (including the name of the item) that, when expanded, reveals three additional links:

  • Download the item
  • Remove the item
  • Prioritize the item
Searching the Wish list by keyword

Upon navigating to your Wish list, locate the Search Wish list field. Type your search keyword and submit your search. Results based on your keyword search will display, each item under its own heading. To return to viewing the entire Wish list, there is a “Clear Search” button that can be activated to clear the previously entered search criteria and display the full Wish list.

Refining your Wish list

Upon navigating to your Wish list, there is a button named Refine Your Wish list, which lets you sort the Wish list, view the format such as audio or braille, view the type such as books, magazines, or music, view by language, and view by subject. After any refinements are made, activate the “Refine Results” button to refine the list. Activating the “Reset All Fields” button at any time will clear the refinements you’ve made and return you to the full Wish list.

Reading History

Reading History displays a detailed record of all the items you have previously accessed. In most cases, the Reading History records display the title, author, subject, narrator (for audiobooks) or number of volumes (for braille books), and a description (if available). This information is followed by a “Take Action” button (including the name of the item) that, when expanded, reveals two links: one to download the item and one to add the item to your Wish list.

Searching your Reading History by Keyword

Upon navigating to your Reading History, locate the search Reading History field. Type in your search keyword and submit your search. Results based on your keyword search will display, each item under its own heading. To return to viewing the entire Reading History, activate the “Clear Search” button to clear the previously entered search criteria and display your full Reading History.

Refining your Reading History

There is a button named Refine Your Reading History, which lets you sort the titles, view the format such as audio or braille, view the type such as books, magazines, or music, view by language, and view by subject. After any refinements are made, activate the “Refine Results” button to refine the list. Activating the “Reset All Fields” button at any time will clear any refinements you had made and return you to the full list of your Reading History.

Subscriptions

The Subscriptions page offers details on which book series and magazines you are subscribed to. When first loaded, the displayed results are book series. The link “Go to Subscriptions: Magazines” will display magazine subscriptions. When your list of magazine subscriptions is displayed, the link “Go to Subscriptions: Book Series” will display the list of book series you are subscribed to. In either case, your subscriptions are listed under the Subscriptions heading. After each subscription title, there is a link to unsubscribe from that item. Activating that link results in a confirmation page confirming you have unsubscribed from that item.

Account Settings

The Account Settings page is where you can make changes to different account settings and submit a request to register a commercial player that will be used to play material downloaded from BARD. Listed under the Display and Account Preferences heading are these settings:

  • Language Interface Settings
  • Color Settings
  • Display Result Formats
  • Display Size
  • Maximum Number of Books to be Displayed on One Page
  • Always remove magazine issues that have been on your Wish List for more than a year
  • Alert me by e-mail when new issues of subscribed books or magazines are available

Following the above settings, and under the Account Settings Links heading, are four additional links:

  • Change Your Password
  • Change Your Email Address
  • Add a Purchased Player
  • Change Your Language Preferences
Language Interface Settings

You have the option to set the language of the BARD interface to English, Spanish or French. Choose your desired language interface preference from the Language settings drop-down menu and then select the “Update” button.

Color Settings

If you wish, change the color for the BARD website. Select Black/White from the drop-down menu labeled Color settings to change the BARD interface text color to black on white and then select the “Update” button.

Display Result Formats

You have the option to modify your format display results. The default setting is to display both braille and audiobooks. Select Audio Only or Braille Only from the drop-down menu labeled Display results and then select the “Update” button to change your display results.

Display Size

To modify the magnification display size, select Theme 1: 1.5XMagnification or Theme 2: 2.0XMagnification from the drop-down menu labeled Display size and then select the “Update” button to change your display size.

Maximum Number of Books to be Displayed on One Page

Set the number of books per page displayed as a result when performing a search. Choose 25, 50, 100, or 250 from the drop-down menu labeled “Maximum number of books to be displayed” and then select the “Update” button.

Always remove magazine issues that have been on your Wish List for more than a year

You have the option to have BARD remove magazine issues that have been on your Wish list for more than one year. To have issued removed, choose “yes” from the drop-down menu called “Always remove magazine issues that have been on your Wish List for more than a year.” If you don’t want those issues removed, choose “no.”

Alert me by e-mail when new issues of subscribed books or magazines are available for download

If you wish to be notified when new items you are subscribed to become available and added to your Wish list, choose “yes” from the drop-down menu called “Alert me by e-mail when new issues of subscribed books or magazines are available.” If you do not wish to receive alerts, choose “no.”

Change Your Password

There are a number of ways to change the password on your BARD account. From within MY BARD, choose Account Settings, and select the Change Your Password link.

To update your BARD password:

  • Type your new password in the “New password” field.
  • Confirm the password in the “Confirm new password” field.
  • After choosing the “Change password” button, BARD will confirm that the password has been updated successfully.

Make sure to follow the password rules listed below:

  • Your password must contain a minimum of eight characters, with a maximum of 32 characters.
  • Your new password cannot contain any special characters, such as !@#$%^&*.

Reminder: You may not share your log-in ID and password with anyone except a trusted personal assistant!

After establishing your new password, BARD will tell you the password has been successfully changed. Choose the link Click here to return to the BARD main page.

Add a Purchased Player

If you wish to use a commercial digital talking-book player to read materials downloaded from BARD, it must be registered to your BARD account. To register a player, use the “Account Settings” link under My BARD, then choose the link “Add a new player” and then fill out the form on the resulting page. A notification will be sent to your network library to approve the purchased player request. Once your library approves the request, a notification will be sent to your player’s manufacturer to authorize the player to be used to play NLS audio materials. Manufacturer response times may vary.

Change Your Language Preferences

Use this preference setting to identify the languages in which you would like to read materials. Check the box associated with each preferred language and select the “Update” button. Note that any language selected from the language drop-down menu adjacent to the “Search the collection” field on the BARD Main page will replace this preference setting during that search.

Browse and Search Books

There are two links under this heading to access material available for download: Recently Added Books and Magazines and Most Popular books.

How to download an item from BARD to your computer

After you have found the item you wish to download, activate the “Take Action” button that follows the annotation, followed by the Download link to begin the download. The book or magazine will be downloaded to the Downloads folder, unless you have changed the downloads location in your browser settings. Each audio file starts with the letters DB and each braille file starts with the letters BR. All files downloaded from BARD end in .zip.
Reading a DB file on your NLS player

The file you download from BARD is a single zipped file. Once the download is complete, use the software of your choice to unzip the contents of the zipped file. It is recommended that users unzip each item to a folder on their hard drive and copy the newly unzipped folder onto a blank cartridge. Note that the number and placement of navigational markers in the audio items (e.g., chapter boundaries) will vary according to the type of item it is, its production date, and anticipated use. The downloaded items do not carry the text of the print copy, only a narrated version that adheres to the print copy’s contents as closely as possible.

Recently Added Books and Magazines

This link lists books and magazines by the date that they were added to the collection, with newer additions appearing at the top. By selecting a preferred language in Account Settings, books in that preferred language will be displayed. To refine your results, select the “Refine Recently Added Results” button to find various ways to refine what you see on this page. Options include choosing a format such as audio or braille, choosing a language if you want to see results in a language other than the preferred language you have selected in Account Settings, and by subject. Note: You can choose more than one subject if you wish to see titles in more than one subject on this page.

Most Popular Books

The next link on the BARD main page lists the most popular audio and braille books downloaded during the past 30 days. This list defaults to displaying fiction only. To choose nonfiction, select Go to most popular nonfiction. To refine your results, select Refine Most Popular Results to see various ways to refine what you see on this page. Options include choosing a format such as audio or braille, choosing a language if you want to see results in a language other than the preferred language you chose in Account Settings, and by subject. Note: You can choose more than one subject if you wish to see titles in more than one subject on this page.

Search Books

The next item on the BARD Main page is the “Search Books” field. Enter a search term in the “Search Books” field and then choose the “Go” button. BARD will search for the term in the author, narrator, subject, annotation, and book number. Note that putting your search term in quotes (” “) will limit the search to that exact phrase. Also note that author’s names are listed last name, comma, first name and narrator names are listed first name then last name.

  • To search by author, type the author’s name is quotes, such as “Smith, John.”
  • To search by narrator, type the narrator’s name in quotes. For example, “Michelle Reader.”
  • To search by title, type the title or partial title into the search box. Narrow your search by typing your search criteria in quotes in order to find books with that exact search criteria. For example, “After the firm.” Remember that this is a keyword search, so it is possible that your search criteria could display in the annotation, not necessarily in the title.
  • To search by annotation, type one or more keywords into the search box. Narrow your search by typing your search criteria in quotes in order to find books with that exact search criteria. For example, “After the firm.” Remember that this is a keyword search, so it is possible that your search criteria could display in the annotation, not necessarily in the title.
  • To search by book number, type the number into the search box. (Note that some materials have the same item number but a different prefix, as they are available in multiple formats from different producers. Adding the prefix will limit the results.) Because this is a keyword search, remember the book number could appear in the annotation, and therefore not as a search result.
  • To search by language, type your search term in the search box and select the language from the language drop-down list.
Advanced Search for Books

This link will take you to an advanced search page where you can tell BARD exactly what you are looking for. You can search by title, book number, author, the original publisher, the format of the book you’re looking for, by language, and by subject. The more search criteria used, the more accurate your search will be.

Music scores and books Link

Choose this link to take you to the BARD music collection main page, titled Music Scores and Books, where there is a description of the music materials available (books and scores). The music collection main page has search and browse functions identical to those on the BARD main page, but they return results from the music collection only. Entering a term in the field labeled “Search music” and then selecting the “Go” button will search for the term in author/composer, narrator, subject, annotation, and book number.

Advanced Search for Music Scores and Books:

Choosing the link Advance search for music scores and books, you will be presented with a list of the following ways to search:

  • To search by author/composer or narrator, type the last name or full name in the search box.
  • To search by title, type the entire or partial title into the search box. Note that there are some titles in the collection in a foreign language, so you will not get all relevant results if searching in English terms only.
  • To search by subject, type the subject into the search box.
  • To search by annotation, type one or more keywords into the search box.
  • Adding the prefix will limit the results.
  • The “Browse by subject” option lists all available materials in the music collection by subject. Choose the subject from the list and then select the “Go” button.
Browse and Search Magazines

To search for magazines, choose Magazines from the BARD main page. On the resulting page, you can find the most recent issues posted for each magazine by choosing the link Most recently added magazine issues. To see the list of issues for a specific magazine, highlight the magazine in the drop-down menu and select the “Go” button. You will be taken to a list of the available issues for that magazine. Note that each magazine in the drop-down list is followed by its format (braille or audio) in parenthesis.

Browse by Subject

To browse all available books on BARD by subject, open the drop-down menu on the BARD main page, highlight the subject, and choose the “Go” button. To refine your results, open the Refine your search results page. Refine the results by format or language. If you wish to include any additional subjects in your search, choose the subjects, then select the “Refine Results” button to search BARD with your updated refinements. To clear your refinements and return to the search results page, choose the “Reset all fields” button.

Other ways to access BARD

In addition to the website, there are multiple ways patrons can access BARD services.

BARD Express

Opening The BARD Express link, you will find information about the interface such as The Frequently Asked Questions and the BARD Express user guide, along with a link to download the Windows-based software. BARD Express simplifies finding, downloading, managing, and transferring BARD audio materials to be played on digital talking-book players. BARD Express also allows for audiobooks and magazines to be played right on a computer, so transferring to another player is not necessary.

BARD Mobile for iOS

Choosing BARD Mobile for iOS External from the BARD main page opens the Apple App Store, where you can read about the app and download BARD Mobile for iOS for finding and playing BARD materials on a iOS mobile device.

BARD Mobile for Android

Choosing BARD Mobile for Android External from the BARD main page provides information and download access to the BARD Mobile application for Android devices.

BARD Mobile for Amazon Fire OS

Open BARD Mobile for Amazon Fire OS External to read about BARD Mobile for Amazon Fire tablets and to download the app from the Amazon App Store to play BARD materials on a Fire device.

Latest DTBM firmware and installations instructions

This link will take you to a web page that contains instructions and an installer package for obtaining the latest DTBM firmware.

Footer
NLS Catalog Quick Search

Opening the NLS Catalog Quick Search will take you to a text-based interface where you can search the NLS collection.

NLS Catalog (full records)

The full NLS Catalog has many features that let you perform detailed and flexible searches of the NLS collection. While this catalog refers to the same database as the NLS Catalog Quick Search, the full interface to the catalog provides the ability to perform advanced searches of the NLS collection. Additional functions provided by the Catalog Full Record include:

  • Create and print book lists
  • Sort search results by date (newest to oldest or oldest to newest)
  • Direct links to BARD from catalog listings
  • An ask-a-librarian feature, which will direct your question to the NLS Information Services section
  • An email button so patrons can send themselves information about a title
  • Optimized searching and cross-searching via links by author, subject, and format
  • The ability to create saved searches
  • Automatic search history tabs
  • Optimized short and long-form formatting for printing
  • Links to the Find Your Library page and other NLS resources
  • Allows patrons to submit a request to NLS for a title not found in the catalog
  • Allows librarians to easily create Lib Guides and print book lists
NLS Book Recommendation Form

Use the NLS Book Recommendation Form External to request books or magazines to be added to the national collection. The Collection Development Section will review your recommendations and consider the titles that meet the collection-building policy. Only books that were published within the past five years will be considered. Contact your cooperating network library if you encounter issues using this form.

Help and support

NLS Patrons have several ways to get help and support when using BARD.

Help

Choosing this link will open a page where you can select from BARD Frequently Asked Questions, and locate information for your NLS cooperating library. To locate the information for your library, choose your library in the drop-down menu, and choose the “Select” button.

BARD-Support mailing list for announcements

You can subscribe to an email-based list called BARD-Support. This is a closed, one-way list to which NLS posts information of interest to the BARD community. Replies to BARD-Support messages will not reach us. If you have questions, send an email to [email protected].

If you would like to subscribe, access the Library Of Congress subscription page. Enter your email address and select the “Submit” button. This will bring you to a confirmation page where you’ll be required to confirm your email address and choose your delivery preferences.

To unsubscribe, access the subscription page, enter your email address and select the “Submit” button. On the resulting page, consent to the Privacy Policy and select the “Submit” button. Check the list you want to unsubscribe from and select the “Submit” button.

Troubleshooting

If you have any technical problems using the BARD website or NLS digital talking-book player, check these instructions and the FAQ page of the BARD website to determine if a solution to the problem has already been posted.

If you do not find a solution in the FAQs, contact your library for help. You can find contact information for your NLS cooperating library on the BARD website.

If you need further assistance, email BARD support at [email protected]. The BARD support team will try to answer all emails as quickly as possible but cannot guarantee a specific turnaround time for problem resolution.

Be sure to include a complete description of the problem and of your computing environment, including any assistive technology products you are using. Remember that this email address is only monitored during library business hours.

If you have a commercial digital talking-book player, technical problems with your player should be directed to its manufacturer.

If you have an NLS eReader, contact your NLS cooperating library for support.

Canceling your BARD account

If you would like to cancel your BARD account, please contact your library. Your user ID and password will be deactivated.

Privacy Policy

View the NLS Privacy Policy to learn how we protect the privacy of our patrons.

Last updated: January 10, 2025
