How do I get a BARD account?

Contact your network library to request a BARD account. Once your librarian creates the account, you will receive an email welcoming you to BARD. That email will contain a web link that when chosen, a web browser will open to a page where you can create your BARD password.

What do I do if I need to change my password?

If you ever forget your BARD password, you have three options:

Option 1 for changing a password:
  1. Open the BARD login page.
  2. Activate the Reset your BARD password here link.
  3. Enter your email address and select the “Submit” button. You will be sent an email with a link to reset your password.
  4. Select the link in the email to open a web page where you can create a new password.
Option 2 for changing a password:

Contact your network library and tell them you’d like assistance resetting your BARD password.

Option 3 for changing a password:

Email [email protected] to request assistance resetting your BARD password.

How do I change my password when already logged in to my BARD account?

While logged in to the BARD website, expand the My BARD menu. Select the Account Settings link, then the Change Your Password link. On the resulting page, enter your email, then select the Submit button to have BARD send you an email that will contain a link to reset your password. When you select that link, your web browser will open to a page where you can create a new password. Be sure to follow the password rules:

  • Your password must contain between 8 and 32 characters.
  • Your new password cannot contain any special characters, such as !@#$%^&*.

Once your new password is accepted, you will receive an email confirming that you have changed your password. Note that you cannot change your password more than once in a 24-hour period.

What do I do if I am locked out of my account after ten attempts to log in?

Getting locked out of your BARD account is a result of too many failed attempts to log in to the service. Call your NLS network library and ask to have your BARD account unlocked. Consider creating a new password since you have been unsuccessful in your login attempts.

How can I change the email address on my BARD account?

Contact your NLS network library to change the email address on your BARD account.

Books, musical scores, magazines, and other materials
How many books and magazines are available for download?

Hundreds of thousands of titles are available on BARD. The number increases nearly every day.

What books are being added?

NLS contracts with agencies around the country to produce books in audio and braille to be added to BARD. Additionally, NLS adds digital books submitted by network libraries, some commercial audiobook producers, and international-language books acquired through the Marrakesh Treaty.

When are books added?

NLS-produced books are added to BARD as soon as they meet our rigorous quality checks. Books submitted by other agencies are added soon after they are submitted.

In what languages are books available on BARD?

Books are available on BARD in the following languages:

  • Afrikaans
  • Albanian
  • Algonquian languages
  • Amharic
  • Arabic
  • Armenian
  • Basque
  • Bengali
  • Bulgarian
  • Central Khmer
  • Cherokee
  • Chinese
  • Church Slavic
  • Croatian
  • Czech
  • Danish
  • Dutch
  • Finnish
  • French
  • German
  • Greek
  • Greek, Ancient
  • Gujarati
  • Hebrew
  • Hindi
  • Hmong
  • Hungarian
  • Italian
  • Japanese
  • Korean
  • Latvian
  • Lithuanian
  • Navajo
  • Nepali
  • Norwegian
  • Ojibwa
  • Panjabi
  • Persian
  • Polish
  • Portuguese
  • Romanian
  • Russian
  • Serbo-Croatian
  • Slovak
  • Slovenian
  • Somali
  • Spanish
  • Swahili
  • Tagalog
  • Telugu
  • Tigrinya
  • Turkish
  • Ukrainian
  • Urdu
  • Vietnamese
  • Welsh
  • Yiddish
  • Yoruba
Why are some books from a series missing? Why are some magazine issues missing?

While NLS always makes an effort to offer the complete book series, some books in a series are not available in a digital form. NLS continues to work toward filling incomplete book series. Please contact your network library if you think you have discovered a title missing from a series, a duplication, or some other inconsistency. The information will be forwarded to appropriate NLS staff for action.

NLS-produced magazine issues must meet our strict quality-control standards. In some cases, individual issues can be held up by production problems that cause them not to meet these standards. NLS works with its magazine producers to solve problems as quickly as possible so the issue can be posted to the website.

What do I do if I find a problem in a book or magazine?

Please report the problem—including the title, author, book prefix, and book number—to [email protected]. Your player can tell you the time elapsed as of your current reading position. If possible, please include this information in your email. Listing the book number and its prefix in your email’s subject line is also helpful.

In cases where the book has a prefix of DBC, contact the producing library to report quality control issues.

What is available here that I cannot get from my network library?

All NLS-produced materials are available at your library on another medium.

BARD includes materials produced by commercial audiobook publishers and network libraries that might not be available at your library. However, your library might be able to get a copy of some materials it does not have through interlibrary loan. Contact your library for more information.

Special collection materials, such as music and international-language books, also may not be available at your NLS library. Contact the NLS Music Section either via phone, 800-424-8567 ext. 2, or via email, [email protected], to request special format music materials. International-language materials can be obtained by your NLS library through interlibrary loan.

What is not available here that I can get from my NLS library?

Your NLS library has access to the entire NLS collection, which is far more extensive than the downloadable collection on BARD. The absence of a title on BARD does not mean the item is not available at all. You should check your library’s catalog or the NLS catalog.

Can I request a title to be added to BARD?

You are welcome to request books or magazines to be added to the collection. The Collection Development Section will review your recommendations and consider the titles that meet NLS’s collection-building policy. Only books that were published within the past five years will be considered. Visit the NLS Collection Building Policy for more information. Please use the link for the Book Recommendation Form External to make your request. Contact your local network library if you encounter issues using this form.

Can I check to see if a particular title is going to be added to the collection?

Books are added to the NLS catalog when they are selected for the collection and put into production. If a book is listed as IN PROCESS in the catalog, it will appear on BARD when production and quality checks have been completed.

How long are magazine issues kept?

At this time, NLS plans to keep magazine issues available indefinitely.

How do I find a book to read?

Enter a search term in the “Search Books” field and then choose the “Go” button. BARD will search for the term in the author, narrator, subject, annotation, and book number. Note that putting your search term in quotes (” “) will limit the search to that exact phrase. Also note that authors’ names are listed last name, comma, first name. Narrators’ names are listed first name then last name.

  • To search by author, type the author’s name in quotes, such as “Smith, John.”
  • To search by narrator, type the narrator’s name in quotes. For example, “Michelle Reader.”
  • To search by title, type the title or partial title into the search box. Narrow your search by typing your search criteria in quotes in order to find books with that exact search criteria. For example, “After the firm.” Remember that this is a keyword search, so it is possible that your search criteria could display in the annotation, not necessarily in the title.
  • To search by annotation, type one or more keywords into the search box. Narrow your search by typing your search criteria in quotes in order to find books with that exact search criteria. For example, “After the firm.” Remember that this is a keyword search, so it is possible that your search criteria could display in the annotation, not necessarily in the title.
  • To search by book number, type the number into the search box. (Note that some materials have the same item number but a different prefix, as they are available in multiple formats from different producers. Adding the prefix will limit the results.) Because this is a keyword search, remember the book number could appear in the annotation, and therefore not as a search result.
  • To search by language, type your search term in the search box and select the language from the language drop-down list.
What is the quickest way to find a specific book?

Choosing the “Advanced Search for Books” link on the BARD main page will take you to an advanced search page where you can tell BARD exactly what you are looking for. You can search by title, book number, author, narrator, publisher, format, language, and subject. The more search criteria used, the more accurate your search will be.

I just downloaded a book to my computer, but now I can’t find it. Where did my download go?

Each web browser handles downloads slightly differently. Unless you have directed the browser to save your downloaded files elsewhere, books will be downloaded to your Downloads folder by default.

I want to download an individual volume of a braille book. Can I do that?

Individual volumes of digital braille books are available by accessing the collection through the online catalog. Follow these steps:

  1. Go to the NLS Catalog Quick Search.
  2. Type all or part of the title in the Title field.
  3. Type the words “web braille” in the Keyword field. Note that the star or asterisk (*) may not be used as a wild card when using the keyword edit field.
  4. Select the Search button.
  5. Links to available downloadable braille volumes are within each record on the resulting page.
  6. After selecting a link to the desired downloadable volume (which will always start with “http://hdl.loc.gov”) users are automatically directed to BARD. If prompted, users must enter their BARD log-in ID and password to download their selection.
Can I put more than one audiobook on a cartridge?

This depends on how your player handles multiple books.

If you are using a commercial player, please see its instructions for the answer to this question.

If you are using an NLS digital player, multiple books can be loaded onto a cartridge. Each book can then be accessed by using the machine’s Sequential Play feature. To use this feature, press the Play/Stop button to go to the next title when the player says “End of book.” The player also has a Bookshelf feature that lists all of the books on the cartridge. To enter bookshelf mode on your NLS player, hold down the Play button until you hear the player announce “Bookshelf” and the number of books on the bookshelf. Release the Play button, then tap the arrows at either end of the Play button to move to the available books. When you hear the title you wish to read, simply press the Play button.

If you have further questions about using your NLS player to play multiple books, please contact the library that loaned the player to you. Note: All files must be in the proper format (digital audio) to be read with the NLS players. Note that the players will not recognize braille titles.

How do I know if the audiobook I want will fit on my cartridge?

The BARD website and BARD Mobile app list the reading time of books. Reading time translates to file size at a rate of approximately 11 megabytes per hour. A one gigabyte cartridge will hold almost 96 hours and a two-gigabyte cartridge will hold about 192 hours.

How do I move an audiobook to my player?

Unzip the file you downloaded to your computer, then copy the unzipped files to a thumb drive or NLS cartridge. There are several popular software packages that handle this function. However, it is also built into all versions of Windows and Mac OS.

When using a Windows-based computer, locate the unzipped books and copy them to the clipboard. Navigate to your thumb drive, cartridge, or commercial SD card and paste the folder. On a Mac, use the Command key instead of Control. Note that certain commercial players require NLS books and magazines to be in sub-directories in order for them to play.

Reading books
My player says I am not authorized to read this book. What does that mean?

This can be caused by three things:

  • If you have purchased a commercial player, you have not completed the steps needed to enable your player to use NLS materials. These players must be registered to your BARD account.
  • You have not completely transferred your digital talking book to your cartridge. This is usually the result of a copy operation that failed or an incomplete download from BARD.
  • You have not transferred your digital talking book into the necessary directory on your commercial player’s memory card. Please refer to your player’s user manual for information on where to place books on the memory card.
Can I listen to downloaded audiobooks on my computer?

Yes. NLS offers a software called BARD Express that includes a player that enables you to listen to books from a Windows-based computer. Visit the BARD Express main page to learn more about the program.

How do I read digital braille books?

NLS patrons are eligible to receive a twenty-cell Braille eReader that connects to BARD through a wireless Internet connection. If you do not have a wireless Internet connection, digital braille books and magazines can be delivered to you through the mail on a cartridge. When the cartridge is connected to the eReader, the books and magazines are automatically copied to the eReader. To receive an NLS Braille eReader, contact your NLS network library.

There are also a variety of devices that can be used to read digital braille materials. Electronic braille files can be read online or downloaded for offline reading or embossing with a braille display, braille-aware notetaker, or braille embosser. Zipped files must always be unzipped before being read.

How do I subscribe to the BARD support mailing list?

NLS patrons can subscribe to an email-based list called BARD-Support. This is a closed, one-way list to which NLS posts information of interest to the BARD community. Replies to BARD-Support messages will not reach us. If you have questions, email [email protected].
If you would like to subscribe, access the Library Of Congress subscription page. Enter your email address and select the Submit button. This will bring you to a confirmation page where you’ll be required to confirm your email address and choose your delivery preferences.

To unsubscribe, access the subscription page, enter your email address, and select the Submit button. On the resulting page, consent to the Privacy Policy and select the Submit button. Check the list you want to unsubscribe from and select the Submit button.

BARD Mobile for iOS devices
Who can use the NLS BARD Mobile app?

The NLS BARD Mobile app is free to download from the online App Store, but only eligible NLS patrons can use it. You must have an established BARD account (username and password) to download and read materials.

What books and magazines are available for download using the app?

NLS contracts with agencies around the country to produce books in audio and braille to be added to BARD. Additionally, NLS adds digital books submitted by network libraries, some commercial audiobook producers, and international-language books acquired through the Marrakesh Treaty.

What type of device do I need to use the app?

The BARD Mobile app for iOS can be used with any device that runs the three latest versions of the operating system.

How do I get the iOS app on my device?

From the device you wish to install BARD Mobile on, go to the App Store and enter the words “bard mobile” in the search text field. Once you find the app, click the Install button. The BARD Mobile app will install on your device.

Do I need to register my device on BARD?

No. The device will register itself the first time you log in to BARD Mobile using your BARD username and password.

I lost my device. What do I do?
  1. If you lose your device, the first thing you should do is change your BARD password using a web browser. This will prevent anyone using the mobile app on the lost phone from logging in and downloading additional books using your account. You will need to log in to BARD again from any other mobile device using your new password.
  2. Report your lost device to your library. Make sure to include your BARD login ID, the type of device it was, and the date you started using it (if you can remember). The library will use this information to delete the device from your account. You may also contact NLS BARD support at [email protected] if you are unable to reach your library.
I don’t know how to use my iOS device. Where can I get some help?

There are many resources available for using iOS devices. Here is a free resource from Hadley External to get you started. Another way to learn about your iOS device is to visit the Apple support website External. Using the search field, search for words such as voiceover, vision, zoom, display and text size, physical and motor, voice control, eye tracking, and hearing.

Why does downloading stop when I leave the app?

iOS limits what apps can do while in the background. BARD Mobile can continue to play an audiobook but cannot download.

How do I return to a long list of titles?

Where there are long lists of titles such as those in Recently Added to BARD and Most Popular books, there are More info buttons. If you select one of these buttons, a screen will appear displaying more information about the book. If you then press the Back button in the upper left corner of the screen, you will be returned to the long list. At this point, the Voiceover focus is on the “get books, back” button. If you flick right, go past the heading, and enter the list from the top of the screen, the display will jump to the top and you will be on the first item of the list. If instead you don’t flick but touch the very center of the screen, the focus will go to the point (or very near the point) from which you left the list.

I need help using VoiceOver. Where can I find it?

You can find several VoiceOver tutorials here.

Where can I get help using the BARD Mobile app?

The BARD Mobile user guide is available from the bookshelf in the app, under Help, and is also available on the NLS webpage www.loc.gov/nls/how-to-enroll/sign-up-for-bard-and-bard-mobile. Another resource is the series of NLS videos covering how to use BARD Mobile for iOS External. Contact your network library for additional assistance.

BARD Mobile for Android devices
Who can use the NLS BARD Mobile app?

The NLS BARD Mobile app is free to download from the Google Play Store, but only eligible NLS patrons can use it. You must have an established BARD account to download and read materials.

What books and magazines are available for download using the app?

NLS contracts with agencies around the country to produce books in audio and braille to be added to BARD. Additionally, NLS adds digital books submitted by network libraries, some commercial audiobook producers, and international-language books acquired through the Marrakesh Treaty.

What type of device do I need to use the app?

The BARD Mobile app for Android can be used with any device that runs the four latest versions of the operating system.

How do I get the Android app on my device?

From the device you wish to install BARD Mobile on, go to the Google Play Store and enter the words “bard mobile” in the search text field. Once you find the app, follow the prompts to install the app on your device.

Do I need to register my device on BARD?

No. The device will register itself the first time you log into BARD Mobile using your BARD username and password.

I lost my device. What do I do?
  1. If you lose your device, the first thing you should do is change your BARD password using a web browser. This will prevent anyone using the mobile app on the lost phone from logging in and downloading additional books using your account. You will need to log in to BARD again using your new password from any other mobile device.
  2. Report your lost device to your library. Make sure to include your BARD login ID, the type of device it was, and the date you started using it (if you can remember). The library will use this information to delete the device from your account. You may also contact NLS BARD support at [email protected] if you are unable to reach your library.
Where can I get help using the BARD Mobile app?

The BARD Mobile user guide is available from the bookshelf in the app, under Help, and is also available on the NLS website at www.loc.gov/nls/how-to-enroll/sign-up-for-bard-and-bard-mobile/bard-mobile-android. Another resource is the series of NLS videos covering how to use BARD Mobile. Access the videos on Android BARD Mobile External. Contact your local braille and talking-book library for additional assistance.

BARD Express
What is BARD Express?

BARD Express is free, downloadable Window-based software that simplifies searching for, downloading, transferring, and playing audio titles from BARD. BARD Express also simplifies searching for and downloading braille titles from BARD. To download BARD Express, visit the Welcome to BARD Express page.

Who can use BARD Express?

Any NLS patron can use BARD Express, though you must first have a BARD account with a username and password and a PC to load and use the software.

What types of books and magazines are available for download?

BARD Express enables users to download any audio and braille materials that are available on BARD.

What type of computer do I need to run BARD Express?

BARD Express is a Windows-based app that runs on PCs running Windows 10 and 11. BARD Express does not run on Apple computers.

Which screen readers work with BARD Express?

BARD Express works with NVDA and JAWS for Windows.

Where do I get BARD Express?

You can download BARD Express from the Welcome to BARD Express main page.

How do I access BARD with BARD Express?

Use your BARD email address and password to log in to BARD Express.

If you don’t have a BARD account, contact your network library. Once your application is processed by your network library, you will be emailed a temporary password. You must log in to BARD using a web browser and change your password before logging in to BARD Express.

Where can I get help with using BARD Express?

BARD Express comes with a comprehensive Help system. While running the program, press the F1 key to access the Help system.

A full BARD Express user guide is available here.

NLS has also produced a set of tutorial videos, called the BARD Express How-To Series, that describe how to use the functions and features of BARD Express. Select a link below to access the video of your choice.

Browsing the BARD Collection
Transferring Materials to a Cartridge External
Browsing the Recently Added and Most Popular Lists External
Searching the BARD Collection External
Using Your Wish List External
Setting and Changing Preferences External
Managing Devices External
Tips and Tricks External

Access the entire series here: BARD Express How-To Series External. Contact your NLS network library for additional assistance or support.

I just downloaded a title using BARD Express. Where did it go?

All materials you download using BARD Express will be stored on your BARD Express Bookshelf, which is accessed using the Bookshelf button from the main menu.

How do I move an audiobook from the computer to a cartridge for playback in my digital talking-book player?

After choosing a title from the BARD Express Bookshelf, use the Copy to Cartridge button to begin the process of moving that book or magazine to an external storage device connected to your computer, such as an NLS cartridge or USB flash drive. Highlight the drive to which you want to move the book or magazine and select the OK button. BARD Express will unzip the file that was downloaded from BARD and copy it to the storage device. Disconnect the device from your computer and enjoy your book!

How do I choose which book or magazine I want to play with the BARD Express player?

If you are using BARD Express in Standard mode, move to the title you wish to play and press F2 to launch the player. If you are in Advanced mode, choose the checkbox associated with the title you wish to play, then press F2 to launch the player.

Users of screen reading software must press Enter to activate any of the buttons in the BARD Express player. The exception to this is the space bar. This key activates the play/pause function, no matter where the focus is. In other words, even if the focus is on the Bookmark button or the Sleep Timer, pressing the space bar will play or stop the narration.

What book formats can be played with the BARD Express player?

Only audiobooks downloaded from BARD can be played using the BARD Express player. Braille books cannot be played with the BARD Express player.

Can I change the speed of the narration in BARD Express?

Yes. To adjust the speed, move to the Speed button, open it, move the slider up or down, then choose OK or press Enter. This can be done whether or not the narration is playing.

What functions are included with the BARD Express player?

The player allows you to fast forward and rewind through a book or magazine; jump between navigation points of the content such as parts, chapters, and articles; adjust the speed of the narration; and more. For a complete list of player functions and how to use them, check out Section 7 of the user guide. You can also press F1 from within the BARD Express player to get context-sensitive information while on any screen of the program.

When using screen reading software, are there keyboard commands that can be used with the BARD Express player?

While using JAWS for Windows or NVDA, you can tab and shift-tab through the player, but you can also tap single keys to quickly move to any of the controls, such as F to fast forward, R to rewind, B for bookmarks, and more. For a complete list of commands, consult Section 7 of the user guide. You can also find a list of keyboard commands from within the Help menu of the main BARD Express menu.

NLS eReader
What is the NLS eReader?

An NLS eReader is a 20-cell braille display that connects to BARD through a wireless Internet connection so that a patron can search for and download braille books.

If I don’t have a wireless connection in my house, can I still use an NLS eReader?

If you do not have a wireless Internet connection, digital braille books and magazines can be delivered through the mail on a cartridge. When the cartridge is connected to the eReader, the books and magazines will automatically be copied to the eReader.

What kind of features does an NLS eReader offer?

There are two versions of the NLS eReader. However, they both offer the same features. The one that you receive is determined by the state where you reside.

  • The core feature of an eReader is to read electronic braille books and magazines from NLS.

  • The eReader has a Book List that shows all of the titles currently stored on it.

  • Connect a mobile device such as a smart phone or tablet to your eReader via Bluetooth.

  • Toggle the eReader’s input mode between computer braille, grade 1 UEB, or grade 2 UEB.

  • Connect the eReader to a computer to read the computer’s output.

What kind of storage comes with the eReader?

The eReader has internal storage that will keep all of the books. In addition, the eReader has a port for a USB thumb drive and a slot for an SD card.

How can I get an NLS eReader?

To receive an NLS eReader, contact your NLS network library.

The My Talking Books smart speaker skill
What is My Talking Books?

My Talking Books is the NLS skill (or app) for Alexa-enabled smart speakers that lets patrons stream English-language audiobooks from BARD. Using the voice-only interface, you can search the BARD catalog, manage your wish lists, book lists, bookshelves, bookmarks, preference settings, sleep timer, reading speed, jumping by time, and book-specific volume setting, as well as find book descriptions and review your streaming history.

How do I sign up for My Talking Books?

Interested NLS patrons can register for an open beta test of the My Talking Books skill using the My Talking Books – Open Beta Registration Form External. Patrons who are minors are required to have a parent or legal guardian fill out the My Talking Books – Parental Acknowledge Form External.

Once logged in, you must use the skill at least once every 30 days to maintain access.

Where can I get a list of voice commands?

The My Talking Books home page has a number of resources that can be used to learn how to use the skill, including a list of voice commands.

Where can I get the My Talking Books user guide?

The user guide can be accessed directly from the smart speaker. While logged into the My Talking Books skill, say “User guide.” You will be asked what topic you wish to learn about. After listening to the choices, speak the name of the topic you want to learn more about.

The user guide is also available online. Scroll through the guide on your computer so you can simultaneously try various functions of the My Talking Books skill.

If I have multiple NLS accounts associated with a phone number and I accidentally choose the wrong account, what can I do?

To sign in to a different account, first open My Talking Books on your device. Then say “leave account.” Ask for the My Talking Books skill again. You will be asked to provide your phone number and then select your preferred NLS account.

My partner is the Alexa account holder. When I log in to My Talking Books it addresses them and not me. How can I change this?

My Talking Books uses the first name given on the Alexa profile that was being used when you initially gave permission for Alexa to share your first name and email to use My Talking Books. You can close the My Talking Books account linked to that Alexa profile by saying “leave account”after opening My Talking Books.

You will then need to set up your own Alexa profile. Alexa allows households to have multiple profiles linked to a single speaker. To create a unique profile, you will need to have your own Amazon account and Alexa app.

How do I give Alexa permission to use My Talking Books?

There are 2 options:

Your first option is to grant permission through the Alexa app.

  • If you have received the email saying your access to the skill has been granted, say “Alexa, open My Talking Books.” You will be asked to give your name. Your smart speaker will instruct you to open the Amazon Alexa app on your device.
  • Open the My Talking Books skill, which should be near the top of the opening screen. Check the two boxes giving Alexa permission to remember your name and email address.
  • Say the command “Alexa, open My Talking Books.” Your smart speaker will ask you for your ten-digit phone number.
  • After giving the phone number, you will be asked how you would like to receive an authentication phrase/numerical code: via text message, a phone call, or email.
  • After receiving the four-character phrase or numerical code, say the command “Alexa, open My Talking Books,” then speak either the phrase or numerical code you were given when the system asks for it.

Your second option is to grant Alexa permissions through the Amazon website.

  • Sign in to your Amazon account External.
  • If you have activated the link above, navigated the page, and selected the “Enable” button, then you are already signed in and can skip this step.
  • If you did not previously sign in to your Amazon account with your browser, or your browser did not remember your credentials, you will find a “Sign In” button instead. You will need to select this button and sign in to your Amazon account.
  • To sign in to your Amazon account, you will need at least the email address or phone number associated with the account along with the password. You may also have to verify the account; if so, Amazon will send you a code via text message or email.
  • Complete the form fields with all the required information until you get the page that says “Amazon.com: My Talking Books: Alexa Skills.”
  • Once signed in, find and activate the button that says “Enable.”
  • Once you enable the skill, the next page will allow you to manage permissions. You will need to locate the checkboxes for “Email Address” and “First Name.” Check both boxes with the space bar, and then activate the “save permissions” button. At this point, you are finished with the web page. Your smart speaker is required for the next step.
  • Say the command “Alexa, open My Talking Books.” Your smart speaker will ask for your ten-digit phone number. Clearly speak your phone number into the speaker.
  • After giving your phone number, you will be asked how you would like to receive an authentication phrase/numerical code: via text message, a phone call, or email.
  • After receiving the four-character phrase or numerical code, say the command “Alexa, open My Talking Books,” then speak either the phrase or numerical code you were given. You should then be authenticated and successfully logged in to the skill.
What does “audio not yet available” mean?

The message “audio not yet available” is given when a book is still being processed to be streamed. The book description and annotations can still be heard, and you can add the book to your BARD Wishlist or Bookshelves to be played at a later date.

Can I read my BARD magazines with the My Talking Books skill?

Magazines are currently not available on this version of My Talking Books.

Which Amazon devices have been tested with the My Talking Books skill?

There are many devices on the market that are Alexa-enabled. However, only the following devices have been verified to work with My Talking Book:

  • Amazon Echo (2nd Generation and above)
  • Echo Dot (2nd Generation and above)
  • Echo Show 5 (2^(nd) Generation and above)
  • Echo Flex
  • Alexa
  • Alexa App on Apple iOS

Account management
How do I get a BARD account?

Contact your NLS network library to request a BARD account. Once your librarian creates the account, you will receive an email welcoming you to BARD. That email will contain a link to create a BARD password. Select the link, and a browser will open to a page where you can create your BARD password.

What do I do if I need to reset my password?

If you ever forget your BARD password, there are three options:

Option 1 for changing a password:
  1. Open the BARD login page.
  2. Select Forgot Your BARD Password?
  3. Enter your email address and select the Submit button. You will receive an email with a link to reset your password.
  4. Select the link in the email to open a webpage where you can create a new password. Be sure to follow the password rules:
  5. Your password must contain between 8 and 32 characters.
  6. It cannot contain three or more of the same characters consecutively.
Option 2 for changing a password:

Contact your NLS network library and tell them you’d like assistance resetting your BARD password.

Option 3 for changing a password:

Email [email protected] to request assistance resetting your BARD password.

How do I change my password when I’m already logged into my BARD account?

While logged into the BARD website, expand the My BARD menu. Select Account Settings, then Change Your Password. On the resulting page, enter your email, then select the Submit button to have BARD send you an email that will contain a link to reset your password. When you select that link, your browser will open to a page where you can create a new password. Be sure to follow the password rules:

  • Your password must contain between 8 and 32 characters.
  • It cannot contain three or more of the same characters consecutively.

Once your new password is accepted, you will receive an email confirming that you have changed your password.

What do I do if I am locked out of my account after multiple attempts to log in?

Getting locked out of your BARD account is a result of too many failed attempts to log into the service. You have three options:

  1. Wait 15 minutes. BARD will unlock your account in 15 minutes, after which you may try logging in again.
  2. Call your NLS network library and ask to have your BARD account unlocked.
  3. Change your BARD password. If you have not been able to successfully log into BARD after 10 tries, there is a good chance you may have forgotten your password.
How can I change the email address associated with my BARD account?

Contact your NLS network library to change the email address on your BARD account.

Books, musical scores, magazines and other materials
How many books and magazines are available for download?

More than a hundred thousand titles are available on BARD. The number increases nearly every day.

Where do the books come from?

NLS produces some audiobooks in-house and contracts with vendors around the country to produce other books in audio and braille specifically for its collection. BARD also contains digital books produced by network libraries, audiobooks acquired from commercial producers and international language books acquired through the Marrakesh Treaty.

When are books added?

New books are added to BARD almost every day. If you are looking for recently published materials, be aware that there may be a delay before they become available on BARD. Even audiobooks acquired directly from commercial producers need to be formatted to work with NLS systems, and NLS conducts rigorous quality checks on the materials it produces.

In what languages are books available on BARD?

Books are available on BARD in the following languages:

  • Afrikaans
  • Albanian
  • Algonquian languages
  • Amharic
  • Arabic
  • Armenian
  • Basque
  • Bengali
  • Bulgarian
  • Central Khmer
  • Cherokee
  • Chinese
  • Church Slavic
  • Croatian
  • Czech
  • Danish
  • Dutch
  • English
  • Finnish
  • French
  • German
  • Greek
  • Greek, Ancient
  • Gujarati
  • Hebrew
  • Hindi
  • Hmong
  • Hungarian
  • Italian
  • Japanese
  • Korean
  • Latvian
  • Lithuanian
  • Navajo
  • Nepali
  • Norwegian
  • Ojibwa
  • Panjabi
  • Persian
  • Polish
  • Portuguese
  • Romanian
  • Russian
  • Serbo-Croatian
  • Slovak
  • Slovenian
  • Somali
  • Spanish
  • Swahili
  • Tagalog
  • Telugu
  • Tigrinya
  • Turkish
  • Ukrainian
  • Urdu
  • Vietnamese
  • Welsh
  • Yiddish
  • Yoruba
Why are some books from a series missing? Why are some magazine issues missing?

While NLS tries to offer complete book series, some books in a series may not be available in digital form. NLS continues to work toward acquiring the missing titles in these incomplete book series. Please contact your NLS network library if you think you have discovered a title missing from a series, a duplication or some other inconsistency. The information will be forwarded to appropriate NLS staff for action.

NLS-produced magazine issues must meet our strict quality-control standards. In some cases, individual issues can be held up by production problems. NLS works with magazine producers to solve these problems as quickly as possible.

What do I do if I find a problem in a book or magazine?

Please report the problem — including the title, author, book prefix and book number — to [email protected]. Your player can tell you the time elapsed as of your current reading position. If possible, please include this information in your email. Listing the book number and its prefix in your email’s subject line is also helpful.

What is available here that I cannot get from my network library?

BARD includes materials produced by commercial audiobook publishers and other network libraries that might not be available at your library. Special collection materials, such as music and international language books, also may not be available at your network library.

If you prefer to receive network-produced and international language books through your network library rather than by directly downloading them from BARD, your NLS network library might be able to obtain copies via interlibrary loan. Contact your NLS network library for more information.

Contact the NLS Music Section either via phone, 800-424-8567 ext. 2, or via email, [email protected], to request specially formatted music materials in formats other than digital download.

What is not available here that I can get from my NLS network library?

Your NLS network library has access to the entire NLS collection, which is more extensive than the downloadable collection on BARD. The absence of a title on BARD does not mean the item is not available at all. You should check your library’s catalog or the NLS Catalog.

Can I request a title to be added to BARD?

You are welcome to request books or magazines to be added to the collection. The Collection Development Section will review your recommendations and consider the titles that meet the NLS Collection Building Policy. Only books that were published within the past five years will be considered. Visit the NLS Collection Building Policy for more information. Use the link for the Book Recommendation Form to make your request. Contact your network library if you encounter issues using this form.

Can I check to see if a particular title is going to be added to the collection?

If you cannot find a book on BARD, you can search the NLS Catalog to see if it is listed as IN PROCESS. This means NLS has selected it for the collection, but it is still in production. It will appear on BARD when production and quality checks have been completed.

How long are magazine issues kept?

At this time, NLS plans to keep magazine issues available indefinitely.

How do I find a book to read?

Enter a search term in the Search Books field and then select the Go button. BARD will search for the term in the author, narrator, subject, annotation and book number fields. Note that putting your search term in quotes (” “) will limit the search to that exact phrase. Also note that authors’ names are listed last name, comma, first name. Narrators’ names are listed first name then last name.

  • To search by author, type the author’s name in quotes, such as “Smith, John.”
  • To search by narrator, type the narrator’s name in quotes. For example, “Michelle Reader.”
  • To search by title, type the title or partial title into the search box. Narrow your search by typing your search criteria in quotes to find books with that exact search criteria. For example, “After the Firm.” Remember that this is a keyword search, so it is possible that your search criteria could display in the annotation, not necessarily in the title.
  • To search by annotation, type one or more keywords into the search box. Narrow your search by typing your search criteria in quotes to find books with that exact search criteria. For example, “After the Firm.” Remember that this is a keyword search, so it is possible that your search criteria could display in the title, not necessarily in the annotation.
  • To search by book number, type the number into the search box. (Note that some materials have the same item number but a different prefix, as they are available in multiple formats from different producers. Adding the prefix will limit the results.) Because this is a keyword search, remember your search criteria could appear in the annotation, not necessarily in the book number field.
  • To search by language, type your search term in the search box and select the language from the language drop-down list.
What is the quickest way to find a specific book?

Choosing the “Advanced Search for Books” link on the BARD main page will take you to an advanced search page where you can tell BARD exactly what you are looking for. You can search by title, book number, author, narrator, publisher, format, language and subject. The more search criteria used, the more accurate your search will be.

Reading books
I just downloaded a book to my computer, but now I can’t find it. Where did it go?

Each browser handles downloads somewhat differently. Unless you directed the browser to save your downloaded files elsewhere, books will be downloaded to your Downloads folder by default.

Can I put more than one audiobook on a cartridge?

This depends on how your player handles multiple books.

If you are using a commercial player, please see its manual for the answer to this question.

If you are using an NLS digital player, multiple books can be loaded onto a cartridge. Each book can then be accessed by using the machine’s Sequential Play feature. To use this feature, press the Play/Stop button to go to the next title when the player says “End of book.” The player also has a Bookshelf feature that lists all of the books on the cartridge. To enter bookshelf mode on your NLS player, hold down the Play button until you hear the player announce “Bookshelf” and the number of books on the bookshelf. Release the Play button, then tap the fast forward or rewind buttons to move to the next or previous book in the list. When you hear the title you wish to read, simply press the Play button.

In addition to the Bookshelf navigation just described, the DA2 player has a dedicated Bookshelf button. Pressing that button will place focus directly in the list of books on the Bookshelf.

If you have further questions about using your NLS player to play multiple books, contact the NLS network library that loaned you the player. All files must be in the proper format (digital audio) to be read with the NLS players. Note that the players will not recognize braille titles.

How do I know if the audiobook I want will fit on my cartridge?

All NLS platforms list the reading time of books. Reading time translates to file size at a rate of approximately 11 megabytes per hour. A one gigabyte cartridge will hold almost 96 hours, and a two-gigabyte cartridge will hold about 192 hours.

How do I move an audiobook to my player?

Unzip the file you downloaded to your computer, then copy the unzipped files to a thumb drive or NLS cartridge. There are several popular software packages that handle this function. However, it is also built into all versions of Windows and Mac OS.

When using a Windows-based computer, locate the unzipped books and copy them to the clipboard. Navigate to your thumb drive or cartridge and paste the folder. Then insert the thumb drive or cartridge into your player. On a Mac, use the Command key.

If using an NLS DA2 player, the player will ask if you would like to copy your books and magazines to the internal storage when an NLS cartridge is inserted into the cartridge slot. Press the Info key to answer Yes, and the titles will be copied to the player’s internal storage.

Certain commercial players require NLS books and magazines to be in sub-directories for them to play. Consult the manual for your commercial player.

My commercial player says I am not authorized to read this book. What does that mean?

This can be caused by three things:

  • If you purchased a commercial player, you may not have completed the steps needed to enable your player to access NLS materials. These players must be registered to your BARD account.
  • Your digital talking book or magazine may not have completely transferred to your cartridge or USB drive. This is usually the result of a copy operation that failed or an incomplete download from BARD.
  • The talking book may not have been transferred into the necessary directory on your commercial player’s memory card. Please refer to your player’s user manual for information on where to place books on the memory card.
Can I listen to downloaded audiobooks on my computer?

Yes, if you have a Windows-based computer. NLS offers a software application called BARD Express that includes a player that enables you to listen to books. Visit the BARD Express main page to learn more about the program.

How do I read braille books downloaded from BARD?

NLS patrons are eligible to receive a twenty-cell NLS Braille eReader that connects to BARD through a wireless Internet connection. If you do not have access to a wireless Internet connection, braille books and magazines can. be delivered to you through the mail on a cartridge. When the cartridge is connected to the Braille eReader, the books and magazines are automatically copied over. To receive an NLS Braille eReader, contact your NLS network library.

There are also a variety of commercial devices that can be used to read braille titles downloaded from BARD. These titles can be read online or downloaded for offline reading or embossing with a braille display, braille-aware notetaker or braille embosser. Zipped files must always be unzipped before being read.

How do I subscribe to the BARD support mailing list?

NLS patrons can subscribe to an email-based list called BARD-Support. This is a closed, one-way list to which NLS posts information of interest to the BARD community. Replies to BARD-Support messages will not reach us. If you have questions, email [email protected].

If you would like to subscribe, access the Library of Congress subscription page. Enter your email address and select the Submit button. This will bring you to a confirmation page where you’ll be required to confirm your email address and choose your delivery preferences.

To unsubscribe, access the subscription page, enter your email address and select the Submit button. On the resulting page, consent to the Privacy Policy and select the Submit button. Check the list you want to unsubscribe from and select the Submit button.

Last updated April 8, 2026
