Section 1: Getting Started with BARD Mobile

Welcome to the Braille and Audio Reading Download (BARD) Mobile app User Guide for devices using the Android operating system. This app allows BARD users to browse, download, manage, and read braille and audio content using an Android phone or tablet.

1.1: Different Ways to Get the BARD Mobile User Guide

There are several ways to access this User Guide:

  1. Within the BARD Mobile app
  2. On the web
  3. From an electronic braille file
1.1.1: Accessing the User Guide within the BARD Mobile App

To read the User Guide conveniently located within the BARD Mobile app:

  1. Locate and select the Bookshelf tab.
  2. Activate User Guide to open it.
1.1.2: Accessing the User Guide on the Web

To access the BARD Mobile User Guide on the web:

  1. From a device with Internet access, open a web browser such as Microsoft Edge, Google Chrome, Safari, etc.
  2. Open the web page for the BARD Mobile Android User Guide.
  3. Consider saving or bookmarking the User Guide for future reference.
1.1.3: Accessing the User Guide from an Electronic Braille File

To download the BARD Mobile User Guide in braille:

  1. From a device with Internet access, open a web browser such as Microsoft Edge, Google Chrome, Safari, etc.
  2. Open the web page Sign up for BARD and Accessing BARD.
  3. Find and activate the link for BARD Mobile User Manual for Android in electronic braille [BRF].
1.2: Making the Most of the BARD Mobile User Guide

Consider the following to help navigate the BARD Mobile User Guide more efficiently:

1.2.1: Settings App vs Settings Tab

Throughout this User Guide, you will be asked to make changes in both the Settings app and the Settings tab. The Settings app is a separate app that is part of your Android device. Although the Settings app has many options, this User Guide will only discuss options that affect the BARD Mobile App. The Settings tab is part of the BARD Mobile app and has options specific to BARD Mobile.

1.2.2: Check the Settings Tab if the App Doesn’t Match the User Guide

While navigating the BARD Mobile app, if some results do not match all the information from the User Guide, you may have made changes in the Settings tab. The User Guide generally documents information with default settings. It is recommended to check the Settings tab or read Section 5: Settings.

1.2.3: Read Notes for TalkBack Users

Throughout this User Guide, there are notes for TalkBack users providing additional information about TalkBack in relation to that portion of the guide. To help you locate these notes, they are inside the * (asterisk) symbols. A general note for TalkBack users is below:

*Note for TalkBack users: This User Guide refers to the use of TalkBack in general. The availability of features, gestures, and commands may vary because of updates and individual devices. Read documentation for your specific Android version and device for more information.*

1.3: Access to BARD

Use of BARD Mobile is restricted to eligible NLS patrons who have a BARD account. Individuals and institutions who are currently not a patron of NLS should read about the NLS eligibility requirements and learn about signing up for NLS services. Eligible NLS patrons with a BARD account can use the BARD Mobile for Android app. For those who do not have a BARD account, contact your network library.

BARD Mobile operates on an Android phone or tablet running Android OS 12 through 15. To download materials, connection to Wi-Fi or cellular data is necessary.

1.4: Creating a Password for the First Time Using the BARD Website

New BARD members must create a password following these instructions:

  1. During the process of signing up for BARD, an active email address is required. Be sure to provide the email address correctly because an email containing log-in information and other valuable resources will be sent to that address.
  2. Use the link provided in the email to go to the BARD website. When this link is activated, the default browser will open and the page for creating a password loads.
  3. After reading the rules listed on the web site to create a new password, type the password in the first input field, then confirm it by typing the password a second time in the password confirmation field.
  4. Activate the Submit button to log in to the BARD website with the newly created password.
  5. Read and agree to the reading pledge. If successful, a page with the title BARD Main Page will open. Be sure to note the email address along with the newly created password since this will be needed to sign in to BARD Mobile.
1.5: Operating BARD Mobile with TalkBack and Magnification

BARD Mobile is accessible to blind and visually impaired patrons when used in conjunction with various features found in Accessibility Settings. Two of these include the TalkBack screen reader and Magnification, two built in features of the Android OS.

1.5.1: Operating BARD Mobile with the TalkBack Screen Reader

TalkBack provides speech and braille feedback for blind or visually impaired users of Android phones and tablets. While the primary navigation method for using an Android device is with gestures, there are notable differences when using gestures with and without TalkBack.

When using TalkBack with gestures, a one-finger single tap does not activate the item; it places focus on that item. This is because a blind or visually impaired user needs to know the location of the item before activating it. When a TalkBack user is ready to activate an item, the gesture is a one-finger double tap. Throughout this User Guide, there will be instructions to activate various items. This means that those who are not using TalkBack will perform a one-finger single tap on the item while TalkBack users will need to first find the item, and then perform a one-finger double tap.

In addition to locating an item with a one-finger single tap to place focus on a specific item, TalkBack users can then navigate to the previous item with a one-finger flick left, or next item with a one-finger flick right anywhere on the screen. This means if you performed a one-finger single tap near the desired item but not on the exact location, you can perform another one-finger single tap until the item is located. However, if you already know that screen and understand the desired item is the next one, then a one-finger flick right is also an option.

Beginner TalkBack users will need to pay close attention to the TalkBack usage hints. These are on by default and are provided after the name of the item.

Although the TalkBack screen reader provides many useful options and tools, beginner users will need to spend time to read and learn this feature. While this User Guide will provide notes and tips for TalkBack specifically with the BARD Mobile app, it is recommended to use the below links along with additional links within these web pages to help improve efficiency when using TalkBack in general:

To activate TalkBack on an Android device, go to Turn on TalkBack External. To learn more about using the TalkBack screen reader on an Android device, go to: Use TalkBack gestures External. To learn about what’s new with TalkBack for the various versions, go to What’s new External.

For short video demonstrations of using TalkBack with the BARD Mobile app, go to: BARD Mobile for Android How-To Series External. While the previous three links go to Android accessibility help from Google, this is a YouTube channel from NLS.

The above links talk about the use of TalkBack in general. The availability of features, gestures, and commands may vary because of updates and individual devices. Read documentation for your specific Android version and device for more information.

1.5.2: Operating BARD Mobile with Magnification

Magnification benefits many low-vision users by providing the ability to increase the screen size.

To learn more about using Magnification in general, go to: Use magnification on your screen External. Generally, the Magnification feature can be found in the device settings, then accessibility; however, results may vary because of updates and individual devices. Read documentation for your specific Android version and device for more information.

1.6: BARD Mobile Installation and Login

Use the following instructions to install and log in to BARD Mobile for the first time:

  1. To install BARD Mobile, open the Play Store on your Android device.
  2. Locate the Search tab on the bottom of the device and if it is not already selected, activate it to select the Search tab.
  3. Locate and activate the search field near the top of the screen and search for BARD Mobile.
  4. Find and activate BARD Mobile in the results, and then activate the install button.
  5. When the download is complete, press the Open button. This opens the BARD Mobile app and the BARD login screen will appear.
  6. Enter the email address associated with your BARD account in the User Name text field.
  7. Enter the password you previously created on the BARD website in the password text field.
  8. Activate the Accept terms of service checkbox to agree. This will open the end user license agreement.
  9. Once the document is reviewed, activate the Close button. You will go back to the BARD login screen and the Accept terms of service checkbox is checked.
  10. Find and activate the Login button.
  11. When successfully logged in, focus will be on the Get Books screen. It is now time to download the first book or magazine!
Section 2: An Overview of BARD Mobile

This section provides an overview of BARD Mobile to help you get a general understanding of the app.

2.1: Orientation of the BARD Mobile App

When the BARD Mobile app is opened, the screen is divided into five areas. The areas from top to bottom are:

  1. The Status Bar at the top (This is an Android feature and device specific)
  2. The Action Bar (Also known as the App Bar)
  3. The tab bar with four tabs allowing you to choose a tab and confirm the currently selected tab
  4. The content of the currently selected tab (This takes up most of the screen)
  5. The Navigation Bar on the bottom (This is an Android feature and device specific)
2.2: The Status Bar

The status bar, located at the top of the screen, provides information about the Android device such as time, Wi-Fi connection, cellular status, battery status, etc. Note that different Android devices may include different items on the status bar. Also, while some Android devices will call this the status bar, others may give it another name.

The Status bar is not part of the BARD Mobile interface. It is a feature of the Android device. Since it appears at the very top of the screen when BARD Mobile is opened, this is mentioned here to provide background information and orientation.

2.3: The Action Bar

The Action bar is below the Status bar and contains the following items from left to right:

  1. The Navigate up button (appears visually as a left arrow)
  2. The title of the current screen
  3. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots)
2.3.1: The Navigate up button

The Navigate up button will not appear on the first screen for each tab. If you choose something from the content area that opens another screen, the Navigate up button will be available on the left side of the Action bar to allow you to navigate back to the previous screen.

2.3.2: The Title

Located in the middle of the action bar, the title informs you the name of the current screen. For example, if you select bookshelf, the title will show “Bookshelf”. In addition, if you then select the Audio Books folder from the Bookshelf screen, then the title will show, “Audio Books Folder.”

2.3.3: The More Options Button

The More Options button, on the right side of the Action bar, allows you to get help for the current screen. It may also have additional options for specific screens. For example, when in the Audio Books folder, you will also get options like Create folder, Rename folder, Delete, and Move.

2.4: The Tab Bar

The tab bar is located below the Action bar and contains the four tabs of the BARD Mobile app. The tabs from left to right are:

  1. Bookshelf
  2. Get Books
  3. Settings
  4. Now Reading

Use the tab bar to choose a different tab to display on the main content area or to find out the tab that is currently selected.

*Note for TalkBack users: While using speech, when navigating through the tab bar, TalkBack will indicate the currently selected tab by saying the word selected in front of the tab that is chosen. For example, “Selected, Bookshelf tab.” When using a braille device with TalkBack, the letters “sel” will be shown after the name of the tab that is selected. For example, “Bookshelf sel.”*

2.5: The Content of the Currently Selected Tab

Below the tab bar is the content of the currently selected tab. This takes up most of the screen. The available information depends on which of the four tabs on the tab bar, located above the content, is selected.

2.5.1: Bookshelf

Bookshelf is the first of four tabs on the tab bar. It is used to view and manage downloaded content. All downloaded books, along with this User Guide, can be found within the bookshelf. Titles are divided within the following:

  1. Audio Books
  2. Audio Magazines
  3. Braille Books
  4. Braille Magazines
  5. User Guide (Opens this User Guide)

To read more about Bookshelf and how to use it, go to Section 3: Bookshelf.

2.5.2: Get Books

Get Books is the second of four tabs on the tab bar. It is used to search, browse, manage, and download content on BARD. Available options from Get Books include:

  1. Search
  2. Wish List
  3. Recently Added to BARD
  4. Most Popular Books
  5. Browse Magazines
  6. Previous Downloads
  7. Search Music

To read more about Get Books and how to work with it, go to Section 4: Get Books.

2.5.3: Settings

Settings is the third of four tabs on the tab bar. It is used to view and customize various settings for the BARD Mobile app. The main screen of Settings includes the following:

  1. Audio settings
  2. Braille and Display settings
  3. User Account settings
  4. Device Accessibility settings

To read more about Settings, go to Section 5: Settings.

2.5.4: Now Reading

Now Reading is the last of four tabs on the tab bar. If the Now Reading tab is selected without an opened title, you will get the following message:

“No book is currently selected to play. Please select a book from the Bookshelf to play.”

To open a title, locate a book or magazine from the Bookshelf, or locate and download a title from Get Books, then activate the Read button, automatically moving you to the Now Reading tab. When a title is opened from Bookshelf or Get books, the Now Reading tab is automatically selected and focus is on the main content area of the Now Reading tab. Once a book is opened, you can select another tab from the tab bar and can also use the tab bar to select and return to the opened title within the Now Reading tab.

When the Now Reading tab is selected, and a book is opened, the content area below the tab bar serves as a player for the currently opened book. Some controls are only available when audio content is opened while others are only for braille content.

Some available controls when audio content is opened include Sleep timer, Jump by chapter, Rewind, Play/stop, Fast forward, and Adjust speed. To read more about working with the Now Reading screen when audio content is opened, go to Section 6: Reading Audio Titles.

Some available controls when braille content is opened include the actual braille book, jump by volume, jump by page, and jump by line. To read more about working with the Now Reading screen when braille content is opened, go to Section 7: Reading Braille Titles.

2.6: The Navigation Bar

The Navigation Bar, located at the bottom of the screen, provides device-specific navigation tools such as Recents button, Back button, Home button, and Overview button. Note that different Android devices may include different items on the Navigation bar. Some devices may not include the navigation bar, requiring you to use gestures to accomplish tasks like going back to the previous screen, returning to the home screen, etc.

The Navigation bar is not part of the BARD Mobile interface. It is a feature of the Android device. Since it can appear at the very bottom of the screen when BARD Mobile is opened, this is mentioned here to provide background information and orientation.

Section 3: Bookshelf

The Bookshelf is used to view and manage downloaded content. All downloaded audio and Braille Books and Magazines, along with this User Guide, can be found within the Bookshelf.

3.1: Accessing the Bookshelf

To access the Bookshelf, do the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the top of the screen.
  2. Find Bookshelf, which is the first of four tabs and is located on the left.
  3. If Bookshelf is already selected, the Bookshelf screen is already shown below the tab bar.
  4. If it is not selected, activate the Bookshelf tab to select it. The Bookshelf screen will appear on the content area below the tab bar.
3.2: Bookshelf Quick Start

This Section provides quick instructions on how to read and delete titles. For more detailed orientation and information about the various screens of the Bookshelf tab, start reading from Section 3.3: The Bookshelf Screen.

3.2.1: Reading Titles

To locate a title from the Bookshelf and start reading it, do the following:

  1. While on the Bookshelf screen, choose one of the first four categories (Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines).
  2. When the screen opens, navigate to a title of interest, and activate the title name. Once the title is chosen, you are automatically moved to the Now Reading tab, away from the Bookshelf, with player controls allowing you to read the title.
3.2.2: Deleting Titles

To delete a title from the Bookshelf, do the following:

  1. While in the Bookshelf screen, choose one of the first four categories (Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines).
  2. When the screen opens, activate the More options button on the top right.
  3. From the pop-up window that appears, activate delete.
  4. A list of titles with checkboxes will appear. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title, author’s last name or recently read.
  5. Choose a title or folder to check or uncheck for deletion.
  6. Activate the ok button to delete items that are checked.
  7. You will be asked if you are sure you want to delete, and if a folder is selected, all books in the folder will be deleted. Choose yes to delete.
3.3: The Bookshelf Screen

The Bookshelf screen shows these items in the following order:

  1. Action bar on the top which has the following two items:
    1. The title that reads “Bookshelf” in the middle
    2. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots) on the right side, which allows you to get help for the current screen.
  2. The tab bar, below the Action bar, shows the four tabs with Bookshelf selected.
  3. Audio Books
  4. Audio Magazines
  5. Braille Books
  6. Braille Magazines
  7. User Guide (opens this User Guide)

*Note for TalkBack users: When navigating to different categories, TalkBack will first indicate the name of the category followed by the number of items within that category. For example: “Audio Books 11”.*

Figure 1: Bookshelf categories

3.4: The Current Category Screen

This screen appears if you select one of the first four categories from the Bookshelf screen (Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines). While the results will be different depending on the chosen category, the screen is very similar. Therefore, this orientation for the Current Category screen applies to all four categories.

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button (appears visually as a left arrow), on the left side, Goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle with the name of the current folder such as “Audio Books”
    3. The More options button on the right side, lets you get access to additional choices for the current title.
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Bookshelf selected.
  3. The Search bar (used to search titles within the current category)
  4. Sorting buttons (by Title, Author, or Latest)
  5. The list of titles (either all items for the current category or results matching the search)
  6. The List of folders you’ve created

Figure 2: The Audio Books Folder

3.4.1: Search by Title and Author

Below the tab bar is the search field. This is used to search the chosen category by either title or author. To search within the current list, do the following:

  1. Activate the search field. An onscreen keyboard should appear on the bottom half of the display. An exception to this is if an external keyboard or braille device is connected, since you are expected to use that instead.
  2. Start typing either the title or the author’s last name. As information is typed, the list of titles below the search and sorting options will change to match the search.
  3. When the search field is activated, there is a cancel button to the right of the search field.

Figure 3: Search Audio Books Folder by author

3.4.2: Sort the List

Below the search field are three buttons allowing you to sort titles. The three sorting options are:

  1. Title (alphabetical order by title, selected by default)
  2. Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)
  3. Latest (sorted by date when you accessed the titles, with most recent on top)

*Note for TalkBack users: When navigating to one of these three buttons, TalkBack will indicate the currently chosen option by either saying the word selected before the name of the button with speech, or with the letters “sel” after the name of the button with braille.*

3.4.3: The List of Titles

Below the three sorting buttons is the list of downloaded titles for the chosen category. By default, this list contains all titles from the currently chosen category. However, if you perform a search by title and author using the search field above the three sorting buttons, then the list will only contain the matching results. In addition, the order of the titles on this list can be sorted with the three buttons above the list itself.

While in the list of titles, each title may include the following information:

  1. Title name
  2. Author or authors
  3. Total reading time (audio only)
  4. Number of volumes (braille only)
  5. More Info button

Activating the More Info button will open another screen allowing you to select additional options for that title.

3.4.4: The List of Folders

If you’ve previously created folders within the current category, they will appear below the list of titles. Activating one of these folders will open another screen with the folder content. This will be similar to the screen that shows titles for a specific category. This means you can search, sort, download, read, and work with the list of titles.

3.5: More Options for the Current Category

When you choose one of the four categories from the bookshelf screen, activating the More options button on the right side of the Action bar will open a pop-up window with the following choices:

  1. Create Folder
  2. Rename Folder
  3. Delete
  4. Move
  5. Help (get help for the current screen)

Figure 4: More options

3.5.1: Create Folder

You can create folders to help you organize your titles within each of the four categories. For example, you may want to have a mystery folder and a science fiction folder within Audio Books or Braille Books.

You can create a folder by doing the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the top left of the screen.
  2. From the Bookshelf screen, Open the category where you want to create the folder.
  3. Once you are in the location where you want to create the folder, activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate Create Folder.
  5. Activate the edit field to bring up the onscreen keyboard.

Figure 5: Create a Folder

Type the name of the folder you want and activate ok to create the folder.

3.5.2: Rename Folder

To change the name of a folder you previously created, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the top left of the screen.
  2. Open the category that has the folder you want to rename.
  3. Once you are in the screen with that folder, activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate Rename Folder.
  5. Check the box for the folder you want to rename and activate ok.
    Figure 6: Choose folder to rename
  6. Activate the edit field to bring up the onscreen keyboard.
    Figure 7: Rename Folder
  7. Type the new name you want and activate ok to rename the folder.
3.5.3: Delete Titles and Folders

To delete previously downloaded titles or folders you’ve created, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the top left of the screen.
  2. Open the category that has the titles or folders you want to delete.
  3. Once you are in the correct location, Activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate delete. A list of titles with checkboxes will appear. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title or author’s last name or by recently read.
    Figure 8: Choose titles or folders to delete
  5. Choose a title or folder to check or uncheck for deletion.
  6. Activate the ok button to delete items that are checked. You will be asked if you are sure you want to delete, and if a folder is selected, all books in the folder will be deleted. Activate yes to delete.
3.5.4: Move Titles and Folders

To move previously downloaded titles or folders you’ve created to another location, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the top left of the screen.
  2. From the Bookshelf screen , Open the category that has the titles or folders you want to move.
  3. Once you are in the correct location, Activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate move. A list of titles with checkboxes will appear. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title or author’s last name or by recently read.
    Figure 9: Move items checkboxes
  5. Check titles or folders that you want to move.
  6. Activate the ok button.
  7. Choose a location where you want the checked items to go.
    1. If you want them to go to the main level of Audio Books, Braille Books, Audio Magazines, or Braille Magazines, activate one of these choices.
    2. If you want the checked items to go into another folder, first expand the category that contains that folder, then activate the folder.
      Figure 10: Select Folders to Move
  8. You will then be asked if you want to move the items to the folder you chose. Activate yes to complete the process.
3.6: The More Info Screen

The More Info screen is used to get additional information or take additional actions for a specific title.

3.6.1: Accessing the More Info Screen

To access the More info screen, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the top left of the screen.
  2. Choose one of the first four categories from the Bookshelf screen.
  3. Activate the More Info button next to the desired title.
3.6.2: Orientation of the More Info Screen

In addition to the usual items on the action bar and tab bar at the top, the More info screen has the following items:

  1. Title name
  2. Author name (if available)
  3. Annotation (books only)
  4. Expand annotation button (appears visually as + , books only)
  5. Narrator (audio only)
  6. Total time (audio only)
  7. Number of volumes (braille only)
  8. Book number
  9. Read button

Figure 11: More Info screen

3.6.3: More Options Pop-up for More Info

While in the More info screen, activating the More options button on the right side of the action bar will open a pop-up window that has the following choices:

  1. Read button (moves into the Now Reading tab, and away from the Bookshelf tab, to read the title)
  2. All books by author button (opens a screen with the list of books by a particular author)
  3. All books in subject button (opens a screen with the list of books matching the subject of the book in focus. There may be multiple buttons if the book belongs to multiple subjects.).
  4. All books in series button (opens a screen with the list of books in that series, only available for books that belong to a series)
  5. Subscribe to or unsubscribe from series button (only available for books that belong to a series)
  6. Subscribe to or unsubscribe from magazine button (magazines only)
  7. Help (get help for the current screen)

The All books buttons (including all books by author, all books in subject, and all books in series) open another screen. These screens are similar to the screen that shows titles for a specific category. This means you can search, sort, download, read, and work with the list of titles.

If a book has multiple authors, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books by each author. The button for the primary author will be first.

If a book belongs to multiple subjects, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books from each subject. The buttons for the subjects are in alphabetical order.

Figure 12: More Options pop-up window

3.6.4: Expanding and Contracting the Annotation

When in the More Info screen for a book, to the right of the annotation is the expand annotation button (appears visually as a plus sign). Once the annotation is expanded, it is maximized, which means the annotation takes up the majority of the screen. When the annotation is expanded, there is a Contract annotation button (appears visually as a minus sign) to let you contract the annotation again.

Figure 13: Expanded annotation

Section 4: Get Books

Use the functions from the Get Books tab to search, browse, download, and manage content from BARD. An Internet connection is required to use the Get Books Tab.

4.1: Accessing the Get Books Tab

To access the Get Books tab, do the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the top of the screen.
  2. Find Get Books, which is the second of four tabs from the left. If Get Books is selected, the Get Books screen appears below the tab bar.
  3. If it is not selected, activate the Get Books tab to select it. The Get Books screen will appear below the tab bar.
4.2: Get Books Quick Start

The following sections provide quick instructions to use the seven features on the Get Books screen to search, browse, download, and manage titles. For more detailed orientation and information about the various features of the Get Books tab, start reading Section 4.3: The Get Books Screen.

4.2.1: Search

To search and download a title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Search (the first feature).
  2. When the Search screen opens, type in the search field or dictate using the voice recognition button from the search bar near the top of the screen.
  3. Review the available search types, formats, and / or languages below the search bar.
  4. To finalize the search, activate the go button from the search bar. Alternatively, activate the search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.
  5. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. The default view is relevant to the Search term; however, it is possible to change the sorting order by title, author, or latest using the buttons above the list of titles.
  6. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  7. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.2: Wish List

The Wish List is used to manage items marked for future reading. To download a title from your Wish List, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Wish List.
  2. When the Wish List opens, read through the titles, or search using the search bar to limit results. The default view is newly added to Wish List on top; however, it is possible to change the sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  3. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  4. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder. Downloaded titles will be automatically deleted from Wish List.
4.2.3: Recently Added to BARD

Recently added to BARD lists books and magazines in the BARD collection in order of when they were added with the most recent titles listed first. To download a title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get books screen, choose Recently added to BARD.
  2. Choose from Audio books, Audio magazines, Braille books, and Braille magazines.
  3. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. Alternatively, search using the search bar to limit results. The default view is newly added on top; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  4. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  5. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.4: Most Popular Books

Most popular books lists the titles in the collection ordered by most to least downloaded. To browse Most popular books and download a title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Most popular books.
  2. Choose from Audio Books and Braille Books.
  3. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. Alternatively, search using the search bar to limit results. The default view is most popular; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  4. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  5. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.5: Browse Magazines

Browse magazines provides access to an alphabetical list of magazines. TO browse magazines, subscribe or unsubscribe to a magazine, and download an issue, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Browse magazines.
  2. Choose from Audio Magazines and Braille Magazines.
  3. When the audio or braille magazines screen opens, either search for the name of a magazine or read through the list and choose one.
  4. When the Results screen for the chosen magazine opens, subscribe, or unsubscribe to that magazine by using the subscribe or unsubscribe button at the top of the screen (to the right of the title).
  5. Alternatively, read through the titles or search using the search bar to limit results.
  6. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  7. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.6: Previous Downloads

This shows a list of previously downloaded titles, to let you download them again on the current device or on another device. To browse and download a previously downloaded title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Previous downloads.
  2. When the Previous downloads screen opens, read through the titles. Alternatively, search using the search bar to limit results. The default view puts the most recently downloaded on top; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  3. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  4. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.7: Search Music

Choosing the Search music option allows you to search for music books and scores from the NLS Music collection. To search, and download music, do the following:

  1. While on the Get Books screen, choose Search music (the last feature).
  2. When the Search music screen opens, type in the search field or dictate using the Voice recognition button from the search bar near the top of the screen.
  3. Review the available search types and formats below the search bar.
  4. To finalize the search, activate the Go button from the search bar. Alternatively, activate the Search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.
  5. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. The default view is relevant to the search term; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title, author, or latest using the buttons above the list of titles.
  6. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  7. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.8: Additional Options

The seven features from the Get Books screen will eventually show a list of titles to download. Before downloading a title, consider activating the More info button for additional choices. The More Info screen will present options such as the book annotation, download title, Add to Wish List, and Delete from Wish List. From that screen, choose the More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots) for additional choices. This opens a pop-up window that lists the previous actions and additional choices such as All books by author, All books in subject, All books in series, Subscribe to or unsubscribe from series, and Subscribe to or unsubscribe from magazine. (These are possible actions, but only actions relevant to the title will be available.)

4.3: The Get Books Screen

The Get Books screen shows these items in the following order:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following two items:
    1. The title in the middle that reads “Get Books”
    2. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots), on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.
  3. Search (by book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator)
  4. Wish List (manage items marked for future reading)
  5. Recently added to BARD (books and magazines recently added to BARD)
  6. Most popular books (most downloaded books)
  7. Browse magazines (all magazines alphabetically by title)
  8. Previous downloads (shows previously downloaded titles for the current account)
  9. Search music (find music books and scores from the music collection)

Figure 14: Get Books tab

Note: Sections 4.4 to 4.10 will cover the seven features from the Get Books screen. For explanations about options in common, including the More Info screen and the More options button, go to Section 4.11: Options That Apply to all Get Books Features.

4.4: Search

This allows you to perform a BARD search by book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, or narrator. When the Search feature from the Get Books screen is selected, the screen for the Search feature opens and shows the following:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (appears visually as a left arrow, goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Search”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.
  3. Search bar (includes search field, Voice recognition, go, and cancel)
  4. Search type (includes Keyword, Title, Author, and Annotation)
  5. Format (includes Audio, Braille, and All)
  6. Language (default options are English and Spanish)

Figure 15: Search

4.4.1: Search Bar

The search bar is used to add a search term by typing or dictating text. You can then edit, cancel, or finalize the search. By default, the search bar includes the search field, the Voice recognition button, and the Go button.

4.4.1.1: The Search Field

The search field is used to type, view, and edit a search term.

  • When the search field is activated, an onscreen keyboard will appear on the bottom half of the display to allow you to start typing. An exception is if an external keyboard or braille device is connected, because you are expected to use that instead.
  • When the search field is activated, the search bar now shows the search field, the Go button to finalize the search, and the cancel button.
4.4.1.2: the Voice Recognition Button

Activate the Voice recognition button to add a search term with speech. Dictated text will appear in the search field.

4.4.1.3: The Go Button

The Go button is used to finalize the search. It is disabled until text is added to the search field. Alternatively, activate the Search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.

4.4.2: Search Type

Located below the search bar, search type determines the search criteria and includes the following:

  1. Search by Keyword (selected by default and includes book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator)
  2. Search by Title
  3. Search by Author (type last name, first name)
  4. Search by Annotation

*Note for TalkBack users: When navigating the buttons to determine search type, TalkBack will indicate the currently chosen option by either saying the word selected before the name of the button with speech, or with the letters “sel” after the name of the button with braille. This also applies to the buttons for format and language.*

4.4.3: Format

Format determines the format(s) that appear in the search results. Available options include:

  1. Format Audio (selected by default)
  2. Format Braille
  3. Format All (includes audio and braille)
4.4.4: Language

Language determines the language of the titles in the search results. The default options include:

  1. Language English (selected by default)
  2. Language Spanish

The above two options are shown by default; however, languages can be added and removed from within the Settings tab. Select User account settings, then Language preferences. English is always the first option. To read more about adding and removing languages, go to Section 5.5.4: Language Preferences.

4.4.5: Results Screen for Search Feature

In addition to the usual items on the action bar and tab bar at the top, this screen has the following items:

  1. Search bar (includes search field with search term from previous screen, Voice recognition button, and Go button, used to modify the search).
  2. Sorting buttons (by Title, Author, or Latest; the default is the one most relevant to the search term on top).
  3. List of titles that match the search (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille if format All is selected, total reading time for audio, number of volumes for braille, date added to BARD if sorting by date, and More Info).

Figure 16: Search Results

4.4.5.1: Modifying the Search

If you activate the search field or the Voice recognition button from the search bar to modify the search term, buttons for search type, format, and language will appear below the search bar and above the buttons for sorting. Once you activate the Go button to confirm the search modification, the additional buttons are no longer available.

4.4.5.2: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles by relevance to the search term, with most relevant on top. Use the three buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)
  3. Sort by Latest (by date when the title was added to BARD, with most recent on top).

Activate one of the above three buttons to select and change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order by relevance, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.5: Wish List

The Wish List is the second option on the Get Books screen. This is a powerful feature used to keep track of books you are interested in reading in the future.

4.5.1: The Benefits of the Wish List

Benefits of the Wish List include:

  • The Wish List is associated with a specific user’s account. If you are signed into several devices with the same account, items added to the Wish List will appear in the Wish List feature of these devices.
  • Items added to the Wish List can be downloaded at a later time or from a different device.
  • If you subscribe to a book series or magazine, any newly added titles will be automatically added to the Wish List when available.
  • Network librarians also have the ability to add, delete, and rearrange items in your Wish List if requested.
  • Once a title is downloaded, it is automatically deleted from the Wish List. If you want to download the same title on multiple devices, either search for and download the book on another device, or work with the Previous downloads feature to download the same title again. For more information, see Section 4.9: Previous Downloads.
  • You can delete items from the Wish List if you don’t want to download them anymore.
4.5.2: The Wish List Screen

This screen has the following items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (Goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Wish List” along with the number of results.
    3. The More options button on the right side (allows user to delete from Wish List and get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get books selected
  3. The Search bar (used to search the Wish List by title or author)
  4. Sorting buttons (by Title and Author; the default is date added to Wish List, with newest on top)
  5. The list of titles in the Wish List (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille if format All is selected, total reading time for audio, number of volumes for braille, date added to BARD if sorting by date, and More Info).
4.5.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the date you added them to the Wish List, with most recent on top. You can also use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to select and change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of date added to Wish List, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.5.3: The Delete Screen of the Wish List

This screen is opened by activating the More options button from the Wish List screen. It is used to manipulate items from the Wish List. It has a similar orientation to the Wish List screen.

The main difference with this screen is the list of titles has checkboxes to let you mark titles for deletion. It does not have a More Info button, since this screen is used to delete titles.

Figure 17: Wish list screen

4.5.3.1: Deleting Titles From the Wish List

To delete titles, do the following:

  1. From the Wish List screen, activate the More options button on the right side of the action bar on the top.
  2. From the pop-up window that opens, choose delete. This opens a list of titles. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title or author’s last name or by date added.
  3. Once focused on the list, check the checkboxes of the titles you want to delete.
  4. Activate ok below the list once you are satisfied with the titles you want to delete.
  5. You will be asked if you are sure you want to delete. Activating yes will delete the titles. Focus will then go back to the main Wish List screen to download titles, not delete titles.
4.6: Recently Added to BARD

Recently added to BARD is the third feature of Get Books and is used to browse books and magazines recently added to the BARD collection.

4.6.1: Orientation of the Recently Added to BARD Screen

The Recently added to BARD screen shows the following:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Recently added to BARD”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.
  3. Audio Books
  4. Audio Magazines
  5. Braille Books
  6. Braille Magazines

Figure 18: Recently Added to BARD categories screen

4.6.2: Results Screen for Recently Added to BARD

After choosing Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines, the results screen shows the following items below the action bar and tab bar:

  1. The Search bar (used to search within the current category by title and author)
  2. Buttons to sort the list by Title or Author (default is by date added to BARD, newest on top)
  3. The list of titles (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille if format All is selected, total reading time for audio, number of volumes for braille, date added to BARD if sorting by date, and More Info).

Figure 19: Recently Added to BARD results screen

4.6.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the date added to BARD, with most recent on top. You can also use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to select them and change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of date added to BARD, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.7: Most Popular Books

Most popular books is used to browse the most downloaded books.

4.7.1: Orientation of the Most Popular Books Screen

The Most popular books screen shows these items in the following order:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Most popular books”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.
  3. Audio Books
  4. Braille Books

Figure 20: Most Popular Books categories screen.

4.7.2: The Results Screen for Most Popular Books

The results screen appears when Audio or Braille Books is chosen and shows the following below the action bar and tab bar:

  1. The Search bar (used to search within the current category by title and author)
  2. Buttons to sort the list by Title or Author (default is by most popular)
  3. The list of titles (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille if format All is selected, total reading time for audio, number of volumes for braille, date added to BARD if sorting by date, and More Info).

Figure 21: Most Popular Books results screen

4.7.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the most downloaded books recently, with the most popular on top. Use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to select and change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of most popular, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.8: Browse Magazines

Browse magazines is used to browse all audio or braille magazines alphabetically by title.

4.8.1: Select a Format Screen

Choosing Browse Magazines will open the screen to select a format. This screen has the following:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Browse Magazines”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get books selected.
  3. Audio Magazines
  4. Braille Magazines

Figure 22: Browse Magazines format selection screen

4.8.2: Choose a Magazine Screen

Choosing a format will open the choose a magazine screen. The below orientation applies to both audio and braille magazines and has the following below the action bar and tab bar:

  1. The Search bar (used to search for a specific magazine)
  2. The list of magazines (in alphabetical order by title)

Figure 23: Browse magazines, audio magazine list

4.8.3: Manage Chosen Magazine Screen

Once you select a magazine title, the resulting screen displays the following items:

  1. In addition to the usual items on the action bar, between the title and the More options button, this screen has a button to subscribe or unsubscribe to the current magazine.
  2. The Search bar (used to search for a specific magazine issue)
  3. The list of magazines (in order by newest on top). Activate the name to download.

Figure 24: Manage chosen magazine screen

4.9: Previous Downloads

The Previous downloads feature is used to view and manage previously downloaded titles.

4.9.1: Benefits of Previous Downloads

Benefits of Previous Downloads include:

  • Allows you to view a list of previously downloaded titles
  • Downloaded titles include books or magazines that are downloaded on another device using the same user account
  • Allows you to redownload a previously deleted title
  • You will be informed if a specific title is downloaded to the current device when browsing titles.
4.9.2: The Previous Downloads Screen

When Previous downloads from the Get Books screen is selected, the following items are shown:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Previous downloads” along with the number of results
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get books selected
  3. The Search bar (used to search by title or author)
  4. Sorting buttons by Title and Author (default is by date downloaded with newest on top)
  5. The List of titles in Previous downloads (includes title where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille, total reading time for audio, number of volumes for braille, and More Info).

Figure 25: Previous Downloads screen

4.9.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the downloaded date, with most recent on top. Use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title).
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name).

Activate one of the above two buttons to select and change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of date downloaded from BARD, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.10: Search Music

Search music is used to find music books and scores from the music collection by series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator.

The Search music feature has similar functions as those from the Search feature (the first feature of Get Books). There are two differences:

  1. Search music only searches the music collection.
  2. Search music does not have buttons to filter by language, since music titles do not have an assigned language.

When Search music from the Get Books screen is selected, the following items are shown:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Search music”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get books selected.
  3. The Search bar (includes search field, voice recognition, Go, and cancel)
  4. Search type (includes Keyword, Title, Author, and Annotation)
  5. Format (includes Audio, Braille, and All)

Figure 26: Search Music screen
