4.11: Options That Apply to All Get Books Features

This section explains the common options that applies to all seven features of the Get Books screen, including the results, More Info, and More Options.

4.11.1: Common Options in the Results Screen

All features of Get Books lead to a results screen which allows you to do the following:

  1. Use the Search box to change the results that appear
  2. Change the sorting order
  3. Activate the name of the title you want to read
  4. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.11.2: Select Folder to Download Screen

This screen only appears when you activate a title you want to download, and the default folder contains subfolders. BARD mobile will know if the title you chose belongs to Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines. Therefore, only the folder of the same name along with its subfolders will be available. If you want the title to go to the default folder, activate the name of that folder.

Figure 28: Select Folder to Download screen top level

Alternatively, there is an option to expand or collapse the default folder. When it is expanded, activate the desired subfolder to download the title.

Figure 29: Select Folder to Download screen with expanded subfolders

4.11.3: The More Info Screen

The More Info screen is used to get additional information or options for a specific title.

4.11.3.1: Accessing the More Info Screen

To access the More info screen, do the following:

  1. Choose one of the seven features from the Get Books screen.
  2. For some features, the results screen will open once that feature is chosen. Other features will require choosing additional options from additional screens before getting to the results.
  3. When the screen that shows titles opens, activate the More Info button next to the desired title.
4.11.3.2: Orientation of the More Info Screen

The More info screen has the following items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle with the name “Details”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get access to additional choices for the current title).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.
  3. Title name
  4. Author name (if available)
  5. Annotation (books only)
  6. The expand annotation button (appears visually as a plus sign, books only)
  7. Narrator (audio only)
  8. Total time (audio only)
  9. Number of volumes (braille only)
  10. Book number
  11. Add to Wish List button (available when the title is not already on the Wish List or downloaded to the current device)
  12. Delete from Wish List button (available when the title is previously added to the Wish List)
  13. Download button (available when the title is not downloaded to the current device)
  14. Read button (available when the title is downloaded to the current device)

Figure 30: More Info screen

4.11.3.3: More Options pop-up for More Info

While in the More info screen, activating The More options button will open a pop-up window that has the following choices:

  1. Download button (available when the title is not downloaded to the current device)
  2. Add to Wish List button (available when the title is not already on the Wish List or downloaded to the current device)
  3. Delete from Wish List button (available when the title is previously added to the Wish List)
  4. Read button (moves into the Now Reading tab and away from the Get Books tab to read the title; available when the title is downloaded to the current device)
  5. All books by author button (opens a screen with a list of books by author, books only).
  6. All books in subject button (opens a screen with a list of books matching subject, may have multiple buttons if book belongs to multiple subjects, books only)
  7. All books in series button (opens a screen with a list of series, only available for books that belong to a series)
  8. Subscribe to or unsubscribe from series button (only available for books that belong to a series)
  9. Subscribe to or unsubscribe from magazine button (magazines only)

The All books’ buttons (including all books by author, all books in subject, and all books in series) open another screen. These screens are similar to the screen that shows titles for a specific category. This means you can search, sort, download, read, and work with the list of titles.

If a book has multiple authors, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books by each author. The button for the primary author will be first.

If a book belongs to multiple subjects, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books from each subject. The buttons for the subjects are in alphabetical order.

Figure 31: More Options pop-over window in the More Info screen

4.11.3.4: Expanding and Contracting the Annotation

When in the More Info screen for a book, to the right of the annotation is a button to expand the annotation.

Once the annotation is expanded, it is maximized, which means the annotation takes up the majority of the screen.

When the annotation is expanded, there is a contract annotation button (appears visually as a minus sign), allowing you to contract the annotation again.

Figure 32: Expanded annotation in the More Info screen

Section 5: Settings

Use the Settings tab to adjust audio, display, and user account settings to optimize your reading experience.

5.1: Accessing the Settings Tab

To access the Settings tab, do the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the top of the screen
  2. Find Settings, which is the third tab from the left
  3. Activate the Settings tab to select it. The Settings screen will appear in the content area below the tab bar.
5.2: The Settings Screen

The Settings screen contains the following items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following two items:
    1. The title in the middle that reads “Settings.”
    2. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Settings selected.
  3. Audio settings
  4. Braille and Display settings
  5. User account settings
  6. Device Accessibility settings (opens the accessibility settings for your Android phone or tablet)
  7. The Currently running version of BARD Mobile

Figure 33: Settings

5.3: Audio Settings

The first button on the Settings screen is the Audio settings button. The Audio settings screen has the following options below the action bar and tab bar:

  1. Speed
  2. Tone
  3. Background playback
  4. Auto-lock during playback
  5. Automatic start playback
  6. Automatic 5 second rewind
  7. Skippable
  8. Verbosity
  9. Silence button beeps
  10. Restore Defaults

Figure 34: Audio settings screen

5.3.1: Speed

The audio settings for speed and tone are the global settings for playing audio content. Speed and tone are also available while in the Now Reading tab for individual audio titles. A global setting will affect audio titles that did not have individual adjustments. For example, if the global audio settings speed is set to 1.5 times and you change a specific audio title to .75 times, then the .75 times would only apply to that specific title. The other audio content will remain at 1.5 times.

Speed allows you to adjust the global audio playback speed. The default speed is 1times. To adjust the speed:

  1. From Audio settings, activate Speed. This will open a screen for speed adjustment.
  2. Activate decrease speed and increase speed to adjust the global reading speed by .125 times.
  3. When you are on the speed that you want, activate ok to save the changes and go back to the previous screen.
5.3.2: Tone

Tone allows you to adjust the global audio playback tone for audiobooks and magazines. Increasing the tone adds more treble and decreasing the tone adds more bass while reading audio content. The default tone is zero. To adjust the tone:

  1. From Audio settings, activate Tone. This will open a screen for tone adjustment.
  2. Activate decrease tone and increase tone to adjust the global reading tone by 1.
  3. When you are on the tone that you want, activate ok to save the changes and go back to the previous screen.
5.3.3: Background Playback

Background playback determines whether or not audio content continues to play when you lock the device or move away from the BARD Mobile app. The default for this setting is On.

When it is on, the audio content will continue to play, even if the device is locked or you move away from BARD Mobile.

When Background playback is set to Off, BARD Mobile will stop playing when you lock the device or move away from the app.

5.3.4: Auto-Lock during Playback

Auto-lock during playback is used to determine how your Android phone or tablet will behave while audio content is playing. It works in conjunction with the Screen timeout feature in the main Settings app of your Android phone or tablet, which determines how much time will pass before the device turns off the display in order to save battery life and prevent unintended operation. The default for Auto-lock during playback is Off.

When the Auto-lock during playback setting is Off and audio content is playing with the BARD Mobile app, the device will not lock, no matter how the Screen timeout feature was set in the main Settings of the device.

When audio content is not playing, the device will lock according to the time chosen for the Screen timeout feature in the main Settings.

When the Auto-lock during playback setting is On, the device will lock according to the time chosen for the Screen timeout feature in the main Settings of the device, whether or not audio content is playing.

If audio content is playing, the device locks, and the Background playback setting is turned off, BARD Mobile will stop playing.

If audio content is playing, the device locks, and the Background playback setting is turned on, BARD Mobile will continue to play.

5.3.5: Automatic Start Playback

Automatic start playback determines the app’s behavior when you choose an audio title to read and is placed in the Now Reading tab with player controls for the audio. This is off by default. When it is off, you must activate the play button to begin playing the title. When Automatic start playback is on, the audio content will begin playing without the need to activate the play button.

5.3.6: Automatic 5 Second Rewind

Automatic 5 second rewind is off by default. When this is off, resuming playback of audio content will continue reading from the last reading position.

When Automatic 5 second rewind is on, audio content will automatically rewind five seconds from the last reading position under the below conditions:

  • While reading an audio title, you read another audio title and then choose to continue reading the original audio title.
  • You exit the BARD Mobile app, reopen it, and resume audio in the Now Reading tab.
5.3.7: Skippable

Skippable allows you to control whether items such as sidebars, page numbers, and footnotes, when marked by the producer of the audio content, will be played or skipped during normal playback. The default setting is to play this information.

5.3.8: Verbosity

This setting determines if messages are spoken when you navigate a book using the buttons on the Now Reading screen. By default, the Verbosity option is on, and spoken messages are provided. This is similar to the messages heard when using a digital talking-book player.

5.3.9: Silence Button Beeps

The Silence button beeps option allows you to determine whether or not to play the beeps that sound when activating the button controls in the Now Reading tab. This is off by default.

When off, audible beeps will sound when player control buttons are activated in the Now Reading tab. When it is on, the beeps will not sound.

5.3.10: Restore Defaults

The Restore Defaults button is located at the bottom of the Audio settings screen. This is used to reset all audio settings to the BARD Mobile default values. To learn about default values for each of the above settings, please read the appropriate sections.

When you activate the Restore Defaults button, this action will take place immediately. There is no confirmation before the action is taken. It is not possible to undo this action.

*Note for TalkBack users: When activating the Restore Defaults button, TalkBack does not say that any changes were made. However, reviewing the settings will reflect the default values.*

5.4: Braille and Display Settings

The second button on the Settings screen is the Braille and Display settings button. Once activated, the following options are available:

  1. Contrast
  2. Braille reflow
  3. Braille display length
  4. Braille autoscroll
  5. Time per Line
  6. Time per Character
  7. Restore Defaults

Contrast is used to configure the way text and background appear visually and is not related to braille.

Figure 35: Braille and Display settings

5.4.1: Contrast

Contrast allows you to choose an option for the text in relation to the background color. This is set to black text on a white background by default. Available contrast choices include:

  1. Black text on a white background
  2. White text on a black background
  3. Black text on a yellow background
  4. Yellow text on a black background
5.4.2: Braille Reflow and Braille Display Length

Braille reflow allows BARD Mobile to tailor line lengths to match braille display size, breaking lines only when necessary (for example, at the ends of paragraphs), to provide a smoother reading experience. This is off by default.

When Braille reflow is on, you can customize the braille display length by doing the following:

  1. Choose the Braille Display length option
  2. Use the decrease and increase buttons to make the adjustment. The default value is 40 cells.
  3. When satisfied with the selection, activate Ok on the bottom of the screen to save the change and go back to the other Braille and Display settings.

For more information, read Section 7.11 Braille Reflow.

*Note for TalkBack users: When braille reflow is off, you cannot adjust braille display length. When navigating to braille display length, TalkBack will indicate “disabled” both with speech and in braille.*

5.4.3: Braille Autoscroll

Autoscroll allows you to read a braille book with minimal hand movement. This is set to off by default. When autoscroll is on, the braille display will begin advancing automatically. Below the autoscroll button are the options for Time per Line and Time per Character. When Braille autoscroll is turned on, Time per Line and Time per Character can be increased or decreased.

The default value for autoscroll Time per Line is 1,500 milliseconds (1.5 seconds) for a 40-cell display. Changing the display length will prorate the amount. For example, setting display length to 20 will result in the autoscroll Time per Line to change to 625 milliseconds. The default Time per Character on the line is 50 milliseconds.

For more information, read Section 7.12: Braille Autoscroll.

*Note for TalkBack users: When Braille autoscroll is off, you cannot adjust Time per Line and Time per Character. When navigating to Time per Line or Time per Character, TalkBack will indicate “disabled” both with speech and in braille.*

5.4.4: Restore Defaults

The Restore Defaults button is the last option on the Braille and Display settings screen. This is used to reset all Braille and Display settings to BARD Mobile default values. To learn about default values for each of the above settings, please read the appropriate sections.

When you activate the Restore Defaults button, the action will take place immediately. There is no confirmation before the action is taken. It is not possible to undo this action.

* Note for TalkBack users: When activating the Restore Defaults button, TalkBack does not say that any changes were made. However, reviewing the settings will reflect the default values.*

5.5: User Account Settings

The third button on the Settings screen is the User account settings button. This has the following options below the action bar and tab bar:

  1. BARD account
  2. Download over mobile network
  3. Show me (both audio and braille, audio only, or braille only)
  4. Language preferences
  5. Storage Location
  6. Application Reset
  7. Restore Defaults

Figure 36: User account settings

5.5.1: BARD Account

The BARD account screen is used to view and edit account information, and read the device ID.

When the BARD account button is activated, a new screen opens displaying the following items below the action bar and tab bar:

  1. Heading that reads “Login Information”
  2. Text field for User name (email address)
  3. Text field for password
  4. Heading for Device ID
  5. The device ID itself

Figure 37: BARD account information

BARD Mobile allows multiple users to use one device. Users log into BARD Mobile using a unique email address and password. This is done by changing the information from the two edit fields to reflect the proper email address and password for each user.

When the same device is used to sign into multiple accounts, the current user will also see books from other users, but the current user cannot read those books.

5.5.2: Download over Mobile Network

Download materials over a mobile network only appears in User Account Settings if the device has cellular capabilities. This is off by default. When on, you can download titles using a mobile network. A data plan is required, and data rates may apply.

5.5.3: Show Me

Show me allows you to customize the preferred format shown when specifically viewing three features of the Get Books tab. This includes Recently added to BARD, Most popular books, and Browse magazines. The default is both audio and braille. Additional options include audio only and braille only.

Note: The sections on the three features lists the options that appear if this setting is set to the default of both audio and braille. When reading Sections 4.6.1: Orientation of the Recently Added to BARD Screen, 4.7.1: Orientation of the Most Popular Books Screen, and 4.8.1: Select a Format Screen, results will be different if this setting is changed.

5.5.4: Language Preferences

Language preferences determine the available language options when using the search feature of the Get Books tab. By default, the first language is English and the second is Spanish. Choose the language preferences button, browse through the available languages, activate a language to add it, and continue to activate an added language to change the order that it appears in the search feature or remove it. While browsing languages, you will be informed of the number of available titles for that specific language.

The first language is English and cannot be changed. Additional languages will be presented in the order they will appear in the search feature. For example, the default has English as the first language and Spanish as the second language. If you add French, this will be the third language. If you would like French to be the second language, activate French again to change it to the second language. When satisfied with your language preferences, activate the navigate up button on the top left to return to the User account settings.

5.5.5: Storage Location

Storage Location provides read only information with the address of where downloaded titles are stored on the device.

5.5.6: Application Reset

Application Reset allows you to reset BARD Mobile. When this option is selected, a confirmation screen opens. Choose Continue to reset the application. Choose Cancel to cancel the reset.

Warning: When resetting the application, all BARD Mobile data from the device will be deleted, including downloaded books and magazines.

5.5.7: Restore Defaults

The Restore Defaults button is the last option on the User account settings screen. This is used to reset account settings for Download over Mobile Network, Show me, and Language preferences to BARD Mobile default values. When you activate the Restore Defaults button, the action will take place immediately. There is no confirmation before the action is taken. It is not possible to undo this action.

*Note for TalkBack users: When activating the Restore Defaults button, TalkBack does not say that any changes were made. However, reviewing the settings will reflect the default values.*

5.6: Device Accessibility Settings

This provides a quick method for you to go to the accessibility settings which is part of the settings app of your device. Some popular options may include TalkBack and Magnification. Since these options are specific to your device, read the User Guide for your phone or tablet for more information.

Section 6: Reading Audio Titles

The Now Reading tab, located near the top right, is used to read audio and braille titles that are downloaded onto the device. The information in the main content area of the Now Reading tab is different depending on if an audio, or braille title is opened. This section provides information about working with audio titles in the Now Reading tab. For information about the Now Reading tab when a braille title is opened, go to Section 7: Reading Braille Titles.

6.1: Accessing the Now Reading Tab

If the Now Reading tab is selected without an opened title, you will get the following message:

“No book is currently selected to play. Please select a book from the Bookshelf to play.”

To open a title, locate a book or magazine from the Bookshelf, or locate and download a title from Get Books, then activate the read button, automatically moving you to the Now Reading tab.

If a title is already opened, and you explore another tab, you can return to the Now Reading tab to access controls for the current title by doing the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the top of the screen.
  2. Find Now Reading, which is the last of four tabs, near the top right.
  3. Activate the Now Reading tab to select it. The main screen of Now Reading with the most recent title will appear on the content area below the tab bar.
6.2: Orientation of the Now Reading Screen for Audio Titles

When the Now Reading tab is selected and an audio title is opened, these items appear on the screen in the following order:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The title in the middle that reads “Now Reading”
    2. The Recently read button (appears visually as an opened book)
    3. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots), on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Now Reading selected.
  3. The title of the Current Book or Magazine
  4. The current section title
  5. The Reading time and current position
  6. The Navigation button
  7. The Progress bar
  8. Sleep timer (includes sleep button and sleep status)
  9. Bookmark
  10. Jump by various navigation points (includes Previous, the Jump by menu, and Next)
  11. Primary player controls (includes Rewind, Play/Stop, and Fast Forward)
  12. Speed/Tone

Figure 38: Now Reading Audio screen

6.3: Recently Read

Recently read lets you view and continue reading Recently read titles on your Android device. This includes both audio and braille. If a Recently read title is deleted, then it will not be included.

6.3.1: View Recently Read

To view Recently read from the Now Reading screen, activate the Recently read button from the action bar near the top right .

6.3.2: The Recently Read Screen

Recently read lists titles with the most recent on top. Each item includes the title and audio or braille icon to identify the format. Choosing a title will exit this screen and open that title in the now reading screen.

To exit Recently read without choosing another title, activate the navigate up button on the top left.

Figure 38: Now Reading Audio screen

6.4: The Title of the Current Book or Magazine

Below the tab bar is the title information, which shows the title of the book or magazine you are currently reading.

6.5: The Current Section title

The current section title can include the title of the current section, part, chapter, article, recipe, etc.

6.6: Reading Time and Progress Bar

The Reading time provides the time elapsed and the total amount of time for the audio title.

Below the reading time is the Progress bar. This is used to adjust the position within the title.

Adjust the slider to the left to move the reading position toward the beginning of the title or to the right to move the reading position toward the end of the title.

*Note for TalkBack users: Although the progress bar is directly below the reading time, if you are on reading time and you perform a one-finger flick right to go to the next item, you will go to the navigation button because it is the next item to the right of the reading time. Performing another one-finger flick right will then take you to the progress bar.

To adjust the progress bar:

  1. Navigate to the current position slider.
  2. Advance the current position by five percent with a one-finger flick up. Go back by five percent with a one-finger flick down.
  3. Alternatively, perform a one-finger double tap and hold, and then slowly move that finger to move by smaller increments. Release your finger when you are finished making adjustments.
  4. When satisfied with the current position, navigate to another item to avoid accidentally making additional adjustments.*
6.7: Navigation

To access the Navigation screen, activate the Navigation button located to the right of the reading time.

The Navigation screen provides several methods to navigate the current title.

6.7.1: Orientation for the Navigation Screen

The navigation screen has the following Items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Book Navigation”
    3. The More options button on the right side (used to change skippable settings).
  2. The Toolbar to determine the items that appear below (includes Contents, Page, and Bookmark; Contents is selected by default.)
  3. List of items determined by the toolbar

Figure 40: Book Navigation screen

6.7.2: Skippable

Skippable allows you to control whether items such as sidebars, page numbers, and footnotes, when marked by the producer of the audio content, are played or skipped during normal playback. The default setting is to play this information. To change this setting:

  1. From the Book Navigation screen, activate the More Options button on the top right.
  2. Activate the Read Skippable Elements option.
  3. Activate the Skip items marked as skippable to toggle the check box.
  4. Activate ok to save the changes and go back to the Book Navigation screen.

This is for the current audio title. To adjust the skippable setting for all titles, change it under Audio settings in the Settings tab.

6.7.3: Navigating by Contents

To browse the contents for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. Make sure Contents is selected on the toolbar.
  2. The contents appear below the toolbar.
  3. A + next to a navigation item indicates that the item can be expanded to display additional information. If an item is expanded, a - will appear next to that item to indicate that the item can be collapsed to hide the additional information. Tap and hold to expand or collapse.
  4. Tap on the desired item to close the Navigation screen. The playing position moves to the selected navigation point.

Figure 41: Book Navigation screen—Contents

*Note for TalkBack users: If a navigation item can be expanded to show more items, TalkBack both verbalizes and shows in braille the word “plus” in front of the item name. Perform a one-finger double tap and hold to expand the item, resulting in additional items below.

If a navigation item is already expanded showing more items below that item, TalkBack both verbalizes and shows in braille the word “minus” in front of the item name. Perform a one-finger double tap and hold to collapse the item, hiding additional items below.

Performing a one-finger double tap on the item you want closes the Navigation screen, and the playing position moves to the selected navigation point.*

6.7.4: Navigating by Page

To go to a specific page for the current title while in navigation:

  1. Make sure Page is selected on the toolbar. (Not all titles have page navigation.)
  2. The pages appear below the toolbar.
  3. Activate the desired page to close the Navigation screen. The playing position moves to the selected page.

Figure 42: Navigation screen—Page

*Note for TalkBack users: When navigating the toolbar, if page navigation is not available, then TalkBack says “Page button disabled” with speech or shows “Page btn disabled” in braille.*

6.7.5: Navigating by Bookmark

These instructions are for accessing bookmarks within Navigation. For detailed information to insert, go to, and remove bookmarks, read Section 6.9: Bookmark.

To go to a Bookmark for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. Make sure Bookmark is selected on the toolbar. The bookmarks appear below the toolbar. This includes Beginning of book, any previously added Bookmarks, and End of book.
  2. Activate the desired bookmark to close the Navigation screen. The reading position moves to the selected bookmark.

Figure 43: Book Navigation screen—Bookmark

6.8: Sleep Timer

The Sleep button is used to automatically stop audio playback after a specific amount of time or at the end of a navigation point.

The Sleep status provides information about the current state of the sleep timer. If the sleep timer is off, then the sleep status will show, “sleep off”. If the sleep timer is on, then it will show, “sleep” followed by the remaining time.

6.8.1: Available Options with the Sleep Timer

Activating the Sleep button will offer the below options in the following order:

  1. 15 minutes
  2. 30 minutes
  3. 45 minutes
  4. 60 minutes
  5. End of navigation point (section, part, chapter, article, recipe, etc.)
  6. Off
6.8.2: Working with the Sleep Button
  1. Activating the Sleep button once sets it for 15 minutes (if the title was not playing before activating the Sleep timer, this starts the audio).
  2. Activating the Sleep button repeatedly in quick succession cycles through the available options.

*Note for TalkBack users: While on the Sleep button, perform a one-finger double tap to set the sleep timer to 15 minutes. Perform additional one-finger double taps in quick succession to cycle through the additional options.*

6.8.3: Stopping the Sleep Timer

Activate the Play/Stop button to stop the Sleep timer as well as the audio playback.

The sleep timer can also be stopped by activating the Sleep button repeatedly in quick succession to eventually land on the off option; however, this does not stop the audio playback.

*Note for TalkBack users: Quickly activate the Play/Stop button by performing a two-finger double tap. This gesture can be performed without the need to first locate the Play/Stop button.*

6.9: Bookmark

A bookmark is used to remember reading positions to read at a later time, such as an interesting passage or a recipe. Each title has its own bookmarks, and BARD Mobile remembers bookmarks even if you close the app.

6.9.1: Inserting Bookmarks

Selecting the Bookmark button inserts a bookmark at the current reading position.

6.9.2: Going to a Previously Added Bookmark

There are two methods to go to a previously added bookmark.

Method one:

  1. From the Now Reading screen, activate the Jump by menu button. Continue activating the Jump by menu button until the Jump by Bookmark option is chosen.
  2. Activate the Previous button to go to the previous bookmark and the Next button to go to the next bookmark.

Method two:

  1. From the Now Reading screen, activate the Navigation button (located to the right of the reading time).
  2. Activate the Bookmark button (located on the right side of the toolbar near the top). Added bookmarks are listed below the toolbar.
  3. Activate the desired bookmark. (This exits the Navigation screen and moves the reading position to the bookmarked location).
6.9.3: Removing Bookmarks

To remove a bookmark while reading a book, stop audio playback, go to the desired bookmark using one of the two methods discussed in section 6.9.2, and then select the Bookmark button.

6.10: Jump by Various Navigation Points

Below the Sleep timer and Bookmark buttons are three buttons to allow you to go to various navigation points. These buttons appear in the following order from left to right:

  1. Previous
  2. Jump by menu
  3. Next

The Jump by menu button is described first because it affects the behavior of the Previous and Next buttons.

6.10.1: Jump by Menu

The Jump by menu button is located between the Previous and Next buttons. Activating the Jump by menu cycles through and selects the different types of navigation. Some possible examples include parts, chapters, sections, recipes, and bookmarks. Once you locate the desired navigation, use the Previous and Next buttons to move to the previous and next navigation.

6.10.2: Previous and Next Navigation

Located to the left of the Jump by menu button is the Previous button, and to the right of the Jump by menu button is the Next button.

Once you select the desired navigation with the Jump by menu button:

  • Activate the Previous or Next buttons to go back or forward by one item.
  • Press and hold the Previous and Next buttons to quickly go back and forward by five navigation points, with the exception of pages which moves ten pages at a time.

*Note for TalkBack users: To go back or forward one navigation point at a time, do a one-finger double tap while on the Previous or Next buttons.

To quickly go back or forward five navigation points or ten pages, do a one-finger double tap and hold while on the Previous or Next buttons.*

When working with the Previous and Next buttons to go back or forward through items, if the audio title is not playing, the heading of the new item selected is read. Activate the Play/Stop button to resume audio playback. If audio is already playing, playback automatically starts from the new location.

6.11: Primary Player Controls

Below the Previous, Jump by menu, and Next buttons are three buttons for primary player controls. These buttons appear in the following order from left to right:

  1. Rewind
  2. Play/Stop
  3. Fast forward

The Play/Stop button is described first because the Play/Stop button affects the behavior of the Rewind and Fast forward buttons.

6.11.1: Play/Stop

The Play/Stop button is located between the Rewind and Fast forward buttons. Activate the button to either start or stop playing a title.

*Note for TalkBack users: once an audio title is opened in Now Reading, and you press the play button to start playback, you can Quickly activate the Play/Stop button by performing a two-finger double tap. This gesture can be performed without the need to first locate the Play/Stop button.

If Background Playback is on and an audio title is opened in the Now Reading tab, you can also use the two-finger double tap gesture to play or stop the audio even when you are away from the BARD Mobile app. For more information about Background playback, go to Section 5.3.3: Background Playback.

The two-finger double tap gesture to play or stop the audio is a standard TalkBack feature. Using it to play audio for BARD Mobile only works if the most recently playing audio is with BARD Mobile.*

6.11.2: Rewind and Fast Forward

Located to the left of the Play/Stop button is the Rewind button, and to the right of the Play/Stop button is the Fast forward button. They are used to move the reading position backwards and forward by a specific amount of time.

  • Activate the Rewind or Fast forward buttons to go back or forward by five seconds.
  • Press and hold the Rewind or Fast forward buttons to go back or forward by larger amounts of time. Release on the desired spoken amount (20 seconds, 1 minute, 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, 2 hours, etc.).

When working with the Rewind and Fast forward buttons, if the audio title is not playing, audio playback does not resume automatically. Activate the Play/Stop button to resume audio playback. If audio is already playing, playback starts from the new location.

*Note for TalkBack users: To go back or forward five seconds at a time, perform a one-finger double tap while on the Rewind or Fast forward buttons.

To Rewind or Fast forward by larger amounts of time, perform a one-finger double tap and hold while on the Rewind or Fast forward buttons. Release on the desired spoken amount (20 seconds, 1 minute, 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, 2 hours, etc.).*

6.12: Speed and Tone

The last controls on the Now Reading screen are used to adjust the Speed and Tone for the current audio title and include:

  1. Decrease button
  2. Speed and Tone button, which is used to change the function of the decrease and increase buttons that appear to the left and right of the Speed and Tone button
  3. Increase button.

These controls are used to adjust the speed and tone for the current audio title. You can change the global speed and tone under Audio settings in the Settings tab. For more information, go to Sections 5.3: Audio settings, 5.3.1: Speed, and 5.3.2: Tone.

*Note for TalkBack users: When focus is on the Speed/Tone button, TalkBack reads the currently chosen function, for example, “Speed button” or “Tone button.” The decrease and increase buttons located to the left and right of the Speed/Tone button are used to adjust the speed or tone, depending on which function is selected.*

6.12.1: Adjusting the Speed

Speed determines how fast or slow the narration is. The default speed is 1 times. To adjust the speed of the current audio title:

  1. Locate the Speed and Tone button.
  2. If it is set to Speed, ignore this step. If it is set to Tone, activate it to change it to Speed.
  3. Activate the decrease speed button to the left of the speed and tone button to slow it down by .125 times.
  4. Activate the Increase speed button to the right of the speed and tone button to speed it up by .125 times.

Figure 44: Speed adjustment on the now playing audio screen

6.12.2: Adjusting the Tone

Tone determines the level of bass or treble in the reading voice. The default tone is zero. To adjust the tone of the current audio title:

  1. Locate the Speed/Tone button.
  2. If it is set to Tone, ignore this step. If it is set to Speed, activate it to change it to Tone.
  3. Activate the Decrease tone button to the left of the speed and tone button to add bass.
  4. Activate the Increase tone button to the right of the speed and tone button to add treble.

Figure 45: Tone adjustment on the Now Playing audio screen.

Section 7: Reading Braille Titles

The Now Reading tab, located near the top right, is used to read audio and braille titles that are downloaded onto the device. The information in the main content area of the Now Reading tab is different depending on if an audio, or braille title is opened. This section provides information about working with braille titles in the Now Reading tab. For information about the Now Reading tab when an audio title is opened, go to Section 6: Reading Audio Titles.

7.1: Accessing the Now Reading Tab

If the Now Reading tab is selected without an opened title, you will get the following message:

“No book is currently selected to play. Please select a book from the Bookshelf to play.”

To open a title, locate a book or magazine from the Bookshelf, or locate and download a title from Get Books, then activate the read button, automatically moving you to the Now Reading tab.

If a title is already opened, and you explore another tab, you can return to the Now Reading tab to access controls for the current title by doing the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the top of the screen.
  2. Find Now Reading, which is the last of four tabs, near the top right.
  3. Activate the Now Reading tab to select it. The main screen of Now Reading with the most recent title will appear on the content area below the tab bar.
7.2: Requirements for Reading Braille Titles for BARD Mobile

Be sure to carefully read all the requirements because not all Android devices will support all required items.

  1. While braille titles are downloaded to the BARD Mobile app on the Android device, a connected refreshable braille display is required to read them. (The process of connecting a Braille display can be different and specific to the device. Refer to the braille display’s User Guide for connection instructions.) For general help with connecting a braille display, read Connect to a braille display - Android Accessibility Help External.
  2. Talkback 13 or higher must be installed and enabled for a braille display to work with an Android device. All instructions for Section 7 assume that TalkBack is running.
  3. To read any braille book or magazine with BARD Mobile, Add North American Braille Computer Code as a language in the braille display settings, and then choose it as the preferred reading language and preferred writing language. Note that some Android devices may not have North American Braille Computer code as an option. If North American Braille Computer code is not an option, then even if you can connect the braille display to your Android device, you cannot read braille titles from the BARD Mobile for Android app.
  4. When navigating the content within the braille title, TalkBack does not speak the content correctly, because BARD Mobile does not translate braille files into visually readable, fully spelled-out text. To avoid listening to incomprehensible speech, mute the TalkBack speech (Do not turn off TalkBack) and read the braille content with the braille display. You can mute or unmute speech by performing a two-finger triple tap and hold gesture or type m with dots 7 and 8 (Dots 1-3-4-7-8) with the braille display.
    Note: when muting and unmuting speech, TalkBack reads “spoken feedback off” or “spoken feedback on” with speech. TalkBack shows “mute” or “unmute with braille.”
7.2.1: Setting Braille Language to North American Braille Computer Code

Braille titles are translated before they are available for BARD. Since titles are translated in different formats and grades, to read braille titles with BARD Mobile, you must Add North American Braille Computer Code as a language in the braille display settings, and then choose it as the preferred reading language and preferred writing language. This prevents the content from being translated again and allows you to read it in the title’s original translation.

The below instructions are confirmed with a Google Pixel phone running Android 15 and Google tablet running Android 14; however, the exact path for other devices may be different:

  1. Open the Settings app of your Android device.
  2. Activate Accessibility.
  3. Activate TalkBack.
  4. While in TalkBack, choose Settings.
  5. Activate Braille display.
  6. Choose languages.
  7. Activate add a language.
  8. Choose English
  9. Locate North American Braille Computer Code and if it is not checked, activate it to check the box and add it.
  10. Activate the navigate up button near the top left twice to go back two screens.
  11. Activate Preferred reading language.
  12. Locate North American Braille Computer Code and if it is not checked, activate it to check the box to choose it. A window will then appear telling you that you can quickly change added reading languages by pressing Dots 1-3-4-7-8. You can choose to keep getting this message every time you change the reading language or for it to not show this in the future. Confirm your choice by activating the ok button.
  13. Activate Preferred typing language.
  14. Locate North American Braille Computer Code and if it is not checked, activate it to check the box to choose it. A window will then appear telling you that you can quickly change added typing languages by pressing Dots 2-4-7-8. You can choose to keep getting this message every time you change the reading language or for it to not show this in the future. Confirm your choice by activating the ok button.
7.3: Quick Start: Braille Shortcut Keys

This provides a quick reference list of shortcut keys that can be used while reading a braille title with BARD Mobile. For more detailed orientation and information about various features of the Now Reading tab with braille titles, start with Section 7.4: Orientation for Now Reading Screen.

Listed below are shortcut keys that can be used from a braille display that includes a braille or Bluetooth keyboard. They are usable from any point on the Now Reading screen when a braille book or magazine is displayed.

The commands are presented in the following order:

  1. Name/purpose of command. Each new command starts with a bullet to the left of the name/purpose.
  2. The shortcut key that has to be typed, which can be a letter or symbol. If the shortcut key needs the feature turned on from BARD Mobile’s settings before it will work, an asterisk appears to the left of the shortcut.
  3. The braille dots that represent the shortcut key, which appear after the shortcut in parenthesis).
  4. Summary of the command
    • Menu (Navigation points) m (Dots 1-3-4) Cycles through the options available on the Jump by menu, such as search keyword, line, volume, page, and bookmark. The first press of the menu key reports the current setting. Subsequent presses cycle through the available options.
    • Previous navigation p or ‘ (Dots 1-2-3-4 or dot 3) Moves to the previous instance of the currently selected navigation point. For example, if Navigation is set to page, then this shortcut moves to the previous page.
    • Next navigation n or , (Dots 1-3-4-5 or dot 6) Moves to the next instance of the currently selected navigation point. For example, if Navigation is set to page, then this shortcut moves to the next page.
    • Volume Navigation v (Dots 1-2-3-6) Moves to the Navigation screen with Volume selected. You are no longer on the Now Reading screen and shortcuts do not work until you return to Now Reading.
    • Page Navigation a (Dot 1) Moves to the Navigation screen with the Page selected. You are no longer on the Now Reading screen and shortcuts do not work until you return to Now Reading.
    • Bookmark Navigation k (Dots 1-3) Moves to the Navigation screen with the Bookmark selected. You are no longer on the Now Reading screen and shortcuts do not work until you return to Now Reading.
    • Where am I? w (Dots 2-4-5-6) Displays the current volume, page, and line number.
    • Search s (Dots 2-3-4) Moves to the search box. The edit box is ready for braille entry and the braille keyboard is used for typing the search term instead of shortcuts. Type the search keyword(s) exactly as it appears in the book.
    • Bookmark b (Dots 1-2) Activates the Bookmark button on the Now Reading screen to add or remove bookmarks.
    • Reading area r (Dots 1-2-3-5) Moves to the reading area showing the current line of the braille book.
    • Last Reading Position p; (Dots 1-2-3) Moves the focus to the “stored last” reading position.
    • Skips to next non- blank line t (Dots 2-3-4-5) Moves to the next line with content.
    • Toggle Braille reflow on and off *d (Dots 1-4-5) Toggles the reflowing of the braille text on and off. The default is 40 cells. To reflow to a display length shorter than 40 cells, the desired length must be configured in settings.
    • Toggle Braille autoscroll on and off */ (Dots 3-4) Toggles the automatic scrolling of the braille text on and off. The default is off. To activate this feature, turn Braille autoscroll on in settings.
    • Increase Braille autoscroll speed *+ (Dots 3-4-6) This key sequence increases the braille autoscroll speed. Note that autoscroll must first be activated in the settings. Changes made to braille autoscroll is not saved for each title when the app is closed.
    • Decrease Braille autoscroll speed *- (Dots 3-6) Decrease the Braille autoscroll speed. Note that autoscroll must first be activated in the settings. Changes made to Braille autoscroll for each title is not saved when the app is closed.
7.4: Orientation for Now Reading Screen

When the Now Reading tab is selected, and a braille title is opened, the main screen for Now Reading is divided into three distinct sections:

  1. The section above the reading area (includes the action bar, tab bar, title bar and search tool bar)
  2. The reading area itself (includes the current title)
  3. The section below the reading area (includes the status and reading controls).

Since there are many features within the three sections, a more in-depth orientation of the Now Reading screen follows:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The title in the middle that reads “Now Reading”
    2. The Recently read button (appears visually as an opened book)
    3. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots), on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The tab bar, below the action bar, shows the four tabs with Now Reading selected.
  3. The title of the current book or magazine
  4. The Search toolbar (used to search the current braille title)
  5. The reading area itself (shows the content of the current title)
  6. Book status information (includes volume number, page number, and line number)
  7. The Navigation button
  8. Bookmark
  9. Jump by various navigation points (includes Previous, the Jump by menu, and Next)
  10. Scroll/Stop button and Autoscroll Time per Character slider (when Braille autoscroll is enabled).

Figure 46: Now Reading Braille

7.5: Recently Read

Recently read allows you to view and continue reading recently read titles on your Android device. This includes both audio and braille. If a recently read title is deleted, then it will not be included.

7.5.1: View Recently Read

To view Recently read from the Now Reading screen, activate the Recently read button from the action bar near the top right.

7.5.2: Working With Recently Read

Recently read lists titles with the most recent on top. Each item includes the title and audio or braille icon to identify the format. Choosing a title will exit this screen and open that title in the now reading screen.

To exit Recently read without choosing another title, activate the navigate up button on the top left.

Figure 47: Recently read braille

7.6: The Title of the Current Book or Magazine

Below the tab bar is the title information, which shows the title of the book or magazine you are currently reading.

7.7: Search Bar

Below the title is the search bar. By default, the search bar includes the search edit field.

7.7.1: Searching within a Braille Title

Activating the search edit field or typing the s shortcut key puts you into edit mode. Once in edit mode, the braille keyboard is shown allowing you to use your braille display to type in your search term. Type the search keyword(s) exactly as it appears in the book.

After typing the keyword(s), press enter (dot 8), or navigate to and activate the go button. The reading position moves to the line of the book matching the search. If the search keyword(s) cannot be found, a double beep sound plays, and your focus will go back to the current location of your book.

7.7.2: Search Previous and Next

After successfully performing a search, search for the previous occurrence of the same search keyword(s) by activating the Previous button or typing p, and the next occurrence by activating the Next button or typing n. If the Jump by menu was changed to another navigation point, then it is necessary to first activate the Jump by menu button or type m to get to “jump by search keyword” before activating Previous or Next. For more information about the Jump by menu, go to Section 7.10: Jump by Various Navigation Points.

7.8: Navigation

Below the reading area is the Book Status information such as volume number, page number, and line number. You can read this information on the braille display at any time by typing w. To the right of the status information is the navigation button. Activating the Navigation button opens the Navigation screen which provides you an alternate way to navigate through the contents of the current title.

7.8.1: Orientation of the Navigation Screen

The navigation screen has the following Items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:

    1. The Navigate up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)

    2. The title in the middle that reads, “Book Navigation”

  2. The Toolbar to determine the items that appear below the toolbar (includes Volume, Page, and Bookmark; Page is selected by default)
  3. The list of items determined by the toolbar.

Figure 48: Book Navigation screen braille

7.8.2: Navigating by Volume

To navigate to another volume for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. Make sure Volume is selected on the toolbar
  2. The volumes appear below the toolbar
  3. Activate the volume of interest to close the Navigation screen, and the reading position moves to the selected volume.

Alternatively, while in Now Reading, type v to open navigation with volume selected.

Figure 49: Book Navigation braille—volume

7.8.3: Navigating by Page

To go to a specific page for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. When Navigation is first opened, Page is selected by default
  2. Activate the page of interest to close the Navigation screen, and the reading position moves to the selected page,

Alternatively, while in Now Reading, type a to open navigation with page selected.

Figure 50: Book Navigation braille-page

7.8.4: Navigating by Bookmark

These instructions are for accessing bookmarks within Navigation. For detailed information to insert, go to, and remove bookmarks, go to Section 7.9: Bookmark.

To go to a bookmark for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. Select Bookmark on the toolbar
  2. The bookmarks appear below the toolbar. This includes Beginning of Book, any previously added bookmarks, and End of book)
  3. Activate the bookmark of interest to close the Navigation screen (the reading position moves to the selected bookmark).

Alternatively, while in Now Reading, type k to open Navigation with Bookmark selected.

Figure 51: Book Navigation braille–bookmark

7.9: Bookmark

A Bookmark is used to remember reading positions to read at a later time. For example, an interesting passage, or a recipe. Each title has its own bookmarks, and BARD Mobile remembers Bookmarks even if you close the app.

The Bookmark button is used to insert and remove bookmarks. To activate it from the braille display, type b.

7.9.1: Inserting Bookmarks

Selecting the Bookmark button inserts a bookmark at the current reading position.

7.9.2: Going to a Previously Added Bookmark

There are two methods to go to a previously added bookmark.

Method one:

  1. From the Now Reading screen, type m or activate the Jump by menu button until the jump by Bookmark option is chosen.
  2. Type p or dot 3, or activate the Previous button, to go to the previous bookmark. Type n or dot 6, or activate the Next button, to go to the next bookmark.

Method two:

  1. From the Now Reading screen, type k to open the Bookmark navigation screen or activate the Navigation button (located on the top right) and then activate the Bookmark button (located on the right side of the toolbar near the top).
  2. All bookmarks are listed below the toolbar.
  3. Activate the desired bookmark. This exits the navigation screen and moves the reading position to the bookmarked location.
7.9.3: Removing Bookmarks

To remove a bookmark while reading a book, go to the desired bookmark using one of the methods provided in section 7.9.2: Going to A Previously Added Bookmark, and then type b or activate the Bookmark button.

7.10: Jump by Various Navigation Points

Below the Bookmark button are three buttons to allow you to go to various navigation points. These buttons appear in the following order from left to right:

  1. Previous
  2. Jump by menu
  3. Next

The Jump by menu button is described first because it affects the behavior of the Previous and Next buttons.

7.10.1: Jump by Menu

The Jump by Menu button is located between the Previous and Next buttons. Activating the Jump by menu or typing m cycles through and selects the different types of navigation. Some possible examples include page, volume, bookmark, search keyword, and line. Once you locate the desired navigation, type p or dot 3, or n or dot 6, or use the Previous and Next buttons to move to the previous and next navigation points.

7.10.2: Previous and Next Navigation

Located to the left of the Jump by menu button is the Previous button, and to the right of the Jump by menu button is the Next button.

Once you locate the desired navigation by typing m or with the Jump by menu button, type p or dot 3, or activate the Previous button, to go to the previous navigation point. Type n or dot 6, or activate the Next button, to go to the next navigation point.

7.11: Braille Reflow

Braille reflow allows BARD Mobile to tailor line lengths to match your braille display size, breaking lines only when necessary (e.g., at the ends of paragraphs), to provide a smoother reading experience.

Braille reflow is off by default. To use Braille reflow, it must be turned on in Braille and Display settings under Settings. Once turned on, select the desired display length using the picker item.

The shortcut key d toggles Braille reflow on and off. When turned on, text reflows to the value selected in Settings. If no value is selected in Settings, typing d sets braille reflow to the default setting of 40 cells.

When Braille reflow is active, paragraphs are indented as usual, page numbers are correctly positioned, and other items, such as lists of ingredients in a recipe, are properly formatted. Braille reflow is an automatic process. Therefore, there may be occasional instances when the reflow is incorrect.

7.12: Braille Autoscroll

Autoscroll allows you to read a braille book with minimal hand movement. When Braille autoscroll is activated, the braille display advances automatically. A fixed amount of time is set for the display of each line, and an additional small amount of time is set for the display of each character on a line, so the app displays short lines for a shorter time than longer lines.

You can start and stop autoscrolling with the / (slash, dots 3-4) key and increase/decrease the rate at which the display advances with the + (plus, dots 3-4-6) and – (minus, dots 3-6) keys respectively. Each press of the (+) or (-) keys changes the autoscroll Time per Character value by 5 milliseconds and has no effect on the autoscroll Time per Line value.

The speed can be changed while autoscrolling is active or inactive but not while it is disabled.

When Braille autoscroll is enabled, an additional button appears on the Now Reading screen, labeled “Scroll” and “Stop.” Activating this button begins automatic scrolling, and activating it again stops autoscrolling. This behavior is analogous to starting and stopping audio playback when reading an audiobook.

When Braille autoscroll is enabled, the speed slider also appears. Adjusting this slider varies the autoscroll Time per Character setting. You can adjust both the time per line and the time per character from the Braille and Display settings screen. These values can only be adjusted if autoscroll is on.

Note that changing autoscroll speed with the plus and minus buttons only changes it until you switch to a new book or removes BARD Mobile from the app switcher and restarts it. Use the controls on the Settings screen for the changes to be permanent.

Section 8: Tips and Hints

This section provides various tips and hints for using BARD Mobile for Android.

8.1: TalkBack Tips

All Tips under 8.1 provide instructions with the assumption that TalkBack is enabled.

8.1.1: Audio Titles

The below tips assume you are in the Now Reading tab with an opened audio title:

8.1.1.1: Gesture to Quickly Start and Stop Playback

Once an audio title is opened in Now Reading, and you press the play button to start playback, you can quickly activate the Play/Stop button by performing a two-finger double tap. This gesture can be performed without the need to first locate the Play/Stop button.

If Background playback is on and an audio title is opened in the Now Reading tab, you can also use the two finger double tap gesture to play or stop the audio even when you are away from the BARD Mobile app. For more information about Background playback, go to Section 5.3.3: Background Playback.

The two-finger double tap gesture to play or stop the audio is a standard TalkBack feature. Using it to play audio for BARD Mobile only works if the most recently playing audio is with BARD Mobile.

8.1.1.2: Rewind and Fast Forward

To rewind or fast forward 5 seconds at a time, Double tap the Rewind or Fast forward buttons. To quickly move by a larger amount of time, double tap and hold the Rewind or Fast forward buttons, then release on the desired spoken amount (20 seconds, 1 minute, 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, etc.).

8.1.1.3: Adjust Current Reading Position

When focused on the progress bar slider, adjust the current reading position by 5 percent with a one-finger flick up or down.

8.1.1.4: Sleep Timer

While on the Sleep timer button, perform a one-finger double tap to set the sleep timer to 15 minutes. Perform additional one-finger double taps in quick succession to cycle through and select the additional options.

8.1.2: Braille Titles

If you are having problems with typing the shortcut keys provided in Section 7.3: Quick Start: Braille Shortcut Keys, go to Section 7.2: Requirements for Reading Braille titles for BARD Mobile to confirm those requirements are satisfied.

If the Android device does not support North American Braille Computer Code, then you cannot read braille books on BARD Mobile even if you are able to connect the braille display.

8.2: General Tips

These are general tips and will work while using BARD Mobile with and without TalkBack.

8.2.1: Pause Downloading Titles

If you want to pause a title from downloading, quickly select the name of the title again on the Search Results screen.

8.2.2: Undo Previous or Next Navigation

While playing an audio title, if you unintentionally move forward or backward by selecting the Next or Previous button, it is possible to “undo” this action and return to the original place. To do this, select the button of the opposite action within 5 seconds. For example, if you are reading Chapter 4, and accidentally select the Next button and jump to Chapter 5, within 5 seconds select the Previous button to return to the previous location within Chapter 4.

8.3: Helpful Links
  • TalkBack Gestures
    Use TalkBack gestures External
  • Instructions for connecting a braille display to an Android device
    Connect to a braille display - Android Accessibility Help External
  • Braille commands. These are braille commands from Google for general use while working with a braille device and TalkBack. They are not the same as the shortcut keys provided in Section 7.3: Braille Shortcut Keys.
    Use braille keystrokes - Android Accessibility Help External

Section 1: Getting Started with BARD Mobile

Welcome to the Braille and Audio Reading Download (BARD) Mobile app User Guide for Fire Tablets. This app allows BARD users to browse, download, and manage braille and audio content using a Fire Tablet from Amazon. Users can also read audio titles.

Although the BARD Mobile app also supports reading braille titles, the Fire Tablet only supports a small list of legacy braille displays. This means that the NLS eReaders and other newer displays are not supported. To read more about this list of older devices go to this Amazon article written in 2018: Use a Braille Display with Your Fire Tablet External. As a result of these limitations, reading braille titles with a connected display is not covered by this user guide at this time.

1.1: Different Ways to Get the BARD Mobile User Guide

There are several ways to access this User Guide:

  • Within the BARD Mobile app
  • On the web
  • From an electronic braille file
1.1.1: Accessing the User Guide within the BARD Mobile App

To read the User Guide conveniently located within the BARD Mobile app:

  1. Locate and select the Bookshelf tab.
  2. From the Bookshelf screen, Activate User Guide to open it.
1.1.2: Accessing the User Guide on the Web

To access the BARD Mobile User Guide on the web:

  1. From a device with Internet access, open a web browser such as Microsoft Edge, Google Chrome, Safari, etc.
  2. Open the web page for the BARD Mobile Fire User Guide.
  3. Consider saving or bookmarking the User Guide for future reference.
1.1.3: Accessing the User Guide from an Electronic Braille File

To download the BARD Mobile User Guide in braille:

  1. From a device with Internet access, open a web browser such as Microsoft Edge, Google Chrome, Safari, etc.
  2. Open the web page Sign up for BARD and Accessing BARD.
  3. Find and activate the link for BARD Mobile User Manual for Fire Tablets in electronic braille [BRF].
1.2: Making the Most of the BARD Mobile User Guide

Consider the following to help navigate the BARD Mobile User Guide more efficiently:

1.2.1: Settings App vs Settings Tab

Throughout this User Guide, you will be asked to make changes in both the Settings app and the Settings tab. The Settings app is a separate app that is part of your Fire Tablet. Although the Settings app has many options, this User Guide will only discuss options that affect the BARD Mobile App. The Settings tab is part of the BARD Mobile app and has options specific to BARD Mobile.

1.2.2: Check the Settings Tab if the App Doesn’t Match the User Guide

While navigating the BARD Mobile app, if some results do not match all the information from the User Guide, you may have made changes in the Settings tab. The User Guide generally documents information with default settings. It is recommended to check the Settings tab or read Section 5: Settings.

1.2.3: Read Notes for VoiceView Users

Throughout this User Guide, there are notes for VoiceView users providing additional information about VoiceView in relation to that portion of the guide. To help you locate these notes, they are inside the * (asterisk) symbols.

1.3: Access to BARD

Use of BARD Mobile is restricted to eligible NLS patrons who have a BARD account. Individuals and institutions that are currently not patrons of NLS should read about the NLS eligibility requirements and learn about signing up for NLS services. Eligible NLS patrons with a BARD account can use the BARD Mobile for Fire Tablets app. For those who do not have a BARD account, contact your local Library.

The minimum requirement for BARD Mobile to operate on a Fire Tablet is Fire OS 7. To download materials, connection to Wi-Fi or cellular data is necessary.

1.4: Creating a Password for the First Time Using the BARD Website

New BARD members must create a password following these instructions:

  1. During the process of signing up for BARD, an active email address is required. Be sure to provide the email address correctly because an email containing log-in information and other valuable resources will be sent to that address.
  2. Use the link provided in the email to go to the BARD website. When this link is activated, the default browser will open and the page for creating a password will load.
  3. After reading the rules listed on the web site to create a new password, type the password in the first input field, then confirm it by typing the password a second time in the password confirmation field.
  4. Activate the Submit button to log in to the BARD website with the newly created password.
  5. Read and agree to the reading pledge if asked. If successful, a page with the title BARD Main Page will open. Be sure to note the email address along with the newly created password since these will be needed to sign in to BARD Mobile.
1.5: Operating BARD Mobile with VoiceView and Screen Magnifier

BARD Mobile is accessible to blind and low-vision patrons when used in conjunction with various features found in Accessibility Settings of the Fire Tablet. Two of these include the VoiceView screen reader and Screen Magnifier, two built-in features of Fire OS.

1.5.1: Operating BARD Mobile with the VoiceView Screen Reader

VoiceView provides speech and braille feedback for blind or low-vision users of Fire Tablets. While the primary navigation method for using a Fire Tablet is with gestures, there are notable differences when using gestures with and without VoiceView.

When using VoiceView with gestures, a one-finger single tap does not activate the item; it places focus on that item. This is because a blind or low-vision user needs to know the location of the item before activating it. When a VoiceView user is ready to activate an item, the gesture is a one-finger double tap. Throughout this User Guide, there will be instructions to activate various items. This means that those who are not using VoiceView will perform a one-finger single tap on the item while VoiceView users will need to first find the item, and then perform a one-finger double tap.

In addition to locating an item with a one-finger single tap to place focus on a specific item, VoiceView users can then navigate to the previous item with a one-finger flick left, or next item with a one-finger flick right anywhere on the screen. This means if you performed a one-finger single tap near the desired item but not on the exact location, you can perform another one-finger single tap until the item is located. However, if you already know the screen and understand the desired item is the next one, then a one-finger flick right is also an option.

Although the VoiceView screen reader provides many useful options and tools, beginner users will need to take time to read and learn this feature. While this User Guide will provide notes and tips for VoiceView specifically with the BARD Mobile app, it is recommended to use the below links along with additional links within these web pages to help improve efficiency when using VoiceView in general:

To activate VoiceView on a Fire Tablet, press the power button 3 times in quick successions while the tablet is turned on. You can also go to Using your Fire tablet: Turn the VoiceView screen reader on and off External. To learn more about using the VoiceView screen reader on a Fire Tablet, go to: Guide to VoiceView Screen Reader for Fire Tablet External.

1.5.2: Operating BARD Mobile with Screen Magnifier

Screen Magnifier benefits many low-vision users by providing the ability to increase the screen size.

To learn more about magnification options, and Screen Magnifier basics, go to: Using your Fire Tablet: Get to know the Screen Magnifier External.

1.6: BARD Mobile Installation and Login

Use the following instructions to install and log in to BARD Mobile for the first time:

  1. To install BARD Mobile, open the App Store on your Fire Tablet.
  2. Locate and activate the Search button near the top right of the screen.
  3. Locate and activate the search field near the top of the screen and search for BARD Mobile.
  4. Find and activate BARD Mobile in the results and then activate the download button.
  5. When the download is complete, press the Open button. This opens the BARD Mobile app and the screen to read and agree to the BARD End User Agreement will appear.
  6. Once you activate the agree button, the BARD login screen will appear.
  7. Enter the email address associated with your BARD account in the User Name text field.
  8. Enter the password you previously created on the BARD website in the password text field. If you would like to confirm your password, you can check the show password check box.
  9. After the show password checkbox, there is a link that informs you that by continuing, you agree to the terms of use. Activating the link will allow you to read the terms.
  10. There is also a forgot your password link that lets you reset your password if needed.
  11. Once you have typed in your email address and password, find and activate the Login button.
  12. When successfully logged in, focus will be on the Get Books screen. It is now time to download your first book or magazine!
Section 2: An Overview of BARD Mobile

This section provides an overview of BARD Mobile to help you get a general understanding of the app.

2.1: Orientation of the BARD Mobile App

When the BARD Mobile app is opened, the screen is divided into five areas. The areas from top to bottom are:

  1. The Status Bar at the top (This is a Fire Tablet feature)
  2. The Action Bar (Also known as the App Bar)
  3. The content of the currently selected tab (This takes up most of the screen)
  4. The tab bar with four tabs allowing you to choose a tab and confirm the currently selected tab
  5. The Navigation Bar at the bottom (This is a Fire Tablet feature)
2.2: The Status Bar

The status bar, located at the top of the screen, provides information about the Fire Tablet such as time, and Wi-Fi connection.

The Status bar is not part of the BARD Mobile interface. It is a feature of the Fire Tablet. Since it appears at the very top of the screen when BARD Mobile is opened, this is mentioned here to provide background information and orientation.

2.3: The Action Bar

The Action bar is below the Status bar and contains the following items from left to right:

  1. The Navigate up button (appears visually as a left arrow)
  2. The title of the current screen
  3. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots)
2.3.1: The Navigate up button

The Navigate up button will not appear on the first screen for each tab. If you choose something from the content area that opens another screen, the Navigate up button will appear on the left side of the Action bar to allow you to navigate back to the previous screen.

2.3.2: The Title

Located in the middle of the action bar, the title displays the name of the current screen. For example, if you select bookshelf, the title will show “Bookshelf”. In addition, if you then select the Audio Books folder from the Bookshelf screen, then the title will show, “Audio Books Folder.”

2.3.3: The More Options Button

The More Options button on the right side of the Action bar allows you to get help for the current screen. It may also present additional options for specific screens. For example, when in the Audio Books folder, it will also include Create folder, Rename folder, Delete, and Move.

2.4: The Content of the Currently Selected Tab

Below the action bar is the content of the currently selected tab. This takes up most of the screen. The available information depends on which of the four tabs on the tab bar, located below the content, is selected.

2.5: The Tab Bar

The tab bar is located below the Action bar and contains the four tabs of the BARD Mobile app. The tabs from left to right are:

  1. Bookshelf
  2. Get Books
  3. Settings
  4. Now Reading

Use the tab bar to choose a different tab to display on the main content area or to find which tab is currently selected.

*Note for VoiceView users: While using speech, when navigating through the tab bar, VoiceView will indicate the currently selected tab by saying the word selected in front of the tab that is chosen. For example, “Selected, Bookshelf tab”. In addition to selecting the desired tab, you can quickly go to the previous and next tab with three-finger flicks. A three-finger flick left moves focus to the next tab, and a three-finger flick right moves to the previous one.*

2.5.1: Bookshelf

Bookshelf is the first of four tabs on the tab bar. It is used to view and manage downloaded content. All downloaded books, along with this User Guide, can be found within the bookshelf. Titles are divided into the following:

  1. Audio Books
  2. Audio Magazines
  3. Braille Books
  4. Braille Magazines
  5. User Guide (Opens this User Guide)

To read more about Bookshelf and how to use it, go to Section 3: Bookshelf.

2.5.2: Get Books

Get Books is the second of four tabs on the tab bar. It is used to search, browse, manage, and download content on BARD. Available options from Get Books include:

  1. Search
  2. Wish List
  3. Recently Added to BARD
  4. Most Popular Books
  5. Browse Magazines
  6. Previous Downloads
  7. Search Music

To read more about Get Books and how to work with it, go to Section 4: Get Books.

2.5.3: Settings

Settings is the third of four tabs on the tab bar. It is used to view and customize various settings for the BARD Mobile app. The main screen of Settings includes the following:

  1. Audio settings
  2. Braille and Display settings
  3. User Account settings
  4. Device Accessibility settings

To read more about Settings, go to Section 5: Settings.

2.5.4: Now Reading

Now Reading is the last of four tabs on the tab bar. If the Now Reading tab is selected without an opened title, you will get the following message:

“No book is currently selected to play. Please select a book from the Bookshelf to play.”

To open a title, locate a book or magazine from the Bookshelf, or locate and download a title from Get Books, then activate the Read button, automatically moving you to the Now Reading tab. When a title is opened from Bookshelf or Get books, the Now Reading tab is automatically selected and focus is on the main content area of the Now Reading tab. Once a book is opened, you can select another tab from the tab bar and can also use the tab bar to select and return to the opened title within the Now Reading tab.

When the Now Reading tab is selected, and a book is opened, the content area above the tab bar serves as a player for the currently opened book. Some controls are only available when audio content is opened while others are only for braille content.

Some available controls when audio content is opened include Sleep timer, Jump by chapter, Rewind, Play/stop, Fast forward, and Adjust speed and tone. To read more about working with the Now Reading screen when audio content is opened, go to Section 6: Reading Audio Titles.

Note: Although the BARD Mobile app also supports reading braille titles, the Fire Tablet only supports a small list of legacy braille displays. This means that the NLS eReaders and other newer displays are not supported. To read more about this list of older devices go to this Amazon article written in 2018: Use a Braille Display with Your Fire Tablet External. As a result of these limitations, reading braille titles with a connected display will not be covered by this user guide at this time.

2.6: The Navigation Bar

The Navigation Bar, located at the bottom of the screen, provides device-specific navigation tools such as Back button, Home button, and Overview button or app switcher. Depending on the Fire OS software version you are running, you will either get the overview button or the app switcher.
