The Navigation bar is not part of the BARD Mobile interface. It is a feature of the Fire Tablet. Since it can appear at the very bottom of the screen when BARD Mobile is opened, this is mentioned here to provide background information and orientation.

Section 3: Bookshelf

The Bookshelf is used to view and manage downloaded content. All downloaded audio and braille books and magazines, along with this User Guide, can be found within the Bookshelf.

3.1: Accessing the Bookshelf

To access the Bookshelf, do the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the bottom of the screen.
  2. Find Bookshelf, which is the first of four tabs and is located on the left.
  3. If Bookshelf is already selected, the Bookshelf screen is already shown below the tab bar.
  4. If it is not selected, activate the Bookshelf tab to select it. The Bookshelf screen will appear on the content area above the tab bar.
3.2: Bookshelf Quick Start

This Section provides quick instructions on how to read and delete titles. For more detailed orientation and information about the various screens of the Bookshelf tab, start reading from Section 3.3: The Bookshelf Screen.

3.2.1: Reading Audio Titles

To locate an audio title from the Bookshelf and start reading it, do the following:

  1. While on the Bookshelf screen, choose one of the first two categories (Audio Books, or Audio Magazines).
  2. When the screen opens, navigate to a title of interest, and activate the title name. Once the title is chosen, you are automatically moved to the Now Reading tab, away from the Bookshelf, with player controls allowing you to read the title.
3.2.2: Deleting Titles

To delete a title from the Bookshelf, do the following:

  1. While in the Bookshelf screen, choose one of the first four categories (Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines).
  2. When the screen opens, activate the More options button on the top right.
  3. From the pop-up window that appears, activate delete.
  4. A list of titles with checkboxes will appear. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title, author’s last name or recently read.
  5. Choose a title or folder to check or uncheck for deletion.
  6. Activate the Ok button to delete items that are checked.
  7. You will be asked if you are sure you want to delete, and if a folder is selected, all books in the folder will be deleted. Choose yes to delete.
3.3: The Bookshelf Screen

The Bookshelf screen shows these items in the following order:

  1. Action bar on the top, which has the following two items:
    1. The title that reads “Bookshelf” in the middle
    2. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots) on the right side, which allows you to get help for the current screen.
  2. Audio Books
  3. Audio Magazines
  4. Braille Books
  5. Braille Magazines
  6. User Guide (opens this User Guide)
  7. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, (shows the four tabs with Bookshelf selected).

*Note for VoiceView users: When navigating to different categories, VoiceView will first indicate the name of the category followed by the number of items within that category. For example: “Audio Books 11”.*

Figure 1: Bookshelf screen

3.4: The Current Category Screen

This screen appears if you select one of the first four categories from the Bookshelf screen (Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines). While the results will be different depending on the chosen category, the screen is very similar. Therefore, this orientation for the Current Category screen applies to all four categories.

  1. The Action bar on the top, which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate up button (appears visually as a left arrow), on the left side, goes back to the previous screen
    2. The title in the middle with the name of the current folder such as “Audio Books”
    3. The More options button on the right side, lets you get access to additional choices for the current title.
  2. The Search bar (used to search titles within the current category)
  3. Sorting buttons (by Title, Author, or Latest)
  4. The list of titles (either all items for the current category or results matching the search)
  5. The List of folders you’ve created
  6. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Bookshelf selected.

Figure 2: Audio Books screen within the Bookshelf tab in BARD Mobile can be sorted by Title, Author and Latest.

3.4.1: Search by Title and Author

Below the tab bar is the search field. This is used to search the chosen category by either title or author. To search within the current list, do the following:

  1. Activate the search field. An onscreen keyboard should appear on the bottom half of the display. An exception to this is if an external keyboard or braille device is connected, since you are expected to use that instead.
  2. Start typing either the title or the author’s last name. Alternatively, press and hold the dictation button from the onscreen keyboard. As information is typed, the list of titles below the search and sorting options will change to match the search.
  3. When the search field is activated, there is a cancel button to the right of the search field.

*Note for VoiceView users: When the edit field is activated and the onscreen keyboard appears, if you want to dictate text, from anywhere, perform a two-finger double tap and hold, say what you want, and then release your two-fingers.*

Figure 3: Screenshot of search results for audio books

3.4.2: Sort the List

Below the search field are three buttons allowing you to sort titles. The three sorting options are:

  1. Title (alphabetical order by title, selected by default)
  2. Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)
  3. Latest (sorted by date when you accessed the titles, with most recent on top)

*Note for VoiceView users: When navigating to one of these three buttons, VoiceView will indicate the currently chosen option by saying the word selected before the name of the button with speech.*

3.4.3: The List of Titles

Below the three sorting buttons is the list of downloaded titles for the chosen category. By default, this list contains all titles from the currently chosen category. However, if you perform a search by title and author using the search field above the three sorting buttons, then the list will only contain the matching results. In addition, the order of the titles on this list can be sorted with the three buttons above the list.

While in the list of titles, each title may include the following information:

  1. Title name
  2. Author or authors
  3. Total reading time (audio only)
  4. Number of volumes (braille only)
  5. More Info button

Activating the More Info button will open the details screen, allowing you to select additional options for that title.

3.4.4: The List of Folders

If you’ve previously created folders within the current category, they will appear below the list of titles. Activating one of these folders will open another screen with the folder content. The new screen is similar to the screen that shows titles for a specific category. This means you can search, sort, download, read, and work with the list of titles.

3.5: More Options for the Current Category

When you choose one of the four categories from the bookshelf screen, activating the More options button on the right side of the Action bar will open a pop-up window with the following choices:

  1. Create Folder
  2. Rename Folder
  3. Delete
  4. Move
  5. Help (get help for the current screen)

Figure 4: Screenshot of audio books category with more options popup opened.

3.5.1: Create Folder

You can create folders to help you organize your titles within each of the categories. For example, you may want to have a mystery folder and a science fiction folder within Audio Books.

You can create a folder by doing the following:

To change the name of a folder you previously created, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the bottom left of the screen.
  2. From the Bookshelf screen, Open the category where you want to create the folder.
  3. Once you are in the location where you want to create the folder, activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate Create Folder.
  5. Activate the edit field to bring up the onscreen keyboard.
  6. Type or dictate the name of the folder you want and activate Ok to create the folder.
    Figure 5: Screenshot for typing in a new folder name
3.5.2: Rename Folder

To change the name of a folder you previously created, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the top left of the screen.
  2. Open the category that has the folder you want to rename.
  3. Once you are in the screen with that folder, activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate Rename Folder.
  5. Check the box for the folder you want to rename and activate ok. Figure 6: Screenshot of Rename a folder
  6. Activate the edit field to bring up the onscreen keyboard.
    Figure 7: Screenshot of choosing titles or folders to delete
  7. Type or dictate the new name you want and activate Ok to rename the folder.
3.5.3: Delete Titles and Folders

To delete previously downloaded titles or folders you’ve created, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the bottom left of the screen.
  2. Open the category that has the titles or folders you want to delete.
  3. Once you are in the correct location, Activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate delete. A list of titles and folders (if you previously created folders) with checkboxes will appear. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title or author’s last name or by recently read.
    Figure 8: Screenshot of a title to move with checkbox
  5. Choose a title or folder to check or uncheck for deletion.
  6. Activate the Ok button to delete items that are checked. You will be asked if you are sure you want to delete, and if a folder is selected, all books in the folder will be deleted. Activate yes to delete.
3.5.4: Move Titles and Folders

To move previously downloaded titles or folders you’ve created to another location, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the bottom left of the screen.
  2. From the Bookshelf screen , Open the category that has the titles or folders you want to move.
  3. Once you are in the correct location, activate the More options button on the right side of the Action bar.
  4. From the pop-up window, activate move. A list of titles with checkboxes will appear. You can search by title or author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by title, author’s last name or by recently read.
    Figure 9: Screenshot of moving items checkboxes
  5. Check titles or folders that you want to move.
  6. Activate the Ok button.
  7. Choose a location where you want the checked items to go. If you want them to go to the main level of Audio Books, Braille Books, Audio Magazines, or Braille Magazines, activate one of these choices. If you want the checked items to go into another folder, first expand the category that contains that folder, then activate the folder.
  8. You will then be asked if you want to move the items to the folder you chose. Activate yes to complete the process.
3.6: The Details Screen

The Details screen is used to get additional information or take additional actions for a specific title.

3.6.1: Accessing the Details Screen

To access the Details screen, do the following:

  1. Select the Bookshelf tab from the tab bar located near the bottom left of the screen.
  2. Choose one of the first four categories from the Bookshelf screen.
  3. Activate the More Info button next to the desired title.
3.6.2: Orientation of the Details Screen

In addition to the usual items on the action bar near the top, and tab bar near the bottom, the Details screen has the following items:

  • Title name
  • Author name (if available)
  • Annotation (books only)
  • Expand annotation button (appears visually as a plus sign,books only)
  • Narrator (audio only)
  • Total time (audio only)
  • Number of volumes (braille only)
  • Book number
  • List of related subjects (books only)
  • Series (if the title belongs to a series)
  • Order in series (if the title belongs to a series and there is an order)
  • Last downloaded (gives the date of the last time this was downloaded)
  • Read button (moves into the Now Reading tab, and away from the Bookshelf tab, to read the title)

Figure 10: Screenshot of more information opened

3.6.3: More Options Pop-up for Details

While in the Details screen, activating the More options button on the right side of the action bar will open a pop-up window that has the following choices:

  • Read button (moves into the Now Reading tab, and away from the Bookshelf tab, to read the title)
  • All books by author button (opens a screen with the list of books by a particular author. There may be multiple buttons if the book has multiple authors).
  • All books in subject button (opens a screen with the list of books matching the subject of the book in focus, and there may be multiple buttons if the book belongs to multiple subjects).
  • All books in series button (opens a screen with the list of books in that series, only available for books that belong to a series, and there may be multiple buttons if the book belongs to multiple series).
  • Subscribe to or unsubscribe from series button (only available for books that belong to a series)
  • Subscribe to or unsubscribe from magazine button (magazines only)
  • Help (get help for the current screen)

The All books buttons (including all books by author, all books in subject, and all books in series) open another screen. These screens are similar to the screen that shows titles for a specific category. This means you can search, sort, download, read, and work with the list of titles.

If a book has multiple authors, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books by each author. The button for the primary author will be first.

If a book belongs to multiple subjects, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books in each subject. The buttons for the subjects are in alphabetical order.

Figure 11: Screenshot of the More Options pop-up window for the Details screen

3.6.4: Expanding and Collapsing the Annotation

When in the Details screen for a book, the Expand Annotation button appears visually as a plus sign. Once the annotation is expanded, it is maximized, which means the annotation takes up the majority of the screen. When the annotation is expanded, there is a collapse annotation button above the annotation (appears visually as a minus sign) to let you collapse the annotation again.

Figure 12: Screenshot for expanded annotation

Section 4: Get Books

Use the functions from the Get Books tab to search, browse, download, and manage content from BARD. An Internet connection is required to use the Get Books Tab.

4.1: Accessing the Get Books Tab

To access the Get Books tab, do the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the bottom of the screen.
  2. Find Get Books, which is the second of four tabs from the left. If Get Books is selected, the Get Books screen appears above the tab bar.
  3. If it is not selected, activate the Get Books tab to select it. The Get Books screen will appear above the tab bar.
4.2: Get Books Quick Start

The following sections provide quick instructions to use the seven features on the Get Books screen to search, browse, download, and manage titles. For more detailed orientation and information about the various features of the Get Books tab, start reading Section 4.3: The Get Books Screen.

4.2.1: Search

To search and download a title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Search
  2. When the Search screen opens, activate the search edit field. Using the onscreen keyboard, type the text you want or dictate with the dictation option.
  3. Review the available search types, formats, and / or languages below the search bar.
  4. To finalize the search, activate the Go button from the search bar. Alternatively, activate the search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.
  5. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. The default view is relevant to the Search term; however, it is possible to change the sorting order by title, author, or latest using the buttons above the list of titles.
  6. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  7. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.2: Wish List

The Wish List is used to manage items marked for future reading. To download a title from your Wish List, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Wish List.
  2. When the Wish List opens, read through the titles, or search using the search bar to limit results. The default view is newly added to Wish List on top; however, it is possible to change the sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  3. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  4. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder. Downloaded titles will be automatically deleted from Wish List.
4.2.3: Recently Added to BARD

Recently added to BARD lists books and magazines in the BARD collection in order of when they were added with the most recent titles listed first. To download a title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get books screen, choose Recently Added to BARD.
  2. Choose from Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, and Braille Magazines.
  3. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. Alternatively, search using the search bar to limit results. The default view is newly added on top; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  4. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  5. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.4: Most Popular Books

Most Popular books lists the titles in the collection ordered by most to least downloaded. To browse Most popular books and download a title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Most Popular Books.
  2. Choose from Audio Books and Braille Books.
  3. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. Alternatively, search using the search bar to limit results. The default view is most popular; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  4. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  5. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.5: Browse Magazines

Browse Magazines provides access to an alphabetical list of magazines. To browse magazines, subscribe or unsubscribe to a magazine, or download an issue, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Browse Magazines.
  2. Choose from Audio Magazines and Braille Magazines.
  3. When the audio or braille magazines screen opens, either search for the name of a magazine or read through the list and choose one.
  4. When the Results screen for the chosen magazine opens, subscribe, or unsubscribe to that magazine by using the Subscribe or Unsubscribe button at the top of the screen (to the right of the title).
  5. Alternatively, read through the titles or search using the search bar to limit results.
  6. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  7. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.6: Previous Downloads

This shows a list of previously downloaded titles, to let you download them again on the current device or on another device. To browse and download a previously downloaded title, do the following:

  1. While in the Get Books screen, choose Previous Downloads.
  2. When the Previous Downloads screen opens, read through the titles. Alternatively, search using the search bar to limit results. The default view puts the most recently downloaded title on top; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title or author using the buttons above the list of titles.
  3. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  4. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.7: Search Music

Choosing the Search Music option allows you to search for music books and scores from the NLS Music collection. To search and download music, do the following:

  1. While on the Get Books screen, choose Search Music (the last feature).
  2. When the Search Music screen opens, activate the search edit field. Using the onscreen keyboard, type the text you want or dictate with the dictation option.
  3. Review the available search types and formats below the search bar.
  4. To finalize the search, activate the Go button from the search bar. Alternatively, activate the Search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.
  5. When the Results screen opens, read through the titles. The default view is relevant to the search term; however, it is possible to change sorting order by title, author, or latest using the buttons above the list of titles.
  6. Activate the name of the title you want to read.
  7. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.2.8: Details and More Options

Once you navigate to a list of titles from any of the seven options on the Get Books tab, activate a title to download it. You can also activate the more info button next to a title to open the book details screen to access more information and options for that title.

4.3: The Get Books Screen

The Get Books screen shows these items in the following order:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following two items:
    1. The title in the middle that reads “Get Books”
    2. The More options button (appears visually as three vertical dots), on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. Search (by book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator)
  3. Wish List (manage items marked for future reading)
  4. Recently Added to BARD (books and magazines recently added to BARD)
  5. Most Popular books (most downloaded books)
  6. Browse Magazines (all magazines alphabetically by title)
  7. Previous Downloads (shows previously downloaded titles for the current account)
  8. Search Music (find music books and scores from the music collection)
  9. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.

Figure 13: Screenshot of the Get Books

Note: Sections 4.4 to 4.10 will cover the seven features from the Get Books screen. For explanations about options in common, including the Details screen and the More options button, go to Section 4.11: Options That Apply to all Get Books Features.

4.4: Search

This allows you to perform a BARD search by book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, or narrator. When the Search feature from the Get Books screen is selected, the screen for the Search feature opens and shows the following:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (appears visually as a left arrow, goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Search”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. Search bar (includes search field, go, and cancel)
  3. Search type (includes Keyword, Title, Author, and Annotation)
  4. Format (includes Audio, Braille, and All)
  5. Language (default options are English and Spanish)
  6. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.

Figure 14: Screenshot of search screen

4.4.1: Search Bar

The Search bar is used to add a search term by typing or dictating text. You can then edit, cancel, or finalize the search. By default, the search bar includes the Search field, and the Go button.

4.4.1.1: The Search Field

The search field is used to type, view, and edit a search term.

  • When the Search field is activated, an onscreen keyboard will appear on the bottom half of the display to allow you to start typing or dictating text by pressing and holding the dictation option. If an external keyboard or braille device is connected, the onscreen keyboard may not appear because you are expected to use that instead.

*Note for VoiceView users: When the Edit field is activated and the onscreen keyboard appears, if you want to dictate text, from anywhere, perform a two-finger double tap and hold, say what you want, and then release your two-fingers.*

  • When the Search field is activated, the search bar now shows the search field, the Go button to finalize the search, and the Cancel button.
4.4.1.2: The Go Button

The Go button is used to finalize the search. It is disabled until text is added to the search field. Alternatively, activate the Search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.

4.4.2: Search Type

Located below the search bar, search type determines the search criteria and includes the following:

  1. Search by Keyword (selected by default and includes book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator)
  2. Search by Title
  3. Search by Author (type last name, first name)
  4. Search by Annotation

*Note for VoiceView users: When navigating the buttons to determine search type, VoiceView will indicate the currently chosen option by saying the word selected before the name of the button with speech. This also applies to the buttons for format and language.*

4.4.3: Format

Format determines the format(s) that appear in the search results. Available options include:

  1. Format Audio (selected by default)
  2. Format Braille
  3. Format All (includes audio and braille)
4.4.4: Language

Language determines the language of the titles in the search results. The default options include:

  1. Language English (selected by default)
  2. Language Spanish

The above two options are shown by default; however, languages can be added and removed from within the Settings tab. Select User account settings, then Language preferences. English is always the first option. To read more about adding and removing languages, go to Section 5.6.5: Language Preferences.

4.4.5: Results Screen for Search Feature

In addition to the usual items on the action bar on the top and the tab bar near the bottom, this screen has the following items:

  1. Search bar (includes search field with search term from previous screen and Go button. This is used to modify the search).
  2. Sorting buttons (by Title, Author, or Latest; the default is the one most relevant to the search term on top).
  3. List of titles that match the search (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille if Format All is selected, Total reading time for audio, Number of volumes for braille, Date added to BARD if sorting by Latest, and More Info).

Figure 15: Screenshot of Search results

4.4.5.1: Modifying the Search

If you activate the Search field from the search bar to modify the search term, buttons for Search Type, Format, and Language will appear below the Search bar and above the buttons for sorting. Once you activate the Go button to confirm the search modification, the additional buttons are no longer available.

4.4.5.2: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles by relevance to the search term, with the most relevant on top. Use the three buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)
  3. Sort by Latest (by date when the title was added to BARD, with most recent on top).

Activate one of the above three buttons to change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order by relevance, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.5: Wish List

The Wish List is the second option on the Get Books screen. This is a powerful feature used to keep track of books you are interested in reading in the future.

4.5.1: The Benefits of the Wish List

Benefits of the Wish List include:

  • The Wish List is associated with a specific user’s account. If you are signed into several devices with the same account, items added to the Wish List will appear in the Wish List feature of these devices.
  • Items added to the Wish List can be downloaded at a later time or from a different device.
  • If you subscribe to a book series or magazine, any newly added titles will be automatically added to the Wish List when available.
  • Network librarians also have the ability to add, delete, and rearrange items in your Wish List if requested.
  • Once a title is downloaded, it is automatically deleted from the Wish List. If you want to download the same title on multiple devices, either search for and download the book on another device, or work with the Previous Downloads feature to download the same title again. For more information, see Section 4.9: Previous Downloads.
  • You can delete items from the Wish List if you don’t want to download them anymore.
4.5.2: The Wish List Screen

This screen has the following items:

  1. The Action bar on the top, which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Wish List” along with the number of results.
    3. The More Options button on the right side (allows user to delete from Wish List and get help for the current screen)
  2. The Search bar (used to search the Wish List by title or author)
  3. Sorting buttons (by Title and Author; the default is date added to Wish List, with newest on top)
  4. The list of titles in the Wish List (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille, total reading time for audio, number of volumes for braille, and More Info)
  5. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get books selected
4.5.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the date you added them to the Wish List, with most recent on top. You can also use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of date added to Wish List, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.5.3: The Delete Screen of the Wish List

This screen is opened by activating the More Options button from the Wish List screen, and then choosing delete. It is used to manipulate items from the Wish List. It has a similar orientation to the Wish List screen.

The main difference with this screen is the list of titles has checkboxes to let you mark titles for deletion. It does not have a More Info button, since this screen is used to delete titles.

Figure 16: Screenshot of the Wish List

4.5.3.1: Deleting Titles From the Wish List

To delete titles, do the following:

  1. From the Wish List screen, activate the More Options button on the right side of the action bar at the top.
  2. From the pop-up window that opens, choose delete. This opens a list of titles. You can search by Title or Author to filter the list. You can also sort the list alphabetically by Title or Author’s last name or by Date Added.
  3. Once focused on the list, check the checkboxes of the titles you want to delete.
  4. Activate Ok below the list once you are satisfied with the titles you want to delete.
  5. You will be asked if you are sure you want to delete. Activating yes will delete the titles. Focus will then go back to the main Wish List screen to download titles, not delete titles.
4.6: Recently Added to BARD

Recently Added to BARD is the third feature of Get Books and is used to browse books and magazines recently added to the BARD collection.

4.6.1: Orientation of the Recently Added to BARD Screen

The Recently added to BARD screen shows the following:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Recently Added to BARD”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. Audio Books
  3. Audio Magazines
  4. Braille Books
  5. Braille Magazines
  6. The tab bar, near the bottom, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.

Figure 17: Screenshot of the recently added to BARD screen

4.6.2: Results Screen for Recently Added to BARD

After choosing Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines, the results screen shows the following items below the Action bar and above the Tab bar:

  1. The Search bar (used to search within the current category by title and author)
  2. Buttons to sort the list by Title or Author (default is by date added to BARD, newest on top)
  3. The list of titles (includes title name where you can Activate and Download, Author or Authors, Total reading time for audio, Number of volumes for braille, and More Info)

Figure 18: Screenshot of audio books list for recently added to BARD screen here.

4.6.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the date added to BARD, with the most recent on top. You can also use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to select them and change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of date added to BARD, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.7: Most Popular Books

Most Popular Books is used to browse the most downloaded books.

4.7.1: Orientation of the Most Popular Books Screen

The Most popular books screen shows these items in the following order:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Most Popular Books”
    3. The More Options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. Audio Books
  3. Braille Books
  4. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.

Figure 19: Screenshot of the most popular books screen

4.7.2: The Results Screen for Most Popular Books

The results screen appears when Audio Books or Braille Books is chosen and shows the following below the action bar and above the tab bar:

  1. The Search bar (used to search within the current category by title and author)
  2. Buttons to sort the list by Title or Author (default is by most popular)
  3. The list of titles (includes title name where you can Activate and Download, Author orAuthors, Total reading time for audio, Number of volumes for braille, and More Info)

Figure 20: Screenshot of audio books recently added

4.7.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the most downloaded books recently, with the most popular on top. Use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of most popular, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.8: Browse Magazines

Browse Magazines is used to browse all audio or braille magazines alphabetically by title.

4.8.1: Select a Format Screen

Choosing Browse Magazines will open the screen to select a format. This screen has the following:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Browse Magazines”
    3. The More options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. Audio Magazines
  3. Braille Magazines
  4. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get books selected.

Figure 21: Screenshot of the browse magazine format screen

4.8.2: Choose a Magazine Screen

Choosing a format will open the Choose a Magazine screen. The below orientation applies to both audio and braille magazines and has the following below the Action bar and above the Tab bar:

  1. The Search bar (used to search for a specific magazine)
  2. The list of magazines (in alphabetical order by title)

Figure 22: Screenshot of the list of audio magazines

4.8.3: Manage Chosen Magazine Screen

Once you select a magazine title, the resulting screen displays the following items:

  1. In addition to the usual items on the action bar, between the Title and the More Options button, this screen has a button to Subscribe or Unsubscribe to the current magazine.
  2. The Search bar (used to search for a specific magazine issue)
  3. The list of magazines (in order by newest on top). Activate the name to download.

Figure 23: Screenshot of the manage the chosen magazine screen

4.9: Previous Downloads

The Previous Downloads feature is used to view and manage previously downloaded titles.

4.9.1: Benefits of Previous Downloads

Benefits of Previous Downloads include:

  • Allows you to view a list of previously downloaded titles
  • Downloaded titles include books or magazines that are downloaded on another device using the same user account
  • Allows you to redownload a previously deleted title
  • You will be informed if a specific title is downloaded to the current device when browsing titles.
4.9.2: The Previous Downloads Screen

When Previous downloads from the Get Books screen is selected, the following items are shown:

  1. The Action bar at the top, which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Previous Downloads” along with the number of results
    3. The More Options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. The Search bar (used to search by title or author)
  3. Sorting buttons by Title and Author (default is by date downloaded with newest on top)
  4. The List of titles in Previous Downloads (includes title where you can Activate and Download, Author or Authors, icon for audio or braille, Total reading time for audio, Number of volumes for braille, and More Info)
  5. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get books selected

Figure 24: Screenshot of the Previous Downloads screen showing a few of the 233 previous downloads

4.9.2.1: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles in the order of the downloaded date, with most recent on top. Use the two buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)

Activate one of the above two buttons to change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order of date downloaded from BARD, activate the currently selected button to unselect it.

4.10: Search Music

Search Music is used to find music books and scores from the music collection by series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator.

The Search Music feature has similar functions as those from the Search feature. There are two differences:

  1. Search Music only searches the music collection.
  2. Search Music does not have buttons to filter by language, since music titles do not have an assigned language.

When Search music from the Get Books screen is selected, the following items are shown:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Search Music”
    3. The More Options button on the right side (get help for the current screen)
  2. The Search bar (includes search field, Go, and Cancel)
  3. Search type (includes Keyword, Title, Author, and Annotation)
  4. Format (includes Audio, Braille, and All)
  5. The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get books selected.

Figure 25: Screenshot of the search music screen

4.10.1: The Search Bar

The Search bar is used to add a search term by typing or dictating text. You can then edit, cancel, or finalize the search. By default, the search bar includes the Search field, and the Go button.

4.10.1.1: The Search Field

The Search field is used to type, view, and edit a search term.

  • When the Search field is activated, an onscreen keyboard will appear on the bottom half of the display to allow you to start typing or dictating text by pressing and holding the dictation option. If an external keyboard or braille device is connected, the onscreen keyboard may not appear because you are expected to use that instead.

*Note for VoiceView users: When the Edit field is activated and the onscreen keyboard appears, if you want to dictate text, from anywhere, perform a two-finger double tap and hold, say what you want, and then release your two-fingers.*

  • When the Search field is activated, the Search bar now shows the Search field, the Go button to finalize the search, and the Cancel button.
4.10.1.2: The Go Button

The Go button is used to finalize the search. It is disabled until text is added to the Search field. Alternatively, activate the Search button near the bottom right if the onscreen keyboard is shown.

4.10.2: Search Type

Located below the Search bar, Search type determines the search criteria and includes the following:

  1. Search by Keyword (selected by default and includes book series, title, author, subject, annotation, book number, and narrator)
  2. Search by Title
  3. Search by Author (type last name, first name)
  4. Search by Annotation

*Note for VoiceView users: When navigating the buttons to determine Search type, VoiceView will indicate the currently chosen option by saying the word selected before the name of the button with speech. This also applies to the buttons for format.*

4.10.3: Format

Format determines the format(s) that appear in the search results. Available options include:

  1. Format Audio (selected by default)
  2. Format Braille
  3. Format All (includes audio and braille)
4.10.5: Results Screen for Search Music Feature

In addition to the usual items on the action bar near the top and tab bar near the bottom, this screen has the following items:

  1. The Search bar (includes search field with search term from previous screen, and Go button; this is used to modify the search).
  2. Sorting buttons (by Title, Author, or Latest; the default is the one most relevant to the search term on top)
  3. The List of titles that match the search (includes title name where you can activate and download, author or authors, icon for audio or braille if Format All is selected, Total reading time for audio, Number of volumes for braille, Date added to BARD if sorting by Latest, and More Info)

Figure 26: Screenshot for Search Music results screen

4.10.5.1: Modifying the Search

If you activate the Search field from the Search bar to modify the search term, buttons for Search type, and format will appear below the search bar and above the buttons for sorting. Once you activate the Go button to confirm the search modification, the additional buttons are no longer available.

4.10.5.2: Sorting Titles

By default, the results screen shows titles by relevance to the search term, with the most relevant on top. Use the three buttons above the titles to do the following:

  1. Sort by Title (alphabetical order by title)
  2. Sort by Author (alphabetical order by author’s last name)
  3. Sort by Latest (by date when the title was added to BARD, with most recent on top)

Activate one of the above three buttons to change the sorting order. To go back to the original sorting order by relevance, activate the Currently Selected button to unselect it.

4.11: Options That Apply to All Get Books Features

This section explains the common options that apply to all seven features of the Get Books screen, including the Results, Details, and More Options.

4.11.1: Common Options in the Results Screen

All features of Get Books lead to a Result screen which allows you to do the following:

  1. Use the Search box to change the results that appear
  2. Change the sorting order
  3. Activate the name of the title you want to read
  4. The title will be automatically downloaded to the default location if it does not have any subfolders. If the default location has subfolders, then you will have to first choose a destination folder.
4.11.2: Select Folder to Download Screen

This screen only appears when you activate a title you want to download, and the default folder contains subfolders. BARD mobile will know if the title you chose belongs to Audio Books, Audio Magazines, Braille Books, or Braille Magazines. Therefore, only the folder of the same name along with its subfolders will be available. If you want the title to go to the default folder, activate the name of that folder.

Figure 27: Screenshot for Search Music results screen

Alternatively, there is an option to expand or collapse the default folder. When it is expanded, activate the desired subfolder to download the title.

Figure 28: Screenshot of Select Folder to Download screen with expanded subfolders

4.11.3: The Details Screen

The Details screen is used to get additional information or options for a specific title.

4.11.3.1: Accessing the Details Screen

To access the Details screen, do the following:

  1. Choose one of the seven features from the Get Books screen.
  2. For some features, the results screen will open once that feature is chosen. Other features will require choosing additional options from additional screens before getting to the results.
  3. When the screen that shows titles opens, activate the More Info button next to the desired title.
4.11.3.2: Orientation of the Details Screen

The Details screen has the following items:

  • The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    • The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    • The title in the middle with the name “Details”
    • The More options button on the right side (get access to additional choices for the current title).
  • Title name
  • Author name (if available)
  • Annotation (books only)
  • Expand Annotation button (appears visually as a plus sign, books only)
  • Narrator (audio only)
  • Total time (audio only)
  • Number of volumes (braille only)
  • Book Number
  • List of related subjects (Books only)
  • Series (If the title belongs to a series)
  • Order in series (If the title belongs to a series and there is an order)
  • Last Downloaded (gives the date of the last time this was downloaded)
  • Add to Wish List button (available when the title is not already on the Wish List or downloaded to the current device)
  • Delete from Wish List button (available when the title is previously added to the Wish List)
  • Download button (available when the title is not downloaded to the current device)
  • Read button (available when the title is downloaded to the current device)
  • The tab bar, near the bottom of the screen, shows the four tabs with Get Books selected.

Figure 29: More Info screen

4.11.3.3: More Options Pop-up for Details

While in the Details screen, activating The More options button will open a pop-up window that has the following choices:

  • Download button (available when the title is not downloaded to the current device)
  • Read button (moves into the Now Reading tab and away from the Get Books tab to read the title; available when the title is downloaded to the current device)
  • All books by Author button (opens a screen with the list of books by a particular author. There may be multiple buttons if the book has multiple authors).
  • All books in Subject button (opens a screen with a list of books matching subject, may have multiple buttons if the book belongs to multiple subjects, books only)
  • All Books in Series button (opens a screen with the list of books in that series, only available for books that belong to a series. There may be multiple buttons if the book belongs to multiple series).
  • Subscribe to or Unsubscribe from Series button (only available for books that belong to a series)
  • Subscribe to or Unsubscribe from Magazine button (magazines only)

The All Books buttons (including all books by author, all books in subject, and all books in series) open another screen. These screens are similar to the screen that shows titles for a specific category. This means you can search, sort, download, read, and work with the list of titles.

If a book has multiple authors, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books by each author. The button for the primary author will be first.

If a book belongs to multiple subjects, there will be buttons that allow you to browse all books from each subject. The buttons for the subjects are in alphabetical order.

Figure 30: Screenshot of More Options pop-over window in the More Info screen

4.11.3.4: Expanding and Collapsing the Annotation

When in the More Info screen for a book, the Expand Annotation button appears as a plus sign.

Once the annotation is expanded, it is maximized, which means the annotation takes up the majority of the screen.

When the annotation is expanded, there is a Collapse Annotation button (appears visually as a minus sign), allowing you to collapse the annotation again.

Figure 31: Screenshot of Expanded annotation in the More Info screen

Section 5: Settings

Use the Settings tab to adjust audio, display, and user account settings to optimize your reading experience.

5.1: Accessing the Settings Tab

To access the Settings tab, do the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the bottom of the screen
  2. Find Settings, which is the third tab from the left
  3. Activate the Settings tab to select it. The Settings screen will appear in the content area above the tab bar.
5.2: The Settings Screen

The Settings screen contains the following items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following two items:
    1. The title in the middle that reads “Settings.”
    2. The More Options button on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. Audio settings
  3. Braille and Display settings
  4. User Account settings
  5. Device Accessibility settings (opens the accessibility settings for your Fire Tablet)
  6. The currently running version of BARD Mobile
  7. The tab bar, near the bottom, shows the four tabs with Settings selected.

Figure 32: Settings

5.3: Audio Settings

The first button on the Settings screen is the Audio Settings button. The Audio Settings screen has the following options below the action bar and above the tab bar:

  1. Speed
  2. Tone
  3. Background Playback
  4. Auto-lock During Playback
  5. Automatic Start Playback
  6. Automatic 5 second Rewind
  7. Skippable
  8. Verbosity
  9. Silence Button Beeps
  10. Restore Defaults

Figure 33: Audio settings screens

5.3.1: Speed

The audio settings for Speed and Tone are the global settings for playing audio content. Speed and Tone are also available while in the Now Reading tab for individual audio titles. A global setting will affect audio titles that did not have individual adjustments. For example, if the global speed is set to 1.5 times and you change a specific audio title to .75 times, then the .75 times would only apply to that specific title. The other audio content will remain at 1.5 times.

Speed allows you to adjust the global audio playback speed. The default speed is 1 time. To adjust the speed:

  1. From Audio settings, activate Speed. This will open a screen for speed adjustment.
  2. Activate Decrease Speed and Increase Speed to adjust the global reading speed by .125 times.
  3. When you are on the speed that you want, activate Ok to save the changes and go back to the previous screen.
5.3.2: Tone

Tone allows you to adjust the global audio playback tone for audiobooks and magazines. Increasing the tone adds more treble and decreasing the tone adds more bass while reading audio content. The default tone is zero. To adjust the tone:

  1. From Audio settings, activate Tone. This will open a screen for tone adjustment.
  2. Activate Decrease Tone and Increase Tone to adjust the global reading tone by 1.
  3. When you are on the tone that you want, activate Ok to save the changes and go back to the previous screen.
5.3.3: Background Playback

Background Playback determines whether or not audio content continues to play when you lock the device or move away from the BARD Mobile app. The default for this setting is On.

When it is on, the audio content will continue to play, even if the device is locked or you move away from BARD Mobile.

When Background Playback is set to Off, BARD Mobile will stop playing when you lock the device or move away from the app.

5.3.4: Auto-Lock during Playback

Auto-lock During Playback is used to determine how your Fire Tablet will behave while audio content is playing. It works in conjunction with the Screen Timeout feature in the main Settings app of your device, which determines how much time will pass before the device turns off the display in order to save battery life and prevent unintended operation. The default for Auto-lock during playback is Off.

When the Auto-lock during Playback setting is Off and audio content is playing with the BARD Mobile app, the device will not lock, no matter how the Screen Timeout feature was set in the main Settings of the device.

When audio content is not playing, the device will lock according to the time chosen for the Screen Timeout feature in the main Settings.

When the Auto-lock During Playback setting is On, the device will lock according to the time chosen for the Screen Timeout feature in the main Settings of the device, whether or not audio content is playing.

If audio content is playing, the device locks, and the Background Playback setting is turned off, BARD Mobile will stop playing.

If audio content is playing, the device locks, and the Background Playback setting is turned on, BARD Mobile will continue to play.

5.3.5: Automatic Start Playback

Automatic Start Playback determines the app’s behavior when you choose an audio title to read and is placed in the Now Reading tab with player controls for the audio. This is off by default. When it is off, you must activate the play button to begin playing the title. When Automatic Start Playback is on, the audio content will begin playing without the need to activate the Play button.

5.3.6: Automatic 5 Second Rewind

Automatic 5 Second Rewind is off by default. When this is off, resuming playback of audio content will continue reading from the last reading position.

When Automatic 5 Second Rewind is on, audio content will automatically rewind five seconds from the last reading position under the below conditions:

  • While reading an audio title, you read another audio title and then choose to continue reading the original audio title.
  • You exit the BARD Mobile app, reopen it, and resume audio in the Now Reading tab.
5.3.7: Skippable

Skippable allows you to control whether items such as sidebars, page numbers, and footnotes, when marked by the producer of the audio content, will be played or skipped during normal playback. The default setting is to play this information.

5.3.8: Verbosity

This setting determines if messages are spoken when you navigate a book using the buttons on the Now Reading screen. By default, the Verbosity option is on, and spoken messages are provided. This is similar to the messages heard when using a digital talking-book player.

5.3.9: Silence Button Beeps

The Silence Button Beeps option allows you to determine whether or not to play the beeps that sound when activating the button controls in the Now Reading tab. This is off by default.

When off, audible beeps will sound when player control buttons are activated in the Now Reading tab. When it is on, the beeps will not sound.

5.3.10: Restore Defaults

The Restore Defaults button is located at the bottom of the Audio settings screen. This is used to reset all audio settings to the BARD Mobile default values. To learn about default values for each of the above settings, please read the appropriate sections.

When you activate the Restore Defaults button, this action will take place immediately. There is no confirmation before the action is taken. It is not possible to undo this action.

*Note for VoiceView users: When activating the Restore Defaults button, VoiceView does not say that any changes were made. However, reviewing the settings will reflect the default values.*

5.4: Braille and Display Settings

The second button on the Settings screen is the Braille and Display settings button. Note that it was previously explained that reading braille titles on BARD Mobile with a connected braille display will not be documented. Although the below will list all options, explanations on braille options that are used to read braille titles will not be provided. Once activated, the following options are available:

  1. Contrast
  2. Braille Reflow
  3. Braille Display Length
  4. Braille Autoscroll
  5. Time Per Line
  6. Time Per Character
  7. Restore Defaults

Figure 34: Braille and Display setting

5.4.1: Contrast

Contrast is used to configure the way text and background appear visually and is not related to braille. It allows you to choose an option for the text in relation to the background color. This is set to black text on a white background by default. Available contrast choices include:

  1. Black text on a white background
  2. White text on a black background
  3. Black text on a yellow background
  4. Yellow text on a black background
5.4.2: Restore Defaults

The Restore Defaults button is the last option on the Braille and Display settings screen. This is used to reset all Braille and Display settings to BARD Mobile default values. To learn about default values for each of the above settings, please read the appropriate sections.

When you activate the Restore Defaults button, the action will take place immediately. There is no confirmation before the action is taken. It is not possible to undo this action.

*Note for VoiceView users: When activating the Restore Defaults button, VoiceView does not say that any changes were made. However, reviewing the settings will reflect the default values.*

5.5: User Account Settings

The third button on the Settings screen is the User Account Settings button. This has the following options below the Action bar and above the Tab bar:

  1. BARD Account
  2. Download over Mobile Network
  3. Show me (both audio and braille, audio only, or braille only)
  4. Language Preferences
  5. Storage Location
  6. Application Reset
  7. Restore Defaults

Figure 35: Screenshot of the user account settings

5.5.1: BARD Account

The BARD Account screen is used to view and edit account information, and read the device ID.

When the BARD Account button is activated, a new screen opens displaying the following items below the action bar and above the tab bar:

  1. Heading that reads “Login Information”
  2. Text field for User Name (email address)
  3. Text field for Password
  4. Heading for Device ID
  5. The device ID itself

Figure 36: Screenshot of the user account info

BARD Mobile allows multiple users to use one device. Users log into BARD Mobile using a unique email address and password. This is done by changing the information in the two edit fields to reflect the proper email address and password for each user.

When the same device is used to sign into multiple accounts, the current user will also see books from other users, but the current user cannot read those books.

5.5.2: Download over Mobile Network

Download Materials Over a Mobile network only applies if the device has cellular capabilities. This is off by default. When on, you can download titles using a mobile network. A data plan is required, and data rates may apply.

5.5.3: Show Me

Show Me allows you to customize the preferred format shown when specifically viewing three features of the Get Books tab. This includes Recently Added to BARD, Most Popular Books, and Browse Magazines. The default is both audio and braille. Additional options include audio only and braille only.

Note: The sections on the three features lists the options that appear if this setting is set to the default of both audio and braille. When reading Sections 4.6.1: Orientation of the Recently Added to BARD Screen, 4.7.1: Orientation of the Most Popular Books Screen, and 4.8.1: Select a Format Screen, results will be different if this setting is changed.

5.5.4: Language Preferences

Language Preferences determine the available language options when using the Search feature of the Get Books tab. By default, the first language is English and the second is Spanish. Choose the Language Preferences button, browse through the available languages, activate a language to add it, and continue to activate an added language to change the order that it appears in the Search feature or remove it. While browsing languages, you will be informed of the number of available titles for that specific language.

The first language is English and cannot be changed. Additional languages will be presented in the order they will appear in the Search feature. For example, the default has English as the first language and Spanish as the second language. If you add French, this will be the third language. If you would like French to be the second language, activate French again to change it to the second language. When satisfied with your language preferences, activate the Navigate Up button on the top left to return to the User account settings.

5.5.5: Storage Location

Storage Location provides read-only information with the address of where downloaded titles are stored on the device.

5.5.6: Application Reset

Application Reset allows you to reset BARD Mobile. When this option is selected, a confirmation screen opens. Choose Continue to reset the application. Choose Cancel to cancel the reset.

Warning: When resetting the application, all BARD Mobile data from the device will be deleted, including downloaded books and magazines.

5.5.7: Restore Defaults

The Restore Defaults button is the last option on the User Account settings screen. This is used to reset account settings for Download over Mobile Network, Show me, and Language Preferences to BARD Mobile default values. When you activate the Restore Defaults button, the action will take place immediately. There is no confirmation before the action is taken. It is not possible to undo this action.

*Note for VoiceView users: When activating the Restore Defaults button, VoiceView does not say that any changes were made. However, reviewing the settings will reflect the default values.*

5.6: Device Accessibility Settings

This provides a quick method for you to go to the accessibility settings, which are part of the settings app of your device. Some popular options may include VoiceView and Magnification. Since these options are specific to your device, read the User Guide for your phone or tablet for more information.

Section 6: Reading Audio Titles

The Now Reading tab, located near the bottom right, is used to read audio and braille titles that are downloaded onto the device. The information in the main content area of the Now Reading tab is different depending on whether an audio, or braille title is opened. This section provides information about working with audio titles in the Now Reading tab.

Although the BARD Mobile app also supports reading braille titles, the Fire Tablet only supports a small list of legacy braille displays. This means that the NLS eReaders and other newer displays are not supported. To read more about this list of older devices go to this Amazon article written in 2018: Use a Braille Display with Your Fire Tablet External. As a result of these limitations, reading braille titles with a connected display will not be covered by this user guide at this time.

6.1: Accessing the Now Reading Tab

If the Now Reading tab is selected without an opened title, you will get the following message:

“No book is currently selected to play. Please select a book from the Bookshelf to play.”

To open a title, locate a book or magazine from the Bookshelf, or locate and download a title from Get Books, then activate the Read button, which automatically moves focus to the Now Reading tab. If a title is already opened, and you explore another tab, you can return to the Now Reading tab to access controls for the current title by doing the following:

  1. Locate the tab bar near the bottom of the screen.
  2. Find Now Reading, which is the last of four tabs, near the bottom right.
  3. Activate the Now Reading tab to select it. The main screen of Now Reading with the most recent title will appear on the content area above the tab bar.
6.2: Orientation of the Now Reading Screen for Audio Titles

When the Now Reading tab is selected and an audio title is opened, these items appear on the screen in the following order:

  1. The Action bar on the top, which has the following three items:
    1. The title in the middle that shows the currently loaded title
    2. The Recently Read button (appears visually as an opened book)
    3. The More Options button (appears visually as three vertical dots), on the right side (get help for the current screen).
  2. The current section title
  3. The Navigation button
  4. The Reading time and current position
  5. The Progress bar
  6. Sleep Timer
  7. Bookmark
  8. Jump by various navigation points (includes Previous, the Jump by menu, and Next)
  9. Primary Player Controls (includes Rewind, Play/Stop, and Fast Forward)
  10. Speed/Tone
  11. The tab bar, near the bottom, shows the four tabs with Now Reading selected.

Figure 37: Now Reading Audio screen

6.3: Recently Read

Recently Read lets you view and continue Reading Recently read titles on your Fire Tablet. This includes both audio and braille. It was previously explained that reading braille titles with a connected display will not be covered by this user guide as a result of the Fire Tablet only supporting a small list of legacy braille displays. It should still be noted that if you download and then attempt to read a braille title, that title will appear on this recently read list. If a Recently read title is deleted, then it will not be included.

6.3.1: View Recently Read

To view Recently Read from the Now Reading screen, activate the Recently read button from the action bar near the top right.

6.3.2: The Recently Read Screen

Recently Read lists titles with the most recent on top. Each item includes the title and audio or braille icon to identify the format. Choosing a title will exit this screen and open that title in the now reading screen.

To exit Recently read without choosing another title, activate the Navigate Up button on the top left.

Figure 38: Screenshot of recently read titles

6.4: The Current Section title

The current section title can include the title of the current section, part, chapter, article, recipe, etc.

6.5: Navigation

To access the Navigation screen, activate the Navigation button located to the right of the current section title.

The Navigation screen provides several methods to navigate the current title.

6.5.1: Orientation for the Navigation Screen

The navigation screen has the following Items:

  1. The Action bar on the top which has the following three items:
    1. The Navigate Up button on the left side (goes back to the previous screen)
    2. The title in the middle that reads “Book Navigation”
    3. The More Options button on the right side (used to change skippable settings)
  2. The Toolbar to determine the items that appear below (includes Contents, Page, and Bookmark; Contents is selected by default.)
  3. List of items determined by the toolbar

Figure 39: Book Navigation screen

6.5.2: Skippable

Skippable allows you to control whether items such as sidebars, page numbers, and footnotes, when marked by the producer of the audio content, are played or skipped during normal playback. The default setting is to play this information. To change this setting:

  1. From the Book Navigation screen, activate the More Options button on the top right.
  2. Activate the Read Skippable Elements option.
  3. Activate the Skip items marked as skippable to toggle the check box.
  4. Activate Ok to save the changes and go back to the Book Navigation screen.

This is for the current audio title. To adjust the skippable setting for all titles, change it under Audio settings in the Settings tab.

6.5.3: Navigating by Contents

To browse the contents for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. Make sure Contents is selected on the toolbar.
  2. The contents appear below the toolbar.
  3. A + next to a navigation item indicates that the item can be expanded to display additional information. If an item is expanded, a - will appear next to that item to indicate that the item can be collapsed to hide the additional information. Tap and hold to expand or collapse.
  4. Tap on the desired item to close the Navigation screen. The playing position moves to the selected navigation point.

Figure 40: Book Navigation screen—Contents

*Note for VoiceView users: If a navigation item can be expanded to show more items, VoiceView says the word “plus” in front of the item name. Perform a one-finger double tap and hold to expand the item, resulting in additional items below.

If a navigation item is already expanded showing more items below that item, VoiceView says the word “minus” in front of the item name. Perform a one-finger double tap and hold to collapse the item, hiding additional items below.

*Performing a one-finger double tap on the item you want closes the Navigation screen, and the playing position moves to the selected navigation point.*

6.5.4: Navigating by Page

To go to a specific page for the current title while in navigation:

  1. Make sure Page is selected on the toolbar. (Not all titles have page navigation.)
  2. The pages appear below the toolbar.
  3. Activate the desired page to close the Navigation screen. The playing position moves to the selected page.

Figure 41: Screenshot of Navigation

*Note for VoiceView users: When navigating the toolbar, if page navigation is not available, then VoiceView says “Page button disabled.”*

6.5.5: Navigating by Bookmark

These instructions are for accessing bookmarks within Navigation. For detailed information to insert, go to, and remove bookmarks, read Section 6.8: Bookmark.

To go to a Bookmark for the current title while in Navigation:

  1. Make sure Bookmark is selected on the toolbar. The bookmarks appear below the toolbar. This includes Beginning of Book, any previously added Bookmarks, and End of Book.
  2. Activate the desired bookmark to close the Navigation screen. The reading position moves to the selected bookmark.

Figure 42: Book Navigation screen—Contents

6.6: Reading Time and Progress Bar

The Reading Time provides the time elapsed and the total amount of time for the audio title.

Below the reading time is the Progress bar. This is used to adjust the position within the title.

Adjust the slider to the left to move the reading position toward the beginning of the title or to the right to move the reading position toward the end of the title.

*Note for VoiceView users: To adjust the progress bar:

  1. Navigate to the current position slider.
  2. Advance the current position by five percent with a one-finger flick up. Go back by five percent with a one-finger flick down.
  3. Alternatively, perform a one-finger double tap and hold, and then slowly move that finger to move by smaller increments. Release your finger when you are finished making adjustments.
  4. When satisfied with the current position, navigate to another item to avoid accidentally making additional adjustments.*
6.7: Sleep Timer

The Sleep Timer is used to automatically stop audio playback after a specific amount of time or at the end of a navigation point.

6.8.1: Available Options with the Sleep Timer

Activating the Sleep timer button will offer the below options in the following order:

  1. 15 minutes
  2. 30 minutes
  3. 45 minutes
  4. 60 minutes
  5. End of navigation point (section, part, chapter, article, recipe, etc.)
  6. Off
6.7.2: Working with the Sleep Timer Button
  1. Activating the Sleep Timer button once sets it for 15 minutes (if the title was not playing before activating the Sleep Timer, this starts the audio).
  2. Activating the Sleep timer button repeatedly in quick succession cycles through the available options.

*Note for VoiceView users: While on the Sleep timer button, perform a one-finger double tap to set the sleep timer to 15 minutes. Perform additional one-finger double taps in quick succession to cycle through the additional options.*

6.7.3: Stopping the Sleep Timer

Activate the Play/Stop button to stop the Sleep Timer as well as the audio playback.

The sleep timer can also be stopped by activating the Sleep button repeatedly in quick succession to eventually land on the off option; however, this does not stop the audio playback.

6.8: Bookmark

A Bookmark is used to remember reading positions to read at a later time, such as an interesting passage or a recipe. Each title has its own bookmarks, and BARD Mobile remembers bookmarks even if you close the app.

6.8.1: Inserting Bookmarks

Selecting the Bookmark button inserts a bookmark at the current reading position.

6.8.2: Going to a Previously Added Bookmark

There are two methods to go to a previously added bookmark.

Method one:

  1. From the Now Reading screen, activate the Jump by Menu button. Continue activating the Jump by Menu button until the Jump by Bookmark option is chosen.
  2. Activate the Previous button to go to the previous bookmark and the Next button to go to the next bookmark.

Method two:

  1. From the Now Reading screen, activate the Navigation button (located to the right of the current section title).
  2. Activate the Bookmark button (located on the right side of the toolbar near the top). Added bookmarks are listed below the toolbar.
  3. Activate the desired bookmark. (This exits the Navigation screen and moves the reading position to the bookmarked location).
6.8.3: Removing Bookmarks

To remove a bookmark while reading a book, stop audio playback, go to the desired bookmark using one of the two methods discussed in section 6.8.2, and then select the Bookmark button to delete.

6.9: Jump by Various Navigation Points

Below the Sleep Timer and Bookmark buttons are three buttons to allow you to go to various navigation points. These buttons appear in the following order from left to right:

  1. Previous
  2. Jump by Menu
  3. Next

The Jump by menu button is described first because it affects the behavior of the Previous and Next buttons.

6.9.1: Jump by Menu

The Jump by Menu button is located between the Previous and Next buttons. Activating the Jump by menu cycles through and selects the different types of navigation. Some possible examples include parts, chapters, sections, recipes, and bookmarks. Once you locate the desired navigation, use the Previous and Next buttons to move to the previous and next navigation.

6.9.2: Previous and Next Navigation

Located to the left of the Jump by Menu button is the Previous button, and to the right of the Jump by Menu button is the Next button.

Once you select the desired navigation with the Jump by Menu button:

  • Activate the Previous or Next buttons to go back or forward by one item.
  • Press and hold the Previous and Next buttons to quickly go back and forward by five navigation points, with the exception of pages that moves ten pages at a time.

*Note for VoiceView users: To go back or forward one navigation point at a time, do a one-finger double tap while on the Previous or Next buttons. To quickly go back or forward five navigation points or ten pages, do a one-finger double tap and hold while on the Previous or Next buttons.*

When working with the Previous and Next buttons to go back or forward through items, if the audio title is not playing, the heading of the new item selected is read. Activate the Play/Stop button to resume audio playback. If audio is already playing, playback automatically starts from the new location.

6.10: Primary Player Controls

Below the Previous, Jump by Menu, and Next buttons are three buttons for primary player controls. These buttons appear in the following order from left to right:

  1. Rewind
  2. Play/Stop
  3. Fast Forward

The Play/Stop button is described first because the Play/Stop button affects the behavior of the Rewind and Fast Forward buttons.

6.10.1: Play/Stop

The Play/Stop button is located between the Rewind and Fast Forward buttons. Activate the button to either start or stop playing a title.

6.10.2: Rewind and Fast Forward

Located to the left of the Play/Stop button is the Rewind button, and to the right of the Play/Stop button is the Fast Forward button. They are used to move the reading position backwards and forward by a specific amount of time.

  • Activate the Rewind or Fast Forward buttons to go back or forward by five seconds.
  • Press and hold the Rewind or Fast Forward buttons to go back or forward by larger amounts of time. Release on the desired spoken amount (20 seconds, 1 minute, 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, 2 hours, etc.).

When working with the Rewind and Fast forward buttons, if the audio title is not playing, audio playback does not resume automatically. Activate the Play/Stop button to resume audio playback. If audio is already playing, playback starts from the new location.
